# Tool Registry · UI Surfaces (v2)

**Status**: shipped
**Owner**: tool-registry
**Routes**: `/administration/ui/surfaces` (admin) · `/surfaces` (user hub) · `/agents/[id]/surfaces` (per-agent bindings)

## What this is

The dedicated admin UI for the `ui_surface` table. Built to scale to the
~100+ surfaces this system has (vs. the typical 1–2 most apps need), with
grouping, bulk operations, usage stats, and inline editing.

The simpler per-row CRUD on `/admin/lookups` (UI Surfaces tab) still exists
and is fine for one-off edits, but a callout banner there directs admins to
the v2 page for serious management.

## Why a dedicated page

The original `UiSurfaceCrud` (under `/admin/lookups`) is a flat table. With
~100 surfaces across 4 clients, that becomes unusable:

- No grouping → an admin can't tell which surfaces are "pages" vs "overlays"
  vs "debug" without reading every row.
- No bulk ops → activating an entire tier of debug overlays would take 10
  individual clicks.
- No usage info → an admin can't tell which surfaces actually have tools or
  agents pointing at them, so dead surfaces accumulate silently.
- No inline edit → changing a description means opening a modal per row.

The v2 page solves all four.

## One name, two systems — "surface manifest" is overloaded

- **Value manifest (this feature, code-first).** The runtime VALUES a surface promises to supply — plus agent roles, config namespaces, evidence sources. Declared in `manifests/*.manifest.ts`; **code is truth, `ui.ui_surface_value` is the synced mirror.**
- **Registered-tool defaults (aidream).** Which Registered tools a surface
  offers and which Executors can run them — `tool.surface_defaults` +
  `tool.definition` / `tool.binding` / `tool.executor`. The DB is authoritative
  for Registered-tool contracts and defaults; Inline tools remain a permanent,
  first-class request-time path for tools authored at runtime. Durability decides
  which path applies. Rules: `docs/official/tool_system_rules.md`.

Both hang off the same `ui.ui_surface` spine and resolve server-side in aidream `tool_merge.py` from the request's `client.surface`. When a doc says "surface manifest", identify which system before acting.

## Agent↔surface bindings — canonical model (associations-only, 2026-07-12)

**A binding is an edge in `platform.associations`** (source `agent` → target `surface`), written ONLY through `bindAgentToSurface` / `upsertAgentSurfaceBinding` in [`services/bind-agent-to-surface.service.ts`](./services/bind-agent-to-surface.service.ts) (which is the sole surface-side caller of `associationsService`). The condemned bespoke `agent_surface` junction (P1–P4: single project/task FKs, single-scope cardinality, passive-context binding, bespoke M2M) is **gone** — data migrated count-verified, table retired to `graveyard`, its service + `console.error` beacons deleted (`migrations/agent_surface_to_associations.sql`).

- **Tier-encoded edge role** is the identity of one scope tier: `binding:global` · `binding:u:<userId>` · `binding:o:<orgId>` · `binding:p:<projectId>` · `binding:t:<taskId>` — so multiple tiers per (agent, surface) coexist under `associations_unique` and each upsert idempotently updates its own tier.
- **The binding's logic is a TYPED edge payload** (Edge Payload System, 2026-07-19): `payload_kind = 'surface_binding'`, `payload = { value_mappings }`, schema-validated on every write by `trg_validate_edge_payload` against `platform.edge_payload_kind` (see "Edge Payload System" below). Edge `metadata` keeps only loose annotations: `{ tier, user_id, project_id, task_id, visibility, version }` + provenance markers.
- **Reads go through `agent.menu_surface`** (pre-joined owner-rights view over associations ⋈ `agent.card` ⋈ `ui_surface` ⋈ orgs; exposes `role` + hoisted tier columns + `value_mappings`). It is **self-scoping by tier role** (`migrations/menu_surface_tier_scoping.sql`): `binding:global` visible to everyone (incl. anon), `binding:u:<uid>` only to that user, `binding:o|p|t:*` only to members of the edge's access org — the associations RLS does NOT apply through the view, so this WHERE is the security boundary. List/stat/drift/usage reads all use it; the browser has NO direct grant on `platform.associations`.
- **`role` is the ONLY tier signal.** `assoc_add` stamps an access `organization_id` on EVERY edge (all tiers), so null-ness of the hoisted columns is meaningless — classify tier by parsing `role` (`tierScopeFromRole` in the binding service), and delete edges with the STORED role, never one recomputed from columns.
- **Binding role and access org cannot diverge.** `trg_surface_binding_scope_integrity` derives org/project/task audience orgs from the tier role at the table edge and validates the caller can use that scope; `agent.menu_surface` independently hides any role/metadata/org mismatch. Org-tier binding also ensures the existing canonical `iam.permissions` viewer grants for both the safe `agent_card` launcher row and the executable `agent` definition, because "available to every member" must include both reads, not only the binding edge.
- **Global-tier writes are super-admin only**, enforced at the DB edge by `trg_guard_global_surface_binding` on `platform.associations` (`migrations/global_surface_binding_write_guard.sql`) — a global edge broadcasts to every viewer, so a normal user's org access must not be able to publish one.
- **Launch resolution**: `fetchSurfaceBindingLayers(agentId, surfaceName)` (same service) returns weakest→strongest `MappingLayer`s for `mergeValueMappingLayers`: inheritance parents first (see below), then per surface `global → org rows by membership (oldest→newest) → user`. Org tiers apply by RLS membership — never an "active org" filter (P3: binding scope comes from the user's EXPLICIT picker selection; `ensureOrgId` supplies only the assoc access org).
- **Binding id = association id** everywhere (Redux slice, shortcut seeding via `create_shortcut_from_agent_surface`, drift remediation — which updates `metadata.value_mappings` on `platform.associations` via the admin client, server-side only).
- Redux stays `agentSurfaceBindingsSlice` + thunks in `redux/thunks.ts` — same shapes, associations-backed.

## Edge Payload System (v1, 2026-07-19 — Arman-approved)

**Real logic on a `platform.associations` edge lives in `payload`, typed by `payload_kind` and schema-validated at the DB edge. `metadata` is for loose annotations only — a payload without a kind is rejected.**

- **Registry**: `platform.edge_payload_kind` — one row per kind: `kind` (PK), `version`, `json_schema` (JSON Schema), optional `source_type`/`target_type` endpoint contract. Readable by all; writes are service/admin-side only. (Distinct from `platform.association_types`, which is one row per (source,target) *pair* — a pair can carry multiple payload kinds, and a kind can span pairs.)
- **Enforcement**: `trg_validate_edge_payload` (BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE of payload/payload_kind, `pg_jsonschema`) hard-fails (`23514`) on: payload without kind, unknown kind, endpoint-type mismatch, kind without payload, schema violation. It fires for ALL writers — client RPC, admin client, server-side SQL.
- **Write path**: `assoc_add(p_payload_kind, p_payload)` (optional params, backward compatible; payload-bearing upsert replaces the payload wholesale, payload-less upsert preserves it) ← `associationsService.add({ payloadKind, payload })`.
- **First adopter**: `surface_binding` v1 — `{ value_mappings }` (the `ValueMapping` union as JSON Schema). All binding edges backfilled; `agent.menu_surface` reads `payload -> 'value_mappings'` (metadata leg is legacy fallback, scheduled for removal).
- **Evolving a kind**: bump `version` + replace `json_schema` in the registry (upsert in a migration). Schema must stay valid for all live payloads — migrate rows in the same migration or widen compatibly.
- **Adding a kind**: registry insert (migration) + writer passes `payloadKind`/`payload`. Adoption backlog + recipe: `.claude/skills/canonical-associations/WORK-QUEUE.md` §Edge-payload adoption.
- **Performance**: reads ride the existing `(source_type, source_id)` / `(target_type, target_id)` btrees — per-slice cost is independent of table size; payload JSONB doesn't affect index traversal (large payloads TOAST). Validation cost is one schema check per write (writes are rare vs reads).

## Surface inheritance (v1, 2026-07-12 — Arman-approved)

`SurfaceManifest.inheritsFrom` declares a parent surface. A child inherits, child-wins-per-key:

| Inherited                       | Resolved in                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Values (bindable declarations)  | `registry.ts` `withInheritance` (parent values first, child overrides by `name`; baselines still floored last)                                                                   |
| Agent bindings (value_mappings) | `fetchSurfaceBindingLayers` — parent layers are WEAKER: `parent:global → parent:org → parent:user → child:*`; inherited layers get `binding:parent:<surface>:…` provenance names |
| Agent roles / config namespaces | `registry.ts` union, child overrides by key                                                                                                                                      |
| Document evidence sources       | `registry.ts` union; the universal launcher turns each resolved declaration into a lazy processed-document context source before value mappings run                              |

- Guards are LOUD: unknown parent, cycle, or depth > 3 **throws at module init** (`getSurfaceAncestry`).
- Manifest sync mirrors `inheritsFrom` → `ui_surface.parent_surface_name` (never clears a DB-authored parent when the manifest is silent) and mirrors the MERGED value set into `ui_surface_value`.
- The chain is code-first (registry manifests). The DB's blanket `parent_surface_name = matrx-default/default` rows do NOT cascade bindings — only manifest-declared parents do (deliberate: cascading Default to all ~100 surfaces would be a behavior change).
- Live families: `matrx-user/transcripts` ⊃ `transcripts-cleanup` (60 effective values, 36 own); `matrx-user/pdf-extractor` ⊃ `extractor-chunker` / `analysis-studio` / `scanner`. Bind an agent ONCE on the parent and it applies on every child.

## THE NAMING LAW + canonical groups (2026-07-24)

**`SurfaceManifest.label` is REQUIRED — the ONE canonical display name for a surface**, unique per client (case-insensitive). Every value and group `label` is equally canonical. The `surfaceLabel` runtime override prop is DELETED, banned by ESLint (`surfaceLabelOverrideBan` in `eslint.config.mjs`).

- **Chrome reads labels through one seam**: `getSurfaceDisplayLabel(surfaceName)` in [`utils/surface-display.ts`](./utils/surface-display.ts) (static, synchronous). On-page section titles / field labels for declared values render via `surfaceValueLabels(manifest)` / `surfaceGroupLabels(manifest)` — byte-identical to the manifest. `labelFromName` slug fallback is for manifest-less DB surfaces only.
- **Canonical groups**: `SurfaceValueGroup {key,label,sortOrder}` in `manifest.groups` (curated band 0–899); every `SurfaceValue.group` references a declared key. `general` / `baseline` / `inherited:*` are RESERVED — the registry synthesizes them (declaring one throws at module init). [`manifests/registry.ts`](./manifests/registry.ts) stamps every resolved value with provenance (own/inherited/baseline) + `groupKey` and sorts values by (group order, value sortOrder): curated first, inherited next, baselines LAST.
- **DB mirror**: `ui_surface.label` + `ui_surface.value_groups` (JSONB) + `ui_surface_value.group_key`, written by [`services/manifest-sync.service.ts`](./services/manifest-sync.service.ts) (label/value_groups ALWAYS written) and `scripts/emit-surface-sync-sql.ts`. Drift report gains `surfaceLabelDrifts` / `valueGroupsDrifts`; `scripts/check-surface-drift.ts` enforces label presence + per-client uniqueness and group key/band/label rules.
- **Registry is the hierarchy source for chrome**: `getSurfaceAncestry` / `getSurfaceChildren` via `getRelatedSurfaces` ([`runtime/fetchRelatedSurfaces.ts`](./runtime/fetchRelatedSurfaces.ts), now synchronous); the Agents popover renders the full ancestry breadcrumb. `ui_surface.parent_surface_name` is a mirror only.
- **THE COMPLETENESS LAW**: every piece of data/state a page loads MUST be declared as a surface value — fields AND natural composites (marketing-page's `page_intent` object beside its four fields). Undeclared runtime keys render loudly as "Undeclared (runtime only)" in the Surface Context window — defects. Optional convenience packs are the only discretionary part.
- **Locate-on-page**: pages tag elements `data-surface-value="<value_name>"`; the Surface Context window's Locate button flashes them ([`utils/locate-on-page.ts`](./utils/locate-on-page.ts)). `SectionCard`/`MetricCell` in `features/marketing/components/shared/MarketingUi.tsx` take an `anchor` prop.
- **Agent-feed contract**: aidream's `/surfaces/{client}/{surface}/manifest` carries per-value `{name, group_key, sort_order, auto_context, always_available}` + `groups[{key, sort_order}]` — **machine names only, labels never enter agent feeds** (`aidream surface_resolver.py`; aidream deploy pending).
- **Worked exemplar**: [`manifests/marketing-page.manifest.ts`](./manifests/marketing-page.manifest.ts) (40+ values, 7 curated groups, inherits marketing-site → marketing-brand; scope builder `features/marketing/lib/marketing-page-scope.ts`; emitter `PageWorkspace.tsx`). This REPLACES notes-editor as the reference implementation (notes-editor stays the simple case).

## Agent roles + namespaced config (runtime settings system)

Two per-surface settings primitives, both resolved `manifest/DB default → global → org-by-membership → [ctx scope, reserved] → user` (newest `updated_at` wins ties, with a console.warn):

- **Agent roles** — where a surface PLUGS IN agents (`SurfaceManifest.agentRoles`, mirrored to `ui.ui_surface_agent_role`; user/org selections in `ui_surface_agent_pref`, kinds `selection`/`roster_item`, `single`/`multi`). Reader/writer: [`services/surface-config.service.ts`](./services/surface-config.service.ts); Redux `redux/surfaceConfigSlice.ts`; hook `hooks/useSurfaceConfig.ts`. Stale roles deleted by sync CASCADE their pref rows (reported).
- **Config namespaces** — typed JSONB buckets in `ui.ui_surface_config` (dictionary, session_defaults, …). Each namespace registers a PURE handler (validate / layered merge / empty) in [`config/namespace-registry.ts`](./config/namespace-registry.ts); adding one = handler + a manifest `configNamespaces` line, zero SQL. A malformed row is rejected loudly, never merged.
- Per-session choices are feature-owned and applied ON TOP of the resolved `effective` — never stored in these tables. Per-user surface UI state is the separate `user_surface_state` primitive (`user-state/`).
- **A per-surface agent choice never lives in `userPreferences` / `useSetting`.** The last one (`transcription.scribeAssistantAgentId`) was deleted 2026-07-12 — the Scribe assistant is now the `assistant` role on `matrx-user/transcript-scribe` (`resolveDefaultAssistantAgentId` reads the resolved role; `useStudioAssistant` hydrates it).

## User hub — `/surfaces` (Wave 5)

`app/(core)/surfaces` — the user-facing LIST view of every active `matrx-user` surface with ≥1 agent role or config namespace ([`components/hub/SurfacesHubPage.tsx`](./components/hub/SurfacesHubPage.tsx)). Detail `/surfaces/[...name]` ([`components/hub/SurfaceHubDetailPage.tsx`](./components/hub/SurfaceHubDetailPage.tsx)): Me|Org scope switcher (selects the WRITE tier; reads always show the resolved effective + provenance), per-role agent pickers, dictionary panel (opens the canonical `DictionarySelectorWindow`), per-namespace JSON config editor (handler-validated via `setNamespaceConfig`), read-only tools panel. Persistence is `surface-config.service.ts` only — never `useSetting`. Declaring a role or namespace on a manifest is what puts a surface in this hub. No `FeatureAdminMap` exists for this feature yet (admin surface is `/administration/ui/surfaces`).

**Registering a surface? Invoke the `surface-registration` skill** — the layered recipe (manifest → roles/namespaces → registry + `pnpm check:surface-drift` → DB sync → emitter → Matrx-vs-matrix verification), with transcripts-cleanup as the worked example.

## Full-screen admin editor — `/administration/ui/surfaces/[...name]`

[`admin-detail/SurfaceAdminDetailPage.tsx`](./admin-detail/SurfaceAdminDetailPage.tsx) (Wave 4). Catch-all route (slash-safe via `surfaceAdminHref` in `utils/surface-hierarchy.ts`) and reusable embedded body for `surfaceContextInspector`. Edits identity (active/description/rename/url_pattern/executor/parent), shows the parent/child tree (`buildChildrenByParent` / `getAncestorChain` — reads the DB mirror), authored-vs-resolved manifest provenance, declared values, roles + prefs, config namespaces, bound agents, and tools. Platform-global config namespace JSON is editable here through the namespace handler's canonical validation; manifest declarations and evidence sources remain code-owned. The 5-panel per-agent shell (`/agents/[id]/surfaces`, `admin/`) and the batch editor (`admin/batch/SurfaceBindingsBatchEditor.tsx`) write through the same associations-backed thunks.

## The 360 loop — read, write, policy (2026-07-29)

Surfaces are no longer read-only. Three seams, ONE system shared by user
bindings, shortcuts (the same system, one opinionated layer stronger), and
internal platform use — never a washed-down user variant beside a private one:

- **Write targets** — `SurfaceManifest.writeTargets` declares what may be
  written INTO a surface; the page registers handlers (provider prop or
  `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` from any depth); every caller lands through
  `applySurfaceWrite` (`runtime/surface-writeback.ts`). Mirrored to
  `ui.ui_surface_write_target` by manifest-sync so aidream feeds them to
  surface-bound agents as a `<surface_write_targets>` block. Live adopters:
  `matrx-user/marketing-page` (`page_meta_tags`, `page_target_keyword`,
  `page_supporting_keywords` — handlers in
  `features/marketing/components/pages/MarketingPageWriteTargets.tsx`),
  `matrx-user/marketing-brand` (`brand_profile`, `brand_identity` — handlers
  in `features/marketing/components/brands/MarketingBrandWriteTargets.tsx`),
  `matrx-user/keyword-intelligence` (`keyword_selection`),
  `matrx-user/agents` (`catalog_filters` — one composite object carrying the
  whole gallery filter/search/sort state; handler in
  `features/agents/components/agent-listings/AgentsGrid.tsx`, live only on
  `/agents/classic`),
  `matrx-user/crm` + `matrx-user/crm-manager` (`search_query`,
  `party_kind_filter`, `column_filters`, `list_sort` — handlers on
  `features/crm/components/CrmListPage.tsx`, the array shared by both
  manifests because write targets do NOT cross `inheritsFrom`), and
  `matrx-admin/lookups` (`lookup_draft` — handlers in
  `features/tool-registry/lookups/components/LookupsSurfaceRuntime.tsx`). The LSI kind
  components (`meta_tag_options` / `keyword_relationship_research` /
  `keyword_search_metrics`, DB components) call
  `runAction("apply_surface_write", …)` — same seam users' agent-authored
  components get.
- **Agent-origin writes (the stream side)** — a run launched on a surface with
  agent-writable targets (declared + handled + resolving to ask/auto) is
  offered ONE inline delegated tool, **`apply_surface_write`**
  (`SURFACE_WRITE_TOOL_NAME` in `runtime/surface-writeback.ts`;
  `listAgentWritableTargets()` builds the per-turn spec in
  `build-tool-injection.ts` — target enum + per-target contract in the
  description). The `tool_delegated` call routes through
  `dispatch-surface-write.thunk.ts` → `applySurfaceWrite(…, {origin:
  "agent", actorLabel: <agent name>})`, so `ask` shows the in-place confirm
  and `manual` refuses loudly. A user DECLINE posts a non-error tool result
  (`{ok:false, declined:true}`) — an error would invite the model to retry
  the write the user just refused. `manual`-only surfaces offer no tool
  (mirrors aidream's `_write_targets_block`: never advertise a write the
  platform refuses). **Making a surface agent-writable? Invoke the
  `surface-write-targets` skill** — judgment bar, declaration + handler
  recipe, mandatory live-agent verification, avalanche contract. Live
  agent-writable adopters: `matrx-user/marketing-page`,
  `matrx-user/tasks` (8 targets — draft fields via `patchTaskEdit` +
  `add_subtasks`/`save_task` entity actions, handlers in
  `TaskEditorBody.tsx`), `matrx-user/marketing-brand` (2 ask-policy entity
  targets through `updateBrand`; confirmed facts/assets/properties have no
  write path by doctrine — human-owned, promoted via discovery review),
  `matrx-user/agent-builder` (6 draft targets — an agent authoring ANOTHER
  agent's prompt: `system_instruction` / `append_system_instruction` (replace
  vs append, both through `withAgentSystemInstruction` + `setAgentMessages`,
  the exact pair the System Prompt textarea dispatches, so non-text blocks
  always round-trip), `agent_description`, `agent_name`, `agent_category`,
  `agent_tags`; handlers in
  `features/agents/hooks/useAgentBuilderWriteHandlers.ts`, registered on the
  builder's own provider — desktop AND mobile — so they stay wired on every
  panel. Model, tools, MCP servers, skills, variables, output schema and all
  governance stay human-only: changing what an agent may REACH is a
  capability change, not a copy edit), and
  `matrx-user/agent-advanced-editor` (4 draft targets over the SAME
  `agent-definition` Redux record the builder edits, reached from the floating
  Agent Advanced Editor window: `editor_system_instruction` /
  `editor_append_system_instruction` (the builder's replace/append pair,
  through the same `withAgentSystemInstruction` + `setAgentMessages`),
  `editor_output_schema`, and the composite `editor_catalog_profile`
  (`{description?, category?, tags?}` in ONE atomic write — the category and
  the tags ARE the classification OF the description, so they are one act with
  one consumer, and one object also cannot half-land the way three sibling
  targets staged in a single turn can). Handlers in
  `features/window-panels/windows/agents/useAgentAdvancedEditorSurface.ts`,
  validators in the React-free `agentAdvancedEditorWrite.ts` so a bad shape
  throws synchronously in the seam; `AgentContentWindow` mounts the surface's
  FIRST provider — before this it declared a vocabulary nothing ever emitted.
  **THE second citation for `quick-tasks`' overlay-naming rule:** every name
  carries an `editor_` prefix because this window floats over any route, so a
  bare `system_instruction` would be out-depthed and captured by
  `agent-builder` on `/agents/[id]/build` — and this window has its own agent
  picker and tab strip, so that is frequently a DIFFERENT agent than the one
  the user is looking at. `agent_name` is deliberately NARROWER than the
  builder's set (here the name is the window's navigation chrome — footer
  EntityRef, tab strip, sidebar row); `output_schema` is deliberately BROADER
  than the builder's exclusion list, because an emitted shape is authored
  content, not a reachable capability, and it lands through the same
  `setAgentOutputSchema` the Output Schema tab's own Apply dispatches, gated
  by the product's real `validateOutputSchema` — flagged in the manifest as
  worth reconciling in ONE direction rather than silently diverged. Model,
  tools, MCP servers, skills, variables, context slots, sharing and all
  governance stay human-only; clearing the schema is not offered and Save is
  never a target), and
  `matrx-user/agent-settings` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target,
  `settings_catalog_profile` — `{description?, category?, tags?}` staged into
  `AgentSettingsForm`'s own `draft` / `tagsInput` COMPONENT state so the Save
  button arms itself exactly as it does for a human edit and the user still
  presses it. This surface also needed `surface-authoring` first: it was
  `readiness: "stub"` with no provider anywhere, so `AgentSettingsWindow` now
  mounts its first `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` via `useAgentSettingsSurface.ts`
  and the manifest grew from 4 window-state values to 22 across 4 groups.
  **THE second shared-vocabulary reference, and the one that shows what
  "shared" does and does not have to mean.** `matrx-user/agent-advanced-editor`
  already ships `editor_catalog_profile` over the SAME three columns on the same
  `agent.definition` row, so the CONTRACT — accepted keys, per-field bounds, the
  replace-vs-patch semantics, and the model-facing prose that states them — was
  lifted into `features/agents/surface-catalog-profile.ts` and BOTH manifests
  now build their description from it. But unlike `lists` / `list-manager`, the
  target NAME and the HANDLER are deliberately NOT shared, for two structural
  reasons: the advanced editor stages into the `agent-definition` Redux slice
  while this form stages into React component state (there is no one handler
  that serves both — `ListDetailClient` is literally the same component from
  both its mounts), and both are floating windows that can be open at once on
  DIFFERENT agents (this form's own Messages / Variables / Tools tiles OPEN the
  advanced editor), so a shared bare name would be captured deepest-first by
  whichever window was on top and stage the rewrite into a record the user who
  pressed Apply cannot see. One contract, two addresses. The lift also moved
  the advanced editor's target onto the canonical `agent-identity-metadata.ts`
  bounds it was not previously enforcing. `AgentSettingsForm` registers its
  handler only behind a `writeSurfaceName` prop, because `AgentContentWindow`
  renders that SAME form on the advanced editor's Overview tab — the `asRoute`
  gate again, for the same reason. The read twins come from the form's live
  draft via a one-slot publish point, not from Redux, so `agent_description`
  means "what is in the box" rather than "what is stored". The agent's NAME,
  the Active / Public / Favorite / Archived switches, the hierarchy scopes, the
  RAG boost, and the Surface pane's bindings all stay human-only), and
  `matrx-user/list-manager` (3 ask-policy entity targets — `add_list_items`
  decomposition plus `active_list_name` / `active_list_description`, handlers
  in `features/user-lists/components/ListManagerFloatingWorkspace.tsx` through
  the canonical `addItemAction` / `updateListAction` server actions; that
  surface has NO draft layer, so `auto` is barred on it and delete /
  visibility stay undeclared), and
  `matrx-user/lists` (the SAME three targets on the `/lists/[id]` ROUTE mount
  of that same list state — `ListDetailClient asRoute`. THE shared-vocabulary
  reference: the List Manager window and this route render the SAME component
  over the same rows, so the targets and their handlers were lifted into
  `features/user-lists/surface-write-targets.ts` +
  `surface-write-handlers.ts` and BOTH manifests / BOTH mounts now import the
  one definition — two target sets over the same fields would be a defect, so
  there is only one set. Registration is gated on `asRoute`, because the
  window renders this component inside its own provider and the registry
  resolves deepest-first; an ungated provider would shadow the shipped window
  surface from inside its own detail pane), and
  `matrx-user/schedules` (8 ask-policy targets across the surface's TWO
  write-capable mounts — 6 `schedule_draft_*` drafts wired by
  `ScheduleForm.tsx`, plus `schedule_title` / `schedule_description` as
  entity targets wired by `ScheduleDetail.tsx` through
  `updateScheduledTask`. THE per-mount-posture reference: one manifest and
  one target list, but `listAgentWritableTargets()` offers a target only
  where a handler is registered, so the read-only detail route gets just the
  two fields that cannot change what a schedule runs or when it fires, while
  everything behavioural stays editor-only for the user to review and save), and
  `matrx-user/education-assessment` (8 draft targets on the GENERATOR form —
  `generation_topic` / `_difficulty` / `_depth` / `_question_types` /
  `_question_count` / `_exam_type` / `_user_request` / `_time_limit_minutes`;
  handlers on `AssessmentCreate`'s own provider, staging through the same
  setters the user's typing uses, so the user still presses Generate — where
  the COPPA gate, the entitlement guard and `assessmentService.createWithItems`
  run. A deliberate per-mount split: the `AssessmentDetail` mount of the SAME
  surface registers no handlers, because it owns a loaded read snapshot rather
  than editor state and its provider also wraps the mid-attempt take flow), and
  `matrx-user/html-page` (2 draft
  targets on the standalone quick-publish editor — `page_seo_metadata`, one
  object carrying the title/description/keywords the Metadata tab edits
  together, and `page_html_content`, the whole standalone document; handlers
  in `features/html-pages/components/HtmlPageEditor.tsx`, which also had to
  MOUNT the surface's first `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`. The HTML body is the
  interesting one: it is a Monaco editor, and Monaco swallows `contextmenu`
  before the v3 menu sees it, so the declared target is the ONLY way an agent
  can reach the document — there is no user-driven text-replace seam to fall
  back on. Indexability, canonical URL, OG image, and publish/delete stay
  human-only: a standalone page goes live the moment it is saved), and
  `matrx-user/shapes` (6 ask-policy DRAFT targets split across the three
  studio mounts that own authored state — `NewShapeClient` the /shapes/new
  intent + sample, `ShapeOwnerEditor` the Details label / title-key / loader,
  `ShapeTestTab` the input-form payload. The list, Instances, and Schema
  mounts register nothing, so those routes offer an agent no tool at all. A
  kind's SLUG, its visibility, and activation are unwritable by design —
  identity, publishing, and a dual-gate verdict respectively), and
  `matrx-user/marketing-crawls` (3 ask-policy DRAFT targets staging the
  crawl command — `crawl_options` as one partial-patch object for the limits,
  render mode and toggles, plus `crawl_include_patterns` /
  `crawl_exclude_patterns` as full-list replacements, because a crawl's SCOPE
  is the decision a user wants to review on its own rather than buried in a
  settings blob; handlers on `NewCrawlWorkspace`'s own provider, landing
  through the same setters the user's typing uses. The sessions-list mount
  registers NONE on purpose — immutable crawl records behind pure table query
  state. Starting the crawl stays human: it spends real time and budget
  against the client's own server), and
  `matrx-user/image-generate` (ONE ask-policy draft target,
  `generation_request` — a partial `{prompt?, style?, image_size?,
  image_count?}` object handled in
  `app/(core)/images/generate/GenerateShellClient.tsx` through the same
  `useState` setters the user's own typing and Select clicks call. THE
  reference for a COMPOSITE target: those four fields are ONE request the user
  composes in a single thought, `generation_request_summary` is already their
  composite read twin, and every key is optional — `{image_size}` alone leaves
  a typed prompt untouched — so one confirm dialog covers one request instead
  of four in a row. Following the `podcast-studio` precedent, **Generate is
  deliberately NOT a target**: starting a run spends real money on image
  models, so the human press stays the gate. Results, status flags, and
  anything about saved files stay undeclared, and the handler THROWS rather
  than editing a request that is already in flight),
  `matrx-user/image-studio` (3 ask-policy draft targets on the `/images/convert`
  batch converter, all registered in `ImageStudioShell`'s `getWriteHandlers` and
  all calling the same setters the panel's own controls call: `selected_presets`
  (full-list replace of WHICH outputs get made, validated against the real
  preset catalog), `conversion_settings` (a partial patch of HOW they are
  encoded, whose `resize_position` is REFUSED outright unless the call resolves
  to the cropping fit — the anchor picker is off screen under the others, so
  staging one would hide the value from the confirm dialog), and
  `filename_base` (a partial map of what the outputs are CALLED, keyed by an
  image's 1-based position / original name / current base — THE reference for
  addressing rows that have NO durable id: the handler resolves the agent's
  coordinate back to the internal file id and refuses a key matching zero or
  more than one image, validates the whole map before the first rename lands,
  and refuses a rename that would leave two images sharing a base because their
  variants would overwrite each other). Generate, Save to library, Download and
  AI Describe all stay the user's press — Generate because it DISCARDS the
  session's existing variants and because the page deliberately gates the first
  click behind its own rename banner), and
  `matrx-user/cms-site` (6 ask-policy targets, and THE reference for
  TAB-SCOPED registration: five DRAFT targets on the Settings tab —
  `site_global_css` / `site_theme_config` / `site_navigation` /
  `site_footer_config` (2026-08-10) plus `site_name`, all staging into the
  same `useState`s the form's own inputs drive, so the human's per-card Save
  stays the only writer — and ONE `entity` target on a DIFFERENT tab,
  `add_page`, creating an UNPUBLISHED empty page stub through the same
  `CmsPageService.createPage` the New Page route calls, then `refreshPages()`
  so `pages_summary` / `site_structure` re-read true. Handlers live on each
  tab's own nested provider (`app/(core)/cms/[siteId]/settings/page.tsx` and
  `app/(core)/cms/[siteId]/page.tsx`) plus `SiteAdvancedSettings` via
  `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, and because `listAgentWritableTargets()` skips
  any target with no live handler, each tab offers an agent exactly the tools
  it can service — one manifest, two disjoint tool sets, no
  declared-but-unwired defect. The split also carries the policy line: the
  staging targets are legal even on a `blocked` site because staging is not
  saving, while `add_page` genuinely writes and so is the ONE target that
  refuses under `agent_write_policy: "blocked"`. Slug, domain, favicon, the
  active flag and delete stay human — identity, assets, and taking a site off
  the air), and
  `matrx-user/war-room` (4 ask-policy ENTITY targets on the room cockpit —
  `room_name` / `room_description` through `updateRoomIdentity`,
  `active_thread_title` through `renameThread`, and `add_threads`
  (decomposition, the `add_subtasks` shape) through `createThread`; handlers in
  `features/war-room/components/room/useWarRoomWriteHandlers.ts`, registered on
  the provider `WarRoomShell` already mounted. Everything is `entity` because
  the room has NO staging buffer — the identity popover and the thread-title
  input both persist on commit — so the ask dialog IS the review step. THE
  reference for CONFIRMING an optimistic write: `updateRoomIdentity` rolls back
  and reports on a server rejection, so the handler fails on its `false`;
  `renameThread` and `createThread` swallow their errors into a toast, so those
  handlers re-read the store and throw when the value did not land. Without
  that, an agent would confidently report a write the server refused.
  `active_thread_title` refuses loudly when no thread is staged rather than
  writing into nothing. Room/project/organization ids, resource assignments,
  recording, and thread delete/park/pin stay undeclared, as do
  `view_mode` / `density` / `projected_tab` — mechanical view toggles nobody
  would ask an agent to flip), and
  `matrx-user/chat-voice` (3 ask-policy DRAFT targets over the Voice
  Playground's settings sheet — `voice_instructions` /
  `append_voice_instructions` (the `agent-builder` replace/append pair applied
  to a VOICE prompt) and `voice_id`, validated against the `VOICES` constant
  the picker itself renders so the model is handed `ara | eve | leo | rex |
  sal` and an invented id comes back as the handler's error. Handlers in
  `features/voice-agent/hooks/useVoicePlaygroundWriteHandlers.ts`, dispatching
  the same `updateConfig` the textarea and select fire; registered by
  `PlaygroundSettingsSheet`, so the `/chat/voice` intro route mounts the
  provider but offers an agent no write tool. **THE reference for a guard that
  must read CURRENT state:** every handler refuses while a voice session is
  live, and `applySurfaceWrite` resolves handlers BEFORE the confirm dialog, so
  the guard reads `store.getState()` at call time rather than a render
  snapshot. The transcript, the mic, the connection, and the realtime tool set
  all stay unwritable — the record of what was said, device state, and what the
  voice agent may reach, respectively),
  `matrx-user/chat` (2 ask-policy targets on the live `/chat` route —
  `input_draft`, a `{text, mode?: replace | append}` DRAFT landing through the
  same `setUserInputText` action every composer keystroke dispatches, and
  `conversation_title`, an ENTITY rename through the canonical
  `renameConversation` thunk. Handlers on `ChatRoomClient`'s own provider,
  which is the surface's ONLY `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` and backs all three
  routes (`/chat/new` via `ChatNewClient`'s `landingContent`, `/chat/a/[id]`,
  `/chat/[id]`), so both handlers key off the conversation actually on the
  page — the other three references to this surface name
  (`RunSettingsEditor`, `RunControlsTabPanel`, `NewChatLandingInput`) are v3
  context-menu wiring that mounts no runtime and therefore offers an agent
  nothing. **THE record-vs-draft reference.** Chat is a RECORD wrapped around
  one authored field, and that asymmetry decides everything: the composer
  holds the message the user has NOT sent yet, so drafting into it is the
  textbook agent case and is fully reversible; the transcript is what was
  actually said by whom, so `all_messages` / `last_user_message` /
  `current_message_text` / `full_conversation_text` get NO target — an agent
  rewriting them does not edit a draft, it fabricates history. The route HAS a
  user-driven "Edit & resubmit" path; it stays a human affordance precisely
  because the record's value is that a human wrote it. SENDING is equally
  undeclared: the send is the outward-facing act that spends a turn, bills a
  model, and can steer or interrupt a run in flight through the inbox modes —
  the human press is the gate, as it is for `podcast-studio` and
  `image-generate`. Also undeclared: the whole run configuration
  (`added_tools` / `added_skills` / `model` / `sandbox_binding`), on
  `agent-builder`'s reasoning that changing what an agent may REACH is a
  capability change and not a copy edit; `attached_resources`, whose read half
  publishes only lean refs to what is already attached and never the user's
  library, so an agent could only guess UUIDs (the `education-fastfire`
  set-picker line); switching the bound agent, since `initial_agent_id` is
  read-only provenance by this feature's own model; `working_document` /
  `scratchpad`, which are lean refs here and belong to their own surfaces; and
  `variable_values`, which is NOT capability governance — a variable is
  substituted into a prompt, not something a run reaches — but fails the
  earlier test, because this surface mounts no editor for it (every
  `setUserVariableValue` dispatcher lives outside `components/chat/`, and
  `ChatRoomClient` only reads it to publish it). The title handler carries the
  surface's one non-obvious guard: a fresh chat holds a CLIENT-MINTED id and
  has no `chat.conversation` row until its first turn commits, so it gates on
  the same `waitForConversationPersisted` predicate URL promotion uses (as a
  single probe) and REFUSES rather than firing an UPDATE that matches zero
  rows while every optimistic mirror shows the new name), and
  `matrx-user/quick-tasks` (8 ask-policy targets across the overlay's TWO
  owners — `quick_add_title` (draft) and `quick_create_task` (entity, a
  `{title, description?, priority?, due_date?}` object) registered by
  `QuickTasksMain`, plus `panel_task_title` / `_description` / `_priority` /
  `_due_date` / `_labels` as DRAFTS and `panel_add_subtasks` as an ENTITY
  action registered by `TaskDetailsPanel`. **THE reference for why an overlay
  must not reuse a route surface's target NAMES.** This window floats over
  whatever page the user is on, and `OverlayController` mounts it from
  `DeferredSingletonCore` — an app-level singleton — so its provider registers
  shallow and a ROUTE's provider can out-depth it. `applySurfaceWrite` resolves
  a name against the deepest surface that DECLARES it, so had these been
  spelled `task_title` / `task_description` like `matrx-user/tasks`, a write
  meant for the window would land in `/tasks`'s `TaskEditorBody` behind it —
  where the user who just confirmed the dialog cannot see it. Distinct
  `panel_*` names make the owner unambiguous and each description names its
  pane. `TaskDetailsPanel` is shared with `/tasks` and the Quick Tasks sheet,
  so it registers only when a mount passes the new nullable `writeSurfaceName`
  prop — the `quick-note-save` gating pattern, and the reason the other two
  mounts of the same component offer an agent nothing. **Saving the panel is
  deliberately NOT a target, and the reason generalizes:** `tasks` can offer
  `save_task` because its draft lives in Redux and the save thunk re-reads the
  store, but this panel's draft is local React state, so a save handler would
  close over the render that registered it — stage a description and save in
  one agent turn and the save can run against the PRE-staged values. Deleting
  a task and toggling complete stay human; the hierarchy cascade
  (organization / scope / project) and `selected_task_id` are navigation and
  ownership context, not authored content, and the window emits the selected
  ids without the lists to pick from; `search_query` / `show_all_projects` are
  mechanical view state. `quick_add_title` closes the details pane before
  staging, because the quick-add input only renders in the empty state and
  staging into a box the user cannot see is worse than not staging at all. The
  surface also gained flat `selected_task_*` read twins for the evidence loop,
  emitted from the SAVED record — a staged panel edit does not echo back
  through them. **That last gap is now closed by `selected_task_draft`**
  (2026-08-12), which emits what the panel's inputs hold RIGHT NOW —
  `{task_id, title, description, due_date, priority, labels, is_dirty}` —
  published by `TaskDetailsPanel` into a ref the window's `getScope` reads and
  nulled on unmount, the `task-create` `surfaceDraftRef` pattern. It is what
  makes the `panel_*` drafts genuinely round-trippable: the three targets that
  tell an agent to "read the current value first if you mean to build on it"
  now point at a value that reflects an edit already staged, instead of one
  that hands back the pre-stage text and silently loses the agent's own
  earlier write).
  `matrx-user/war-room-thread` (4 ask-policy targets on ONE thread's agent
  panel — `thread_note_content` / `append_to_thread_note` as DRAFTS (the
  `agent-builder` replace/append pair applied to the note anchored to the
  thread, landing through the same `updateNoteContent` dispatch that
  `ThreadNotesTab`'s editor `onChange` fires) plus `thread_task_title` /
  `thread_task_status` as ENTITY writes through `updateTaskFieldThunk`, the
  thunk `ThreadTaskTab` already dispatches; the status enum validates against
  the canonical `TASK_STATUSES` the description interpolates, so the offered
  and accepted vocabularies cannot drift. Handlers in
  [`features/war-room/components/thread/useWarRoomThreadWriteHandlers.ts`](../war-room/components/thread/useWarRoomThreadWriteHandlers.ts),
  registered on the SAME provider inside `ThreadAgentPanel` that emits the
  read twins — which is the point: `ThreadTabContent` is a `switch`, so only
  one tab body is mounted at a time, and handlers registered from
  `ThreadNotesTab` / `ThreadTaskTab` would have been unregistered exactly when
  the agent tab was on screen, which is the only time an agent runs here. THE
  reference for **a lazily-loaded read twin**: a thread whose Notes tab was
  never opened has no record in the notes slice at all, so both note handlers
  hydrate through `fetchNoteContent` BEFORE writing and then read the buffer
  back and throw if it did not take — "not loaded" is never the same answer as
  "empty", and a draft target that silently no-ops is worse than one that
  refuses. Deliberately undeclared: RECORDING (it captures a live human
  conversation — the `podcast-studio` / `image-generate` line), the transcripts
  and the conversation (evidence, the `markdown-editor` `processed_data`/`ast`
  rule), and the thread TITLE — already writable one level up as
  `active_thread_title` on `matrx-user/war-room`, and a second declaration
  would be the second write path into one row the doctrine forbids).
  `matrx-user/content-plan-entities` (4 targets on the E-E-A-T roster, all
  registered by `EntityManager.tsx` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`
  against a parent-owned `draftRef` — `entity_draft` (`draft`/ask, stages
  `{label?, entity_type?, source_type_id?}` into the OPEN editor dialog),
  `save_entity_draft` (`entity`/ask, commits that draft through the dialog's
  own create/update path), `add_entities` (`entity`/ask, appends roster rows
  through `createPlanEntity` with duplicate-label refusal and a post-write
  label check) and `open_entity_editor` (`ui`/auto, the parent surface's
  `select_node` precedent). Deletion, node↔entity attachment and identity
  columns stay human. **THE reference for unblocking a "no option list"
  exclusion.** `source_type_id` was refused for exactly the reason
  `content-plan-node` still keeps `node_primary_keyword_id` manual — a category
  UUID an agent could only guess at — and the fix was to DECLARE THE VOCABULARY
  rather than loosen the check: `source_type_options` publishes `{id, name}`
  from the same `useCategories(planSourceType)` read the dialog's
  `CategorySelect` renders, and the handler refuses any id absent from it,
  including while the dimension is still loading (an empty list cannot tell a
  dangling FK from a real one). It is `autoContext: true` on purpose — shipped
  fetch-on-demand it came back `context_not_attached` in a live run and the
  agent correctly refused to invent a UUID, which makes the target unusable.
  **The PRECONDITION TRAP this surface used to carry is now resolved in code:**
  `entity_draft` throws unless the editor is open, and that dialog is MODAL —
  its `fixed inset-0 z-[10000]` `aria-hidden` overlay covers the header Agents
  button AND the chat composer — so `open_entity_editor` takes an id from
  `entities_detail` (or null for a blank dialog) and lets the agent do the open
  and the stage in ONE turn, which is what its description instructs. The
  remaining constraint is only that a USER cannot type a follow-up while the
  modal is up), and
  `matrx-admin/ai-models` (2 ask-policy ENTITY targets over the only AUTHORED
  COPY on a model row — `model_description` and `model_common_name` — through
  `aiModelService.update`, the exact call the panel's Save button makes.
  Handlers are registered by `AiModelDetailPanel` via
  `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, NOT by `AiModelsContainer`: the container mounts
  the provider and owns the catalogue, but the detail pane owns the form
  state, the save path and the `saving` flag a write must respect. Both are
  inside the SAME provider — unlike `schedules` or `shapes` the pane is a
  child of the list mount, not a second surface mount — so the list registers
  nothing, because the only state it owns is browse state. **THE reference for
  building the human correction path BEFORE the target.** `description` is a
  real column rendered to USERS as the second line of every model-picker row,
  and this manifest already declared it as a read value, but it had NO editor
  anywhere: the Details form had no field and the Raw JSON tab STRIPPED it as
  an unknown column. That is the `admin-tool-registry` `tool_group` one-way
  door. Rather than declare it anyway or drop the surface's only prose field,
  the same change added the Description textarea to `AiModelForm` and put
  `description` in the panel's column whitelist — the fix an admin needs to
  correct an agent exists first, and the target follows it. Capabilities,
  context window, max tokens, the deprecated/primary/premium flags,
  visibility, the fallback FKs and the cost/speed ratings all stay human: each
  changes what the platform DOES with the model for every caller on it, the
  same line `tool_is_active` / `tool_gating` drew. `model_name` is the hardest
  no — it is the provider-facing dispatch key and the join key the
  provider-cache lookup matches on, so a wrong one breaks every call site at
  once. Handlers additionally refuse, loudly and by throw, when the panel is
  in create-new mode (`is_creating_model`), when a save is already in flight,
  and when the Raw JSON tab is dirty — that tab saves the WHOLE row, so an
  unsaved edit there would silently overwrite whatever the agent just wrote), and
  `matrx-user/agent-run` (3 ask-policy targets on the standalone run route —
  `user_input_draft` (draft, the `{text, mode: "replace" | "append"}` composer
  pair) and `variable_values` (draft, a PARTIAL per-key patch validated at
  apply time against the agent's own `agent_variable_definitions`, so an
  undefined name comes back as the declared list and NOTHING is applied), plus
  `conversation_title` (entity, through the canonical `renameConversation`
  thunk behind a `waitForConversationPersisted` probe — a fresh run holds a
  client-minted id and the rename would match zero rows). Handlers in
  `features/agents/components/run/AgentRunnerPage.tsx`, past its
  `isAgentRunSurface` gate, so the `/code` workspace that reuses the SAME
  component registers no provider and is offered nothing. **THE reference for
  a run/instrument surface:** everything the run PRODUCED — `agent_response`,
  `agent_reasoning`, `all_messages`, `tool_calls`, `completion_stats`,
  `is_streaming`, `run_status` — stays unwritable, because writing it would
  forge the record the user opened this page to read; and the whole `agent_*`
  contract (instruction, model, tools, variable DEFINITIONS) stays with
  `matrx-user/agent-builder`, since editing the contract from inside the
  instrument that measures it means the run on screen is no longer a run of
  the agent now described. Executing is not a target either — it spends real
  tokens, so the agent stages and the human presses send. Deliberately NO
  mid-stream guard, unlike `chat-voice`: per the chat feature's Flow 6 a send
  during a run QUEUES or steers and the composer stays live, so refusing while
  streaming would re-impose the hard lock removed on 2026-07-29), and
  `matrx-user/analysis-studio` (4 ask-policy targets on the PDF workbench —
  `annotation_label` / `annotation_extracted_text` / `annotation_redact` as
  `entity` through `useAnnotations().update` (the same mutator the region
  context menu and the canvas drag already use), plus
  `studio_focus_annotation` as `ui`. Handlers in
  `features/file-analysis/studio/analysis-studio-write-handlers.ts`, built at
  APPLY time through `StudioShell`'s provider ref. The agent NAMES what the
  human DREW: creating an annotation stays human because it is inescapably
  spatial — the user drags a rectangle and the server snaps it to the
  tightest text-block bounds — so an agent could only invent a `bbox`.
  `label_category` is resolved against the live label CATALOG rather than
  guessed. Delete, geometry, and `canvas_mode` stay undeclared).
  `matrx-user/connections-skills` (5 ask-policy DRAFT targets over the skill
  editor — `skill_label`, `skill_description`, `skill_type` (validated against
  the `KNOWN_SKILL_TYPES` list the Type dropdown itself renders), `skill_body`
  (the whole markdown document inlined into an agent's system prompt) and
  `skill_trigger_patterns` (full-set replacement). Handlers in
  `features/skills/components/SkillDetailEditor.tsx`, staging through the same
  `set(key, value)` helper every input's `onChange` calls, so an applied write
  is literally a keystroke the agent typed and the Save button stays the
  human's. **THE reference for a draft whose read twin lives in a DIFFERENT
  component:** the emitter is `SkillsSection`, but the draft is `useState` one
  level down, so `getScope` could not see it and the only declared twin
  (`selected_skill_summary`) reports the SAVED row — a draft target whose twin
  never moves is an evidence loop that lies. The editor therefore hands a
  `ConnectionsSkillsDraftSnapshot` up through an `onDraftSnapshot` callback
  into a ref that `getScope` spreads at Run time, giving the new
  `skill_draft_*` twins the STAGED value while `selected_skill_summary` keeps
  reporting the saved row. Both new props are optional, so the super-admin
  console's mount of the same editor declares no targets and is untouched (the
  `quick-note-save` gating pattern, expressed as props rather than a second
  provider). `skill_id` (the identity every agent inclusion resolves against),
  delete, and `is_system` / `is_public` stay human-only — identity, destruction
  and blast radius; `version` / `model_preference` / `icon_name` /
  `disable_auto_invocation` are bookkeeping and cosmetics nobody would ask an
  agent to flip. A system skill viewed by a non-admin renders the whole form
  read-only, and every handler THROWS in that state rather than staging into a
  form the user cannot save), and
  `matrx-user/data-tables` (2 ask-policy ENTITY targets on `/data/[id]` —
  `table_description` through the canonical `updateTableMetadata` service, and
  `cell_value`, ONE cell named by an explicit `{row_id, field_name, value}`
  object, through `upsertCell` (`udt_upsert_cell`, the surgical `jsonb_set` the
  grid's own inline editor calls). Handlers in
  `features/data-tables/hooks/useDataTableWriteHandlers.ts`. This change also
  MOUNTED the surface's FIRST `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` and wrote its first
  emitter (`agent-context/buildDataTablesScope.ts`) — the manifest had declared
  14 values with `readiness: "stub"` and no runtime at all, so every one of
  them was a read lie. **THE reference for a target whose COORDINATES must
  travel with the value.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler before the
  confirm dialog is answered, so a cell write that read "the current cell" off
  a render closure would land in whatever cell the grid pointed at by the time
  Apply was pressed — silently writing the WRONG cell, which on a grid is worse
  than refusing. So row + column + value are ONE object the agent must have
  READ off the page, checked at apply time against live state published through
  a ref reassigned every render, and REFUSED (with what to re-read) when they
  no longer resolve. The second bound is visibility: the row must be on the
  page currently ON SCREEN — `displayRows`, i.e. after column filters — so a
  cell an agent writes is one the user watches change. `is_read_only` was added
  as a new read value because both handlers gate on it, and it is deliberately
  three-valued in the ref (null = "not loaded, refuse"): `isReadOnly` computes
  to `false` before the table row and the signed-in user have loaded, and false
  means writable. The provider is OPT-IN via `emitSurfaceScope`, which only
  `DataTableDetailClient` passes, because `UserTableViewer` is also rendered
  inside `DatasetOverlay`, `ViewTableModal` and the `UserTableWindow` panel —
  and since `listLiveWriteTargets()` walks the whole mounted stack, an
  unconditional provider would offer THIS surface's targets on someone else's
  page (the `matrx-user/lists` `asRoute` gate, expressed as a prop). The `/data`
  LIST route mounts nothing: table cards, no authored state. Declined, and each
  for a different reason: `table_name` / `table_id` are identity; `table_schema`
  / `column_list` are STRUCTURE, where `udt_change_field_type` rewrites every
  row and un-castable values become null — a migration wearing a form's
  clothes, with no undo; `full_table_json` / `visible_data_csv` /
  `current_row_json` are EVIDENCE, not write paths, because no user can review
  a 400-row diff in a confirm dialog (ten cells means ten calls, slower on
  purpose); row delete stays human; and `search_term` is the user's own filter.
  `selected_range` was DELETED from the manifest in the same change: the grid
  has no multi-cell selection concept at all, so nothing could ever emit it —
  its DB mirror row was purged after confirming zero bindings referenced it.
  **One honest caveat about blast radius that is NOT about this seam:** the
  bound agent also carries server-side dataset tools of its own, and during
  verification it replaced the fixture table's rows through one of those after
  being asked to. The surface write tool itself behaved correctly — asked to
  enumerate its targets it named exactly `table_description` and `cell_value`
  and refused `table_name` and `search_term` — so the narrow posture here does
  not by itself bound what an agent can do to a user table. That is a separate
  tool surface, and worth a look on its own.
  `matrx-user/smart-code-editor` (the SAME trio as `matrx-user/code-editor` —
  `replace_selection` / `insert_at_cursor` / `content` — on the floating
  4-column agentic editor, handlers on the `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` this change
  MOUNTED in `SmartCodeEditor.tsx` (the surface had none, and that shared core
  backs both the window and `SmartCodeEditorModal`). The whole-buffer target is
  named `content`, matching THIS surface's read value, not `code-editor`'s
  `current_file_content`. **THE reference for an editor whose canonical state
  path is NOT undo-safe:** this Monaco is uncontrolled — React state flows back
  down as `initialCode` and `SmallCodeEditorImpl` applies out-of-band changes
  with `model.setValue()`, which discards undo history — so writing through the
  React setter, which is what "reuse the path the user's typing takes" naively
  suggests, would have cost the user their undo stack. The handlers edit the
  MODEL directly (`pushEditOperations` between undo stops), which fires the
  change event that feeds the same `handleContentChange` typing feeds: state
  converges, the `setValue` branch never trips, and ⌘Z reverses an agent edit
  like a paste. Model-level edits also survive this editor's read-only DEFAULT
  (it opens with the pencil off), which the widget's `executeEdits` would have
  swallowed silently. Nothing here persists at all — `ephemeral: true` window,
  in-memory files, no Save control anywhere — so the targets stay `draft` (the
  honest classification of the stakes) while the descriptions avoid leaning on
  the seam's "nothing is saved until you save", which is literally true but
  implies a Save this surface does not have. `editor_title` was considered and
  REJECTED: an opener-supplied prop with no canonical write path and no user
  gesture that renames the window, so a handler would have to invent state
  nobody else writes. Diagnostics and the git/workspace values are a report of
  where the code came from, not an input; file lifecycle and handing code back
  to the opener stay human).
  than letting an agent read a stale value as confirmation), and
  `matrx-user/transcript-scribe-live` (2 ask-policy ENTITY targets over the ONE
  artifact the Live tab exists to build — `working_document_content` (full
  replace) and `append_working_document` (the replace/append pair, where append
  is documented as the default because replace overwrites the user's work).
  Handlers in
  [`features/transcript-studio/hooks/useScribeLiveWriteHandlers.ts`](../transcript-studio/hooks/useScribeLiveWriteHandlers.ts),
  registered on `ScribeLiveScreen`'s existing provider, both landing through
  `updateWorkingDocumentContentThunk` — the same thunk the editor's debounced
  autosave fires on every keystroke. **THE reference for a surface that already
  had a bespoke agent path serving a DIFFERENT agent population.** This tab
  already let the client-side VOICE agent mutate the document through realtime
  client tools (`scribe_working_doc_append` / `_append_heading`), but those live
  inside the xAI turn loop and are reachable only by the agent the user is
  TALKING to; an agent launched from the header Agents popover is an ordinary
  turn-based run with no realtime socket, so it could already READ
  `working_document_content` and had no way to write a word of it back. These
  targets close that half of the loop for the second population, and add full
  replacement, which the realtime mutators deliberately never offered.
  **`entity`, not `draft`, and deliberately no phase guard** — the mirror image
  of the sibling `matrx-user/chat-voice`. There is no draft/save bar here, only
  an autosaving editor, so `draft` would be a lie; and where chat-voice REFUSES
  mid-session because its config is consumed once at connect, this document is
  MEANT to change mid-session — `useWorkingDocumentDraft` explicitly merges
  remote edits in whenever the user is not actively typing, so a live-session
  guard would break the collaboration loop rather than protect it. The document
  row is resolved from the store at CALL time (`selectWorkingDocument`, which
  filters on `kind === "working_document"`), because `applySurfaceWrite`
  resolves the handler BEFORE showing the confirm dialog and an append built on
  a body captured in a render closure would silently drop whatever the user or
  the voice agent wrote while the dialog sat open. A session with no working
  document yet refuses loudly rather than creating one. Identity
  (`working_document_id`, `session_id`, `live_agent_id`,
  `voice_conversation_id`), the derived `working_document_word_count`, the
  microphone and connection state, and the spoken transcript with its per-turn
  and interruption record are all undeclared — the last of those because
  writing it would forge what was actually said), and
  `matrx-admin/bundles` (2 ask-policy DRAFT targets on the tool-registry
  bundles console — `new_bundle_draft` (`{name?, description?}` staged into
  the "New bundle" form) and `bundle_description` (the selected bundle's
  description in the inline detail panel). Handlers + scope in
  [`features/tool-registry/bundles/components/BundlesSurfaceRuntime.tsx`](../tool-registry/bundles/components/BundlesSurfaceRuntime.tsx),
  which also had to MOUNT the surface's first `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` — the
  `matrx-admin/lookups` page-scoped-store shape, because the two editors are
  owned by children that only mount while they are on screen. **THE reference
  for splitting rather than collapsing a partial-patch object:** `email_draft`
  and `lookup_draft` are one object because one open dialog is the review unit
  and one Save commits it; here the two targets do not even share a RECORD —
  one stages a bundle that does not exist yet behind a modal committed by
  "Create bundle", the other rewrites copy on an existing row in an inline
  panel committed by "Save". Collapsed, the destination (and whether `name` is
  accepted) would flip on whether a URL param happens to be set, so a
  mis-inference would pop a stray create form while the admin's selected
  bundle sat untouched beside it; split, the agent's CHOICE OF TARGET is its
  declaration of intent and that is what the admin reads in the confirm.
  A bundle's NAME is writable on CREATE and refused on an EXISTING row, for a
  reason this surface can show rather than assert: `tool.bundle` has
  `UNIQUE (name)` and every bundle's lister tool is named `bundle:list_<name>`
  (verified live — `agent-core` → `bundle:list_agent-core`), that lister name
  is what a MODEL calls, and `updateBundle` PATCHes the bundle row only, so
  renaming leaves every agent still calling `bundle:list_<old-name>`. Same
  reasoning refuses a member's `local_alias` — the alias IS the identifier the
  model invokes once `bundle_lister` hot-swaps members in. The Active toggle
  (`listAgentBundleOptions` filters on it), the System switch, the metadata
  JSON (it carries `server_slug` / `lister_tool_id` dispatch pointers) and
  MEMBERSHIP are capability, not copy. There is no delete on this console at
  all — `bundles.service.ts` exposes none — which is just as well: agents hold
  a bundle by its lister tool's UUID in their own `tools` array. Two traps the
  lookups chip paid for and this one inherited: the create form is a Radix
  MODAL, so the handler OPENS it itself (an admin cannot open it and THEN
  reach the chat), and its `DialogContent` needs
  `onInteractOutside={preventDefault}` or dismissing the surface-write confirm
  closes the form and discards what was just staged — both re-confirmed live
  here, the second by staging, declining a SECOND write, and watching the
  form survive with its values intact).
  writing it would forge what was actually said),
  `matrx-admin/email` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target on the Email Users compose
  tool — `email_draft`, a partial `{subject?, message_body?}` staged through the
  same `setSubject` / `setMessage` the admin's own typing calls; handler on the
  `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` that `AdminEmailPage` had to MOUNT, since the
  manifest previously had no emitter at all. Validation lives in the pure
  [`features/admin/shared/email-compose-draft.ts`](../admin/shared/email-compose-draft.ts),
  which the manifest description also interpolates its bounds from, so the
  contract an agent reads IS the contract enforced. **THE reference for a
  surface where the dangerous half is WHO, not what.** Subject and body are
  textbook authored copy; the recipients are not, and they sit on the same
  form. `recipient_mode`, `custom_emails_raw` and `selected_user_ids` decide who
  on the platform receives real mail, so they stay undeclared — an admin
  reviewing prose in a confirm dialog is not auditing a recipient list, and
  widening an announcement from three addresses to the whole user base is a
  different class of mistake from rewriting a sentence. `custom_from` is sender
  identity and stays human on the spoofing rule. SENDING is deliberately not a
  target — the `image-generate` / `marketing-crawls` / `podcast-studio` ORDER
  vs FIRE line, drawn here against the one action on that list that cannot be
  undone at all once it leaves. Composite over split follows `image-generate`'s
  `generation_request` and not `marketing-crawls`' scope/intensity split:
  subject and body are ONE piece of copy, so one dialog covers one email rather
  than leaving a mismatched half-applied pair. There is no `apply_template`
  target either — a template only copies into those same two fields, so it
  would be a second door onto state `email_draft` already owns; the agent reads
  `available_templates` and writes the template's copy through the one target.
  The in-flight guard reads `loading` through a REF, not the render closure,
  because the seam resolves handlers before the first dialog is answered),
  `matrx-admin/feedback` (2 ask-policy DRAFT targets over the only AUTHORED
  COPY on the Feedback & Announcements console — `announcement_draft`, ONE
  partial-patch object carrying `{title?, message?, announcement_type?}` (the
  `matrx-admin/email` `email_draft` call: a title, its body and the severity it
  is written at are one piece of copy reviewed by one Save, not three
  independently declinable decisions), and `category_draft`
  (`{name?, description?}` on the Categories tab — a SECOND target rather than
  more keys on the first, because a taxonomy entry is a different object in a
  different tab saved by a different button). Contracts are pure modules
  (`features/admin/feedback/announcement-draft.ts`, `category-draft.ts`) that
  the manifest interpolates into the model-facing description AND the handler
  calls, so the advertised contract IS the enforced one; `ANNOUNCEMENT_TYPES`
  was added to `types/feedback.types.ts` with `AnnouncementType` derived from
  it, the `FEEDBACK_TYPES` pattern, so the enum check reads the same array the
  `<Select>` renders. This change also MOUNTED the surface's first
  `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (`readiness: "stub"` → `partial`).
  **THE reference for a MODAL editor an agent could otherwise never reach.** A
  Radix modal sets `pointer-events: none` on the body, so an admin cannot open
  the announcement dialog and THEN ask for help — verified live: with the Edit
  dialog open the chat composer will not accept input at all, even through
  React's native setter. A target that only writes into an already-open dialog
  is correct-looking dead code, so `announcement_draft` OPENS the create dialog
  itself when none is open (the `CreateStoreInline` move), and both dialogs
  gained `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` — without it the
  SECOND confirm in a two-call turn renders outside the dialog the FIRST call
  just opened and closes it, discarding the staged copy (proven: two write
  calls in one turn, dialog survived, both fields landed). Because two
  components own "the announcement editor", handlers could not be registered
  per-component — `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` from both would be a last-one-wins
  collision, not a mode gate — so the dialogs and the categories tab publish
  HANDLES into a page-scoped ref registry (`FeedbackConsoleEditorStore.tsx`)
  and the container owns one handler per target that resolves which editor is
  live, prefers the open Edit dialog, and REFUSES rather than guessing when
  both are open. Every guard is read off that ref at call time, never the
  render closure `resolveHandlers` captured before the confirm. Validation runs
  on the WHOLE payload BEFORE anything opens, so a rejected write leaves no
  stray empty dialog behind (verified: an invalid `announcement_type` returned
  the enum verbatim to the agent and opened nothing). Deliberately undeclared:
  PUBLISHING — no create/save target and no `is_active`, because an
  announcement interrupts every user at login, the same line
  `matrx-admin/email` drew at Send; `min_display_seconds`, the seconds every
  user is FORCED to wait before dismissing it, which is an imposition on other
  people's time rather than copy; and the whole feedback TRIAGE vocabulary —
  `status`, `priority`, `work_priority`, `admin_decision`, `assigned_to`,
  `category_id` — which is governance, not authoring. The
  `FeedbackDetailDialog` fields that look most agent-shaped (`admin_direction`,
  `admin_notes`, the user-reply compose box) are omitted on REACHABILITY, not
  taste: that dialog is modal too, and the only way to make them live would be
  the handler opening a feedback record itself, i.e. the agent choosing WHICH
  user's report to act on. If that triage view ever grows a non-modal path,
  those three are the first fields to add).
  `matrx-admin/agent-slots` (2 ask-policy targets on the slots console —
  `slot_exemplar_draft`, a partial `{label?, variables?, user_input?}` object
  staged into the test bench's "+ Exemplar" composer by `SlotTestBench` via
  `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, plus `select_slot` as `ui`, implemented on
  `AgentSlotsConsole`'s own provider because that component owns `selectedId`.
  **THE reference for an admin surface where almost nothing earns a target.**
  Health, the roll-ups and the unhealthy list are MEASURED, so writing them
  would fabricate the state of the system; repin and the per-principal
  `slot_binding` overrides are agent identity by UUID over live production
  capacity, with no agent catalog on the surface to pick from — the
  `pdf-extractor` `agent_id` deferral; enable/disable and the bench's Run all
  spend real capacity and budget. What is left is the one genuinely AUTHORED
  artifact on the page: an exemplar, the stored real input the bench replays
  against every candidate, which an agent composes from the slot's contract
  far faster than an admin hand-typing JSON. Three new read values feed it —
  `selected_slot_contract` (the `required_variables` the exemplar must fill),
  `selected_slot_exemplars`, and `slot_exemplar_draft` itself — and because
  the composer lives in a grandchild that mounts only while a workbench is
  open, the bench publishes them upward through `bench-draft.ts` for the
  console's `getScope`. `select_slot` is `"ask"`, NOT `content-plan`'s
  `select_node` `"auto"`: this workbench remounts per slot, so moving the
  selection discards whatever is typed in the repin, override and exemplar
  editors — and the handler refuses outright while an unsaved exemplar draft
  is staged. **Carries the id-guarded answer to the resolve-before-confirm
  trap:** handlers are resolved before the first dialog is answered, so a
  bench that has since unmounted still holds a live-looking closure over its
  own setters; each bench compares `readSlotBenchOwner()` against its own
  registration id and throws rather than staging into a composer that is no
  longer on screen).
  `matrx-user/documents` (2 ask-policy ENTITY targets on the DOCUMENT route —
  `document_name` and `document_description`, the two human-authored fields on
  `udt_documents` — through the canonical `document-service` setters
  `renameDocument` and a new `updateDocumentDescription` sibling, added by this
  adoption exactly as `updateWorkbookDescription` was added by the workbooks
  one, because `document-service` carries a standing contract to mirror
  `workbook-service`. `commitRename` was refactored into one `applyRename`
  that the header field's blur/Enter commit AND the write target both call, so
  an agent rename is indistinguishable from the user typing it. `entity` for
  the `workbook_name` / `ScheduleDetail` reason — this route has no Save bar,
  so `draft`'s "nothing is saved until you save" would be a lie. **Carries the
  STALE-CLOSURE fix as ordinary practice rather than a discovery:** handlers
  read the row through a `docRef` advanced synchronously by the one
  `commitDocument` that also sets state, because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves
  a target's handler BEFORE awaiting its confirm — verified live, where the
  agent staged both targets in one turn and the seam fired the DESCRIPTION
  dialog first. **The judgment call worth reusing is `document_summary`,**
  which reads like the jackpot ("summarize this document") and is a trap:
  there is no summary column on `udt_documents` — that value is the composite
  READ-TWIN of the whole identity group, so writing it would mean writing
  eleven fields, nine of them identity, provenance or timestamps. It is the
  evidence loop, not a write path, and it moves on its own when the two real
  targets land; an agent that wants to summarize writes that prose into
  `document_description`. Per-mount split as usual: the library route
  registers NOTHING and says so in code. Also undeclared: `document_version`
  (a concurrency token, the `scratchpad` refusal), identity/ownership ids,
  `document_source` / `document_original_file_id` (provenance — where a
  document CAME from is a fact the import flow recorded), timestamps,
  permissions and `is_public`, and the document BODY, which Univer owns and
  which nothing lifts to the route — it is not even a declared READ value.
  Follow-on 2026-08-12: the header rename FIELD now carries
  `maxLength={DOCUMENT_NAME_MAX_LENGTH}` from that same module, so the bound is
  enforced on the human path too. **Worth generalizing when you put an
  `entity` target beside an existing input:** the handler and the manifest
  agreeing is only two of the three legs. Here the field accepted a pasted
  300-character title, Postgres rejected it against `varchar(255)`, and
  `commitRename` — which swallows failures on purpose, because a blur handler
  must not throw — reverted the title with nothing on screen to explain why.
  Bounding the target without bounding the control leaves the agent better
  validated than the user.)
  `matrx-user/files` (9 targets, handlers in
  `features/files/components/surfaces/useFilesSurfaceWriteHandlers.ts`,
  registered on PageShell — the ONE `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` mount for this
  surface, so NOT a per-mount posture job: `RowContextMenu` and
  `FileRightClickMenu` name the surface for the context-menu READ seam and
  mount no runtime. `active_file_name` / `active_folder_name` are
  `entity`+`ask` and go through `validateRenameInput` plus the `renameFile` /
  `updateFolder` thunks the Rename dialog dispatches — same validator, same
  sibling-collision check, so an agent inherits every guard the user gets; both
  REFUSE loudly when `tree_status !== "loaded"` or an upload is in flight,
  because that collision check is only as good as the rows in the store.
  `search_query`, `chip_filter`, `kind_filter`, `list_sort` (ONE object target
  `{sort_by, sort_direction}`, because a column-header click always sets both)
  and `view_mode` are `ui`+`auto` — each maps 1:1 onto a control in the header,
  none touches the server, one click undoes it. `active_folder_id` /
  `active_file_id` are `ui` but `ask`: activation MOVES the user and, for a
  real folder, fires `loadFolderContents` and rewrites the URL. Enum
  vocabularies validate against the RUNTIME lists the controls render from —
  `CHIP_FILTER_KEYS`, `KIND_FILTER_VALUES`, `VIEW_MODE_VALUES` and
  `COLUMN_SPECS.sortKey`, all newly exported for this — so `view_mode` refuses
  `"columns"`, a value in the `ViewMode` type with no renderer and no button.
  Deliberately NOT writable: `selected_file_ids` (the selection arms bulk
  DELETE and MOVE — the `extraction_selected_row_ids` call; staging a selection
  is loading a gun even when a human pulls the trigger),
  `active_file_visibility` / `active_folder_visibility` (flipping a file public
  PUBLISHES it — a capability change, the line `agent-builder` and
  `admin-tool-registry` drew), move / delete / upload (a rename is a label; a
  move changes where the user's data lives), `details_level` (no on-screen
  control at all, so an agent could reach a state the user cannot click back),
  `column_filters` / `visible_columns` (no enumerable vocabulary in scope), and
  every count / `visible_*` / upload projection, which are reads with no write
  path).
  `matrx-user/image-studio` (3 ask-policy DRAFT targets on the `/images/convert`
  batch converter, all on `ImageStudioShell`'s existing provider:
  `conversion_settings` (ONE partial-patch object over format / quality /
  background fill / fit / crop anchor — the `image-generate` composite case,
  with `studio_settings_summary` as its literal composite read twin),
  `selected_presets` (a full-list REPLACE of catalog ids — the
  `marketing-crawls` pattern-list case, because presets are the run's SCOPE and
  the thing a user most wants to approve on its own: "you're about to make 7
  favicons"), and `image_description` (the per-FILE authored metadata —
  `alt_text` / `caption` / `title` / `description` / `keywords` /
  `filename_base` — landing through the same `updateImageMetadata` the Metadata
  panel's own inputs call). **THE per-file addressing reference.** Studio
