  sources are browser-local `File` objects with no durable id until "Save to
  library" mints one, so `image_description` carries `file`, matched
  case-insensitively against the `name` / `filename_base` the surface already
  reports, resolved against LIVE state through a ref, throwing with the real
  filenames listed on a miss or a tie. Unlike the other two, this handler DOES
  read page state to decide WHERE a value lands, an agent legitimately calls it
  once per image in one turn, and a `filename_base` write renames the very
  string the next lookup matches on — so the ref is required by the seam's
  ordering, not defensive. Its validation lives in a pure module
  (`features/image-studio/lib/image-studio-write-targets.ts`) while the other
  two validate inline, because it is the only rule that must resolve a row
  before it can check anything. **The read twin came with it:** the target sat
  on the not-writable list until `source_files` was extended to carry the six
  authored fields, because a write nobody can verify from a read value is not
  worth declaring — the `quick-note-save` `all_folder_names` move, applied to
  rows instead of a vocabulary. Generate is deliberately undeclared for a
  reason specific to this page rather than the usual one: it spends no model
  money, but it RESETS `variants: {}` on every file, and the shell interposes a
  rename banner in front of the first click — an agent firing it would discard
  reviewed work and route around a gate built to stop unattended runs. Save to
  library, download, adding or removing source images (an agent cannot produce
  a `File`), and running Describe with AI (a commit — it spends a model call
  and uploads a preview) all stay human, as do the result fields.
  `dominant_colors` is the instructive NO: it sits inside the very metadata
  object the text fields do and is rejected BY NAME, because those hex codes
  are measured off the pixels by a model that can SEE the image — the one
  field in the group that is observed rather than authored).
  voice agent may reach, respectively), and
  `matrx-user/knowledge` (9 targets across ALL THREE of the surface's mounts —
  ONE `entity`/`ask` target, `extraction_dataset_name`, through the same
  `updateJob` service the inline rename commits through, plus 8 `mode:"ui"`
  view-state targets: `extraction_query` / `extraction_sort` on the review
  grid, `graph_search` / `graph_kind_filter` / `graph_layout` /
  `graph_detail_level` on the canvas, and `suggestions_filter` /
  `focused_suggestion_id` on the triage queue. Handlers live on each mount's
  own provider. **The `auto`-vs-`ask` line this surface draws is the reusable
  part:** `auto` iff the write is ephemeral client-only view state, instantly
  visible, undone by one control already on screen, and spends no server
  request — so `graph_detail_level` is `ask` (it re-REQUESTS the graph at up to
  a 1000-node budget) and `suggestions_filter` is `ask` (it re-queries AND
  resets pagination, throwing away where the user was in a triage pass).
  Accepting / rejecting / deferring / starring a suggestion is NOT writable at
  any policy — the queue exists so a machine proposes and a person decides, and
  an agent that could accept its own pipeline's output would close that loop on
  itself; extracted rows and cells are not writable either, being retrieved
  evidence).
  `matrx-user/marketing-findings` (2 ask-policy ENTITY targets on the finding
  DETAIL route — `finding_suppression`, one `{suppressed, reason?}` object
  through `suppressFinding` / `unsuppressFinding`, and
  `finding_lifecycle_status` (`acknowledged | open`) through
  `acknowledgeFinding` / `unacknowledgeFinding`; handlers in
  `features/marketing/components/analysis/FindingWriteTargets.tsx`. THE
  reference for a surface whose write vocabulary is decided by an EXISTING
  ownership line rather than by counting inputs: `finding-mutations.ts` already
  says the analyzer owns detection and the user owns judgement, and judgement is
  exactly two verbs — so those two are declared and the register's whole write
  surface is covered, while `resolved`/`reopened`, severity, category and the
  immutable `analysis_result` evidence stay unwritable because writing them
  would forge the analyzer's verdict. The manifest's intro already told agents
  to RECOMMEND a suppression and leave the decision to the user; `ask` is what
  that sentence looks like once the agent can propose it, so the intro now
  points at the targets. The list-route mount registers no handlers, and each
  handler re-checks the row the canonical write RETURNS, throwing when the
  value did not land rather than reporting a success the DB never took).
  `matrx-user/settings` (7 ask-policy ENTITY targets — `theme_mode`,
  `accent_theme`, `display_layout`, `text_generation_style`,
  `language_defaults`, `assistant_name`, `voice_persona` — handled in
  `features/settings/route-shell/SettingsTabContentImpl.tsx`. Every one
  dispatches `getSliceBinding(slice).write(key, value)`, the exact action
  `useSetting` fires for the user's own click, so there is no parallel write
  path and the sync policy picks a change up identically. THE reference for a
  DURABLE surface: settings have no draft/save step and the synced tiers
  follow the user to every device, so `ask` is used even on the cosmetic
  targets a `ui`-mode surface would give `auto` — an agent silently
  repainting someone's app and having it still repainted on their phone
  tomorrow is not a cheap, self-correcting change. Also the reference for
  READING PAST THE OPEN TAB: the tabs are lazy components with no registry of
  setting keys, so rather than emit per-tab fields the emitter reads these
  seven straight from Redux — they are readable and writable from ANY settings
  tab, and each has a 1:1 read twin. Enum vocabularies live in
  `features/settings/agent-writable-settings.ts`, which the tabs now RENDER
  their `<SettingsSelect>` options from and the manifest interpolates into its
  contract prose, so the enum the agent is told, the enum the handler checks,
  and the enum the UI shows are one list (it also collapsed three divergent
  hand-typed language lists into one). The exclusions are the point on this
  surface and are argued in the manifest docblock: account identity (the
  Profile tabs), security (passwords/MFA/sessions/keys), billing,
  organization membership and roles, capability governance (AI Models, Admin
  Server), the entire Privacy tab — `alwaysWatching` and auto knowledge-graph
  are background-capture escalations, not preferences — integrations and
  connected accounts, navigation (`active_tab_id` is where the user IS), and
  the experimental/transient shell toggles. Only the route mount registers
  handlers; the WindowPanel/drawer settings shell owns no surface state and
  registers nothing).
  `matrx-user/keyword-research` (3 ask-policy targets on the workbench —
  `research_input_keyword`, a DRAFT staging the primary keyword, registered by
  `KeywordResearchLauncher` itself because it owns that input and gated on a
  `writeTargetSurfaceName` prop only the workbench passes, so the floating
  `KeywordResearchWindow` above some other page can never answer for this
  surface; plus `library_search` and `cluster_scope` as `ui` targets on
  `KeywordResearchWorkbench`. Shared validation lives in the pure
  [`keyword-research-write.ts`](../marketing/seo/keyword-research/keyword-research-write.ts),
  which also owns `MAX_STAGED_KEYWORD_LENGTH` so the manifest description
  interpolates the bound it is actually enforced from. **THE paid-pipeline
  line:** `POST /seo/keywords/research` and the volume refresh are both paid
  provider calls, so NO target starts either — the agent stages the seed and
  the USER presses Research, the same place `image-generate` stopped at
  Generate and `scraper` at Scrape. `seo.keyword_market` volume / CPC /
  competition / trajectory is observed provider evidence with no write path at
  all, and archiving keywords stays a human click behind its confirm).
  `matrx-user/marketing-competitors` (3 ask-policy targets on the Competitor
  Opportunity Autopsy — `autopsy_run_plan`, ONE draft object bundling
  `competitor_domains` + `max_competitors` + `pages_per_competitor` because
  they are one decision submitted by one button and splitting them lets an
  agent name five rivals while the budget silently discards four; plus
  `competitor_tracking` and `opportunity_status` as entity targets through the
  canonical `seo.update_competitor_tracking` /
  `seo.update_competitor_opportunity_status` RPCs, so an agent's triage carries
  the same `human_ruling` provenance a row-action click does. Handlers in
  `features/marketing/competitors/CompetitorAutopsyWorkspace.tsx`. THE
  selection-scoped reference: both entity targets carry their row id IN the
  value and resolve the visible rows through a REF at apply time, because
  `applySurfaceWrite` resolves EVERY handler before the user confirms the first
  dialog — a render-closure row list lets a multi-target turn write onto rows
  the user can no longer see; an id that is not currently loaded refuses the
  WHOLE write and reports the loaded count. RUNNING the autopsy is never a
  target — it spends DataForSEO provider calls, crawls
  `max_competitors × pages_per_competitor` pages of someone else's site and runs
  an LLM strategist, and the run-history table on this very page carries
  `reported_cost` — and neither is `site_id` (it redirects where the paid run
  points) nor `force_refresh` (its only axis is spend). Vocabulary and
  validators live in `features/marketing/competitors/autopsy-controls.ts`,
  which the manifest INTERPOLATES, the handlers validate against, and the run
  form's two selects render from, so the enum the agent is told about, the one
  its value is checked against, and the one the UI renders cannot drift).
  `matrx-user/marketing-authority` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target,
  `authority_guidance`, replacing the whole "Optional priority" note in the
  Internal Authority Router's run form — the surface's only authored input, and
  a single-target adoption on the `matrx-admin/applications` precedent that a
  surface can be worth exactly one target. The bound `AUTHORITY_GUIDANCE_MAX_CHARS`
  is imported by the Textarea's `maxLength`, interpolated into the manifest
  description and enforced in the handler, so the limit the agent is told, the
  limit enforced, and the limit the control imposes cannot drift. Running the
  analysis is NOT a target — it joins backlinks, the crawl graph, Search
  Console and the content plan and then spends model budget on the strategist,
  so the human press stays the gate — and neither is the map/routes/evidence
  view toggle or any computed model value (`authority_summary`,
  `authority_pages`, `authority_candidates`, `authority_recommendations`),
  which are the RECORD of what the analysis found. The two triage actions
  (dismiss a route, add one to the link plan) are DEFERRED rather than
  rejected: both clear the judgment bar and both already have canonical server
  actions, but `seo.collection_run` RLS made them impossible to live-verify
  from the test account, and an unverified target is not shipped).
  `matrx-user/voice-pad` (2 ask-policy DRAFT targets over the pad's ONE
  authored field — `draft_text` replaces the whole working buffer,
  `append_draft_text` adds to the end without re-sending it; handlers on

  the textarea fires on every keystroke. THE calibration case for the
  ~2-YES-field floor: one editable buffer earns targets anyway because
  cleaning up dictation is the pad's whole purpose. `transcript_entries` and
  `live_transcript` are OBSERVED EVIDENCE and stay read-only permanently;
  both handlers refuse while `is_recording` or `is_transcribing` is true,
  reading that state at call time rather than from the render closure. Single
  mount by design — `VoicePadAdvanced` / `VoicePadEmbed` mount no runtime and
  offer an agent nothing).
  `matrx-user/sandboxes` (2 ask-policy DRAFT targets on the sandbox terminal —
  `command_input` stages one shell command into the terminal's input box and
  `working_directory` sets where it will run; handlers on the detail page's own
  provider, `app/(core)/sandbox/[id]/page.tsx`, driving the same
  `setCommandInput` / `setCwd` the user's typing drives. THE reference for the
  **compose/execute split**: the agent writes the command, the human reads it in
  the box and presses Enter, and that keystroke is the consent. There is
  deliberately NO run/submit target and no handler may reach `handleExec` — a
  sandbox shell is arbitrary code execution on a live machine, and the seam's
  one-line `ask` confirm is not an informed review of what a shell string will
  do. This is exactly where the `tasks` surface's `save_task` action target
  stops generalizing: `save_task` persists an already-previewed draft into a row
  the user owns and can edit again, while a command is neither
  previewed-by-its-value nor reversible. `working_directory` is honest only
  because the page now sends the displayed cwd on every exec, so the prompt is
  authoritative rather than a lagging mirror of the orchestrator's session cwd.
  **THE STAGED-VS-TYPED AFFORDANCE reference** (added 2026-08-12 reconciling a
  two-branch collision on this surface): the input carries a "staged by agent ·
  not run" marker and the prompt's cwd turns amber whenever a value arrived
  through a target, clearing on the user's first keystroke, on history recall,
  and on exec — so an agent-written command is never presented as one the user
  typed, and the marker cannot outlive the value it describes. That closed the
  last objection of a parallel assessment which had ruled this surface OUT; the
  other objection it raised was a real defect (the handler used to `focus()` the
  prompt after staging, putting a live shell one keystroke from executing) and
  is fixed. Post-fix the gate reads: ask confirm → Apply → the command sits
  visible, unfocused and MARKED → deliberate click → Enter, one act longer than
  the `matrx-admin/database` `sql_query` precedent it was measured against.
  Terminal output, command history and the executing flag are OUTPUT and status,
  never targets; Stop / Extend / Delete stay human. The list mount registers
  none — tier/template/TTL are machine-provisioning config, not authored
  content).
  offer an agent nothing), and
  `matrx-user/scraper` (5 targets — `scrape_command` (partial-patch
  `{mode?, url?, keyword?}`), `scrape_page_limit` / `scrape_result_limit`
  (the two budgets), and `selected_result_page` / `selected_search_hit`
  (`mode:"ui"` selection over things ALREADY fetched). Handlers are ONE
  factory, `features/scraper/agent-context/scraperWriteHandlers.ts`, shared
  by FIVE mounts: the floating `scraperWindow` workspace and, since
  2026-08-12, all four `/scraper/*` ROUTES via
  `agent-context/ScraperSurfaceMount.tsx`. THE per-mount-by-capability
  reference: a mount passes only the setters it owns and the factory returns
  a handler only for the targets those cover, so `listAgentWritableTargets`
  offers `/scraper/search-and-scrape` exactly `scrape_command` +
  `scrape_page_limit` + `selected_result_page`, and `/scraper/search`
  exactly the search-mode three — the routes cannot switch MODE at all,
  because there the mode IS the route. Running a scrape is never a target:
  it spends real time against someone else's server, and every scraped
  value stays read-only forever), and
  `matrx-user/marketing` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target, `site_editor_draft`,
  staging `{site, name?, description?}` into the site editor dialog on
  `/marketing/sites`; handler on `SitesPortfolio`'s own provider, staging
  through the same `setDraft` a keystroke fires, so the human still presses
  "Save site" and the existing version-guarded `updateSiteIdentity` runs.
  Validation is the pure, unit-tested `lib/site-write-targets.ts`, whose
  `SITE_NAME_MAX` / `SITE_DESCRIPTION_MAX` the manifest interpolates into the
  model-facing description so the advertised contract IS the enforced one.
  **THE per-mount-posture reference for a surface whose mounts DISAGREE**:
  five components mount this one surface and only ONE registers a handler.
  `MarketingConnectionsCatalog` owns provider credentials and Search Console /
  Bing property ids (identity, never writable), `WorkspaceCostWorkspace` is a
  spend report and `MarketingHub` is a navigation map — nothing to author. The
  interesting refusal is `BrandsPortfolio`: its editor writes
  `{name, industry, description}`, but `matrx-user/marketing-brand` ALREADY
  ships `brand_identity` over industry/description and declares the brand NAME
  human-owned, so a second target set here would be the two-paths-into-one-row
  defect, not coverage — **the brand half of this hub earns nothing, and
  shipping only the site half is the finding, not a shortfall.** The target
  RESOLVES the row from the loaded list (exact id / domain / name, then a
  unique partial) and refuses unknown or ambiguous selectors with the loaded
  rows listed; it OPENS the editor when none is open, because that dialog is
  modal and a user therefore cannot open it and THEN reach the composer — the
  `matrx-admin/feedback` reachability rule — while refusing outright when an
  editor is already open on a DIFFERENT site, since closing it would destroy
  unsaved work. The dialog also gained
  `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}`: a write confirm renders
  OUTSIDE it, and answering one would otherwise count as an outside
  interaction and discard the copy just staged — verified by a two-call turn
  where the editor survived the second confirm intact. Read twin
  `site_editor` (the STAGED name/description, empty when no editor is open) is
  new, and `visible_sites` now actually emits the `description` its manifest
  had always advertised. Deliberately NOT writable: `root_url` / `domain`
  (identity — the editor itself shows them read-only, since a change is a
  page-registry migration), `brand_id` and organization (ownership), logo /
  favicon / OG image URLs (the `matrx-user/html-page` line — an agent can only
  invent a plausible URL), `status` / `visibility` (pausing governs whether
  metered crawl and scan work runs, `error` is system-written, visibility is
  permissions), delete, and site CREATION — `/marketing/sites/new` mounts no
  runtime and its only non-identity field is a display name that already
  defaults to the domain), and
  `matrx-user/markdown-editor` (3 ask-policy targets on the floating Markdown
  Editor overlay's `MarkdownClassificationTester` — `markdown_content` /
  `append_markdown_content` drafts through the same `setMarkdown` the user's
  keystrokes call, plus `pipeline_processor` as a `ui` target validated
  against the live `PROCESSOR_REGISTRY`. THE reference for RETIRING an
  exclusion on evidence and for refusing one on a crash: `processor_id` was
  first excluded as "clobbered by the next effect pass" and ships now because
  a live run disproved that, while `view_id` stays out because selecting
  "Key Points" from the default state throws in `KeyPointsDisplay` and the
  overlay error boundary eats the whole window — a crash a plain user click
  reproduces, so it is the surface's to fix, not a target to declare), and
  `matrx-user/rag-library` (4 ask-policy targets serving ONE flow — find a
  document in a corpus nobody can eyeball, open it, and give it a findable
  name: `library_filters` and `catalog_filters` are composite `ui` objects
  (`{search_query, status_filter}` / `{search_query, entitled_only}`) landing
  through the same setters the search boxes and status buttons dispatch,
  `selected_document_id` opens a row and is validated against the LIVE visible
  list read from a ref at call time, and `selected_document_name` is the one
  ENTITY target — the document rename, registered from the descendant
  `LibraryDocDetailSheet` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` because that component
  owns the canonical write, which was extracted into a shared
  `renameDocumentTo` so the Rename dialog and the agent share ONE path.
  **THE reference for a surface two prior scouts ruled OUT being reopened on
  evidence** — the 2026-08-11 and 2026-08-12 scans both binned it as "pure
  list/search/filter view state", correctly for what they looked at, because
  the only authored field on the page is not page-level state at all: it lives
  in a child sheet's rename dialog. Ingest, re-embed, re-process, delete and
  catalog subscribe/unsubscribe stay human — an embedding run spends real
  money, so the user's own click is the spending gate — and every count is
  derived and unwritable by construction), and
  `matrx-user/education-planner` (4 ask-policy targets on a surface that had
  NO manifest at all — `route-to-surface.ts` already pointed `/education/planner`
  at it, so every agent run there resolved a surface that did not exist. Three
  ENTITY goal targets — `create_goal`, `update_goal`, `goal_status` — plus one
  DRAFT `plan_setup`. THE reference for **coordinates-with-the-value**: a goal
  edit carries `{goal_id, title?, target_date?, topic?}` because WHICH goal is
  selected decides where the value lands, and the read twin `study_goals`
  carries the ids the agent addresses them by. The canonical writes were inline
  in `StudyPlanner.tsx`'s editor dialog and were extracted into
  `features/education/study/planner/goalWrites.ts`, which THROWS instead of
  toasting — the dialog catches and toasts, the agent handler lets the throw
  reach the seam — and `GOAL_STATUSES` was promoted to a runtime constant in
  `study/types.ts` with `GoalStatus` derived FROM it, so the status validator
  cannot drift from the type. Goal handlers are registered on
  `PlannerWorkspace`'s own provider and the goal LIST was lifted there, so goals
  stay readable and writable from BOTH tabs; `plan_setup` is registered from
  `PlanGenerateForm` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, so it is offered only while
  that form is genuinely on screen and never resolves into a form nobody can
  see. `getScope` is SYNCHRONOUS over a module snapshot store
  (`planner/plannerSnapshot.ts`) that the plan view and the form publish into —
  the Surface Context window samples `getScope` every 400ms, so an emitter that
  fetched would hammer the DB on every open context window. Every mastery,
  struggle and progress number is DERIVED EVIDENCE and deliberately unwritable:
  an agent that could write a mastery percentage would forge the measurement the
  planner exists to produce. Deleting a goal stays human, so no delete target
  exists and `goalWrites.ts` exposes no delete helper for one to reach; marking
  a plan BLOCK done/skipped is the learner's own record of what they actually
  studied and it feeds mastery, so the agenda is read-only; and generating /
  re-planning / archiving spend an agent run and rewrite a schedule, so those
  buttons stay human-pressed), and
  `matrx-user/education-flashcard-editor` (3 ask-policy entity targets on a
  surface this campaign had to AUTHOR before it could write to it —
  `set_details` (`{name?, topic?, description?}` in ONE composite, with the
  matching `set_details` READ twin declared alongside the three scalar fields
  so `updatesValue` names a real 1:1 value), `card_content`
  (`{card_id, front?, back?}` — the coordinates-with-the-value rule: WHICH
  card decides where the text lands, so the id rides in the payload), and
  `add_cards` (`{cards:[{front, back?, card_kind?}]}`, the decomposition
  action). Handlers on the provider `EditSetView.tsx` now mounts, all three
  through the SAME `fcService.updateSet` / `updateCard` / `addCards` the
  learner's own typing autosaves through. **THE reference for "the route the
  prize is on had no surface at all".** The scouting brief pointed at
  `matrx-user/education-flashcards` and its missing `writeTargets`, but every
  value that manifest declares describes the LIBRARY LIST — `sets_loaded`,
  `visible_sets`, `folders`, `search_query` — and its `createScope` helper
  makes four of them REQUIRED. Mounting it on `/education/flashcards/[setId]/edit`
  would have forced the emitter to promise a folder taxonomy and a visibility
  facet that do not exist there, so the honest fix was a new surface
  (`surface-authoring` first, then this skill) plus a route regex above the
  `/education/flashcards` prefix, following the `analysis-studio` precedent
  for a dynamic segment mid-path. **A prefix mapping is not a surface**: the
  edit route already RESOLVED to the list surface, which is exactly what made
  the gap invisible to a manifest-shaped scan. Deleting a card or a set stays
  human, reordering is mechanical, share visibility is a permissions decision,
  and everything in `card_mastery` is derived review history — writing it
  would be fabricating the learner's past. `card_kind` is emitted NORMALIZED
  through the component's own `asCardKind`, because real rows carry legacy
  generator kinds (`definition`, `concept`, `image_prompt`) that this editor
  renders as basic front/back; the row value is preserved on save rather than
  clobbered. A per-card remount token is what makes `card_content` land
  VISIBLY: each card editor seeds `front`/`back` into local state once, so a
  saved row alone leaves the old text on screen — bumping only the written
  card's token re-seeds that editor and leaves the user's typing in every
  other card alone), and
  `matrx-user/education-practice-oral` (2 ask-policy targets on a surface that,
  like `education-planner`, had NO manifest at all — `route-to-surface.ts`
  already pointed `/education/practice-oral` at it, so every agent run there
  resolved a surface with no values, no targets and no intro. `practice_mode`
  (`ui`) picks which of the four practice modes is being set up; `practice_setup`
  (`draft`) is ONE composite `{focus?, difficulty?, count?, deck_id?,
  source_text?}`. **THE reference for "the source kind gates which fields are
  meaningful"**: deck grounding is rendered only in the two modes whose
  `offersDeckGrounding` is true, and the paste box is HIDDEN while a deck is
  chosen, so `setupWrites.ts` judges the RESULT of the patch — sending the deck
  and the text that replaces it in one call is fine, a state with both set is
  refused — rather than pretending the fields are independent. Composite over
  five micro-targets because the fields are one thought ("practice me on the
  French subjunctive at graduate level, 8 questions"); `practice_mode` stays
  separate only because it is the one write available from the home screen,
  before the setup form exists to be filled. `COUNT_OPTIONS` was local to
  `PracticeSetup.tsx` and `DIFFICULTY_OPTIONS`/`MODE_CONFIG` sat behind
  `lucide-react` icon imports, so the icon-free half was promoted to
  `spoken-practice/vocabulary.ts` (`constants.ts` re-exports it, so no importer
  changed): the manifest builds its enum PROSE from those constants and the
  handler validates against the same ones, so the picker the learner sees, the
  enum the agent is told about, and the check that runs cannot drift.
  **Handlers are registered on the PRE-SESSION mounts only, deliberately** —
  `SpokenPracticeSurface` returns NO handlers once `phase !== "idle"`, and
  `PracticeSetup` registers `practice_setup` only while it is itself mounted, so
  `listAgentWritableTargets()` offers an agent on the runner or the summary no
  write tool at all. STARTING the session is NOT writable: `start()` spends an
  agent run, opens the microphone, and meters `education.spoken_practice` — the
  same line the scraper drew, stage the command and never run it. Every score,
  verdict, transcript, pronunciation rollup and examiner's review is DERIVED
  EVIDENCE from grading a real recording, so none of it is writable; and
  `session_prompts` is the ANSWER KEY for questions not yet asked, so it is
  declared `autoContext: false` and the intro tells the agent never to leak it.
  `getScope` is SYNCHRONOUS over a module snapshot store
  (`spoken-practice/setupSnapshot.ts`) cleared on unmount, same reason as the
  planner's. **COLLISION, UNRESOLVED — do not treat this as the settled design.**
  A sibling agent's competing manifest for this same surface was synced to the
  shared DB mirror at 2026-08-13 00:12 UTC with a DIFFERENT vocabulary
  (`setup_open` / `practice_focus` / `practice_calibration` / `practice_phase`
  values, and four separate `draft` targets `practice_focus`,
  `practice_calibration`, `practice_deck`, `practice_material`) — those are four
  of the twelve stale `db_only` rows the drift entry below names. That design is
  NOT on main and NOT on any remote branch, and this one deliberately did NOT
  run a manifest sync, so the sibling's mirror rows are untouched. Two target
  sets covering the same fields is a defect, not a merge: a human picks ONE
  before either lands)
- **UI-state reads** — `runtime/surface-ui-state.ts`: the page PUBLISHES
  interaction-state projections (`publishSurfaceUiState`), rendered blocks
  read by key (`useCurrentSurfaceUiState` — stack-walking, same resolution as
  writes). Never a store, never domain data, never callbacks.
- **Apply policy** — per-target `applyPolicy: manual | ask | auto` is the
  SURFACE's default opinion (transient `ui` writes can be `auto`; persisted
  `entity` writes default `ask`; `manual` = user-gesture only). The BINDING is
  where the user controls it: `write_policies` on the `surface_binding`
  payload (v2), merged through the same weakest→strongest layers as value
  mappings, registered at launch (`registerSurfaceWritePolicies`), resolved at
  apply time. A binding may tighten but can NEVER open a `manual` target.
  **Editor UI (built 2026-07-29):** the ONE controlled editor is
  [`components/bind/WritePolicyEditor.tsx`](./components/bind/WritePolicyEditor.tsx)
  (Default/Manual/Ask/Auto per declared target; the `manual` floor is shown
  disabled, never silently ignored). Consumers: the per-agent 5-panel shell's
  **Agent access** column ([`admin/columns/AgentAccessColumn.tsx`](./admin/columns/AgentAccessColumn.tsx),
  converted from the old Playground stub — saves through
  `upsertAgentSurfaceBindingThunk`, creating a personal mapping-less binding
  when none exists), the batch editor's per-surface "Agent write access"
  section, and the shortcut editor (`ShortcutEditorNext`). **Round-trip law:**
  the `surface_binding` payload is replaced WHOLESALE on save, so every save
  path carries the binding's current `value_mappings` + the FULL policy map
  (BindingColumn, the batch editor, and the Agent access column all do).
  **Shortcut storage (no DDL):** the shortcut's overrides live INSIDE
  `agent.shortcut.value_mappings` under the reserved `__write_policies` key —
  serializer pair in `features/agents/redux/agent-shortcuts/converters.ts`
  (`parseShortcutWritePolicies` / `packShortcutValueMappings`;
  `parseValueMappings` strips the key). The launch thunk pushes the
  shortcut's `writePolicies` on the `"shortcut"` layer, so a policy-only
  shortcut still participates in the merge, and `matrx-user/pdf-extractor` (2 draft/`ask` targets on the Content-extractor template in the PDF studio's right inspector — `extraction_template_draft` (partial `{template_name, page_range, chunk_size, chunk_overlap}`) and `extraction_output_columns` (the whole Results table definition); handlers in `ChunkingConfigForm` through the same `patchDraft` the form's own inputs dispatch, mode-gated on the panel's EDITING state and refused while a run is in flight, with a base refusal layer on `PdfStudioShell`'s provider for when the panel is not mounted. Declared HERE and not on `matrx-user/extractor-chunker`, which the campaign named: that surface has no client mount at all — it is a server-side per-chunk vocabulary catalog, so targets on it would never be offered), and
  `matrx-user/cms` (the CMS HUB — ONE ask-policy `mode:"draft"` target,
  `new_site_draft`, a partial `{name?, slug?, domain?}` staged into the Create
  New Site dialog; handler on `app/(core)/cms/page.tsx`'s existing provider
  through the same `handleNameChange` / `setNewSlug` / `setNewDomain` the
  dialog's own inputs call, so `name` alone re-derives the slug exactly as
  typing in the Name field does. **THE reference for a target whose handler must
  OPEN the form it stages into.** The create dialog is a MODAL, so while it is
  open the header's "Agents for this page" button sits in an `aria-hidden`
  subtree and cannot be clicked — every agent-originated write therefore arrives
  with the dialog CLOSED, and a handler that refused on a closed form would
  refuse always. Opening it is how the `education-grade-work` rule ("never
  report applied for a value nobody can see") is honoured when refusing is off
  the table; the state is always mounted, only its rendering is gated, and
  Cancel/Escape undo it with nothing persisted. Distinct from the sibling
  `matrx-user/cms-site`, whose 4 targets are per-site settings. Not writable, on
  a LIST surface where almost everything is a record of sites that already
  exist: **creating** the site (`CmsSiteService.createSite` mints a real
  `client_sites` row and routes away — the `crm-create-party` / `image-generate`
  line, an agent may fill the form and the human presses Create Site),
  `is_active` (publishing), `agent_write_policy` (an agent editing the rule that
  governs agents is the campaign's clearest NO, in either direction), the data
  API key (a credential the manifest already refuses to EMIT), the identity of
  any existing site (renaming a slug moves its public URL — that is `cms-site`
  settings), `selected_site_id`/`selected_site` (a hover pointer), and
  `sites_load_error` (the page's own account of a failed fetch)), and
  `matrx-admin/scheduling` (2 ask-policy `mode:"ui"` targets on the CRON TESTER
  tab only — `cron_expression` and `cron_timezone`, handlers in
  `features/scheduling/lib/cron-tester-surface.ts`, registered from
  `cron-tester/page.tsx`, which also had to MOUNT the surface's FIRST
  `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` and write its first emitter; the manifest had
  declared 27 values at `readiness: "stub"` with no runtime at all, and is now
  `"partial"` because the other six tabs still mount nothing. **THE
  deliberately LOW-stakes adoption, and an honest floor case at exactly two
  fields.** The tab is a pure client-side validator — it schedules nothing,
  reads no DB, spends nothing, and has no Save bar — so `mode:"ui"` is the
  truthful mode; they are still `"ask"` rather than `"auto"` because the
  expression is what the admin is actively reasoning about and silently
  replacing what they typed would be surprising. What earns the targets is that
  translating "every 15 minutes between 9am and 5pm on weekdays" into
  `*/15 9-17 * * 1-5` is real derivation work, and the page then closes the
  evidence loop in view: cronstrue humanizes the expression and the next N fire
  times recompute. **Validation reuses the page's OWN parser**, `validateCron`
  from `lib/scheduler-client/next-due` — the exact function the error alert
  renders from — so the value a handler accepts and the value the page previews
  cannot drift, and the parser's own message (`Constraint error, got value 99
  expected range 0-23`) is what reaches the agent. `CRON_TESTER_TIMEZONES` moved
  out of the page into that module so the picker and the enum check read ONE
  list: a re-typed list could accept a zone the `<Select>` cannot display,
  leaving it blank over a value the agent believes it set. **NOT a competing set
  with `matrx-user/schedules`**, whose `schedule_draft_trigger` also carries a
  cron expression + tz — that stages the trigger of a REAL task through
  `ScheduleForm.tsx`, and this admin console does not render `ScheduleForm`
  anywhere; checked first, because two target sets over the same fields would be
  a defect rather than coverage. Declined, and each for a reason: enabling or
  disabling a task, re-cronning one, and the orphan-lease "mark failed" action
  change what the platform actually RUNS for real users, so they stay human;
  Runs / Overview / Scanner health / Orphan leases are reports and register
  nothing; Templates is a hardcoded in-file `SEEDS` array, not real page data;
  and the tester's own "Show next N" is a mechanical view knob nobody would ask
  an agent to flip. **Extended the same day**: the shell
  (`SchedulingAdminLayoutClient`) now mounts an OUTER provider and the other
  six tabs publish their slices through
  `features/scheduling/lib/admin-scheduling-scope.ts`, so the whole console
  emits rather than just this tab — the cron provider nests inside it and still
  wins here by depth), and
  `matrx-user/marketing-reputation` (ONE ask-policy entity target,
  `reputation_case_triage` — a composite `{case_id, status, note?}` recording
  the human lifecycle decision on one reputation case plus the rationale
  justifying it; handler on `ReputationWorkspace`'s own provider through the
  same `useUpdateReputationCase` mutation the card's Dismiss / Monitor /
  Accept / Start action / Complete buttons call. **THE reference for a
  one-target adoption that earns it by completing a declared agent ROLE.** The
  manifest already shipped a `reputation_adjudicator` role whose stated job is
  to "issue defensible handling decisions from closed evidence"; before this
  target that role could read every fact, inference and evidence ref on a case
  and had no way to record a single ruling. The surface has exactly ONE
  canonical write path — `seo.update_reputation_case` — so one target is the
  honest count, and it is a composite over three fields decided together
  rather than a bare field. `case_id` is REQUIRED and validated against the
  cases actually on the page, because this is a QUEUE with no selected case;
  `accepted` is refused unless the case is currently `open`, mirroring the
  Accept button the card only renders while open; `open` itself is never
  writable because no control sets a case back to it. Mode is `entity`, not
  the preferred `draft`, because there is NO staging buffer here — the buttons
  mutate directly and `reputation_cases` reports the stored row, so a staged
  status would be a value the read twin could never see. **Provenance guard
  worth copying:** the column is literally called `human_ruling` and this
  surface's doctrine keeps facts, model inferences and human rulings separate,
  so the handler stamps `decided_via: "agent_surface_write"` alongside the
  note — the human still rules by pressing Apply, but the record never claims
  a human wrote the prose. `seo.update_reputation_case` REPLACES
  `human_ruling` wholesale and recomputes `resolved_assessment` as
  `analysis || human_ruling`, so the handler always sends a complete ruling
  object and the description says the note replaces rather than appends. NOT
  writable: `reputation_brief` / `reputation_narratives` /
  `publication_opportunities` / `evidence_inventory` are model output admitted
  only after deterministic provenance gates — writing them forges evidence,
  and they are derived with no write path; starting an analysis run spends
  model budget so the human press stays the gate; `verdict` / `pitch_angle` /
  `priority` / scores are pipeline output with no UI affordance and no write
  path; the `?tab=` toggle is mechanical view state, and there is no per-case
  selection state to point at, so building one purely to reach a second target
  would have been inventing product to hit a count), and
  `matrx-user/marketing-backlinks` (ONE ask-policy entity target,
  `backlink_refresh_schedule` — a composite `{enabled?, cadence?,
  detail_limit?}` over the DataForSEO refresh schedule; handler on
  `BacklinksWorkspace`'s own provider through `persistSchedule`, the function
  the card's Save button now calls too. **THE reference for a write target
  found INSIDE a page classified as a read-only report:** four campaign scans
  binned this surface under "read-only reports and analysis OUTPUT" because
  they classified at the SURFACE level; the schedule card is a real editor
  behind a collapsed settings toggle, and control-level scanning is what
  found it. Every backlink row, referring domain, anchor, competitor, score,
  verdict and summary stays unwritable — writing them forges evidence, which
  the surface intro forbids outright — so one target is the honest count, and
  it is a composite because the three fields sit behind ONE Save and are one
  decision. Omitted keys keep the SAVED value, so "check weekly" need not
  restate a row limit. Mode is `entity` despite a staging buffer existing
  (`schedule` useState + `scheduleDirty` + a disabled Save), which is the
  interesting call: the read twin `refresh_schedule` is built from
  `savedSchedule` and its own description says it "always reflects the saved
  site row, not unsaved edits", so a `draft` write would be invisible to the
  twin AND to the user behind the collapsed card — an apply with no evidence
  loop. `cadence` and the 1-1000 `detail_limit` bound are INTERPOLATED into
  the model-facing description from `dataForSeoCadences` /
  `DATAFORSEO_DETAIL_LIMIT_MIN|MAX`, which the schedule input and
  `validateSiteIntegrations` now read too, so the contract cannot drift from
  the validator. Also ruled NO, recorded so nobody re-scouts it:
  `refresh_profile`, the toolbar's weekly/monthly/bootstrap selector — real
  view state, but a mechanical knob sitting beside the Refresh button the
  user must press anyway, where the agent's actual contribution is the
  recommendation it can already make in prose), and
  `matrx-admin/agent-apps` (6 ask-policy targets across TWO of the surface's
  SEVEN mounts, and THE per-mount-posture reference now that both halves have
  landed: 3 `mode:"draft"` targets on the CATEGORIES editor — `category_name`
  / `category_description` / `category_icon`, staged through the same
  `handleEditChange` the admin's typing calls, so the admin's own Save is what
  runs `updateAgentAppCategory` — plus 3 `mode:"entity"` targets on the
  edit/[id] APP shell: `app_metadata` (a composite `{name?, tagline?,
  description?}`, exactly the field set the "Edit name / tagline" modal
  edits), `app_category` and `app_tags`, handlers on that page's own provider.
  The two mounts landed independently a day apart and are both kept whole. The
  edit shell is `entity` because it has NO draft layer to stage into — it
  holds the loaded row plus a modal whose form state exists only while the
  modal is open — so each handler lands through the SAME
  `PATCH /api/agent-apps/[id]` the modal's Save calls; that PATCH is a raw
  `.update(body)` passthrough with **no server-side column allow-list**, so
  the handler's allow-list in the unit-tested
  `features/agent-apps/lib/admin-app-write-targets.ts` IS the guard, and the
  tests assert `slug` / `status` / `is_featured` / `is_verified` /
  `is_public` / rate limits / `component_code` / `user_id` are all rejected.
  `app_tags` has no validator of its own: it imports `validateAppTags` from
  `features/agent-apps/route/agent-app-entity-writes.ts`, the one the
  user-facing `matrx-user/agent-apps` surface already uses, so
  `app.definition.tags` keeps ONE contract across both surfaces.
  `app_category` is the one place the admin console is deliberately STRICTER
  than its user-facing twin — `validateAppCategory` there accepts any
  non-empty string because a user's own picker offers free-text categories,
  while here the value must match the live `platform.categories` vocabulary
  case-insensitively, since an agent inventing a category fragments the
  taxonomy this console governs. That vocabulary is loaded by the page and
  republished as the new `available_app_categories` read value, so the list an
  agent is told to pick from and the list the handler accepts cannot drift.
  The other FIVE mounts register nothing, by decision: dashboard, apps list,
  executions/errors and analytics are read-only reports whose only mutable
  state is filters and sort, and rate-limits is enforcement. Verified live from
  both directions — three targets applied and persisted on the edit shell, and
  the apps-LIST mount offered the agent no `apply_surface_write` tool at all),
  and
  `matrx-admin/mcp-servers` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target, `new_server_draft`,
  staging `{name?, vendor?, category?, description?}` into the Add-MCP-server
  wizard — handlers + the surface's FIRST `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` in
  `features/tool-registry/mcp-admin/components/McpServersAdminPage.tsx`. **THE
  citation for "the scouted target did not exist and the surface was adopted
  anyway."** This console was chipped for description / category / tags on the
  selected server, the shape its sibling `/administration/agents/mcp-tools`
  shipped — and the detail pane has no editor at all: `ServerDetail` renders
  `server.description` as a `<p>`, the Metadata tab is a read-only `<pre>` of
  `serverMeta(server)`, and `mcpAdmin.service.ts` has no `updateServer`, its
  one row-level mutator `setServerStatus` being exported and called by
  nothing. There is no canonical write path to an existing `tool.mcp_server`
  row in this feature, so that target would have had to invent one. What the
  console DOES author is a NEW server, in `AddMcpServerDialog` — four YES
  fields consumed by one `provisionMcpServer` call, which is the `cms`
  `new_site_draft` case line for line, including opening the modal so the
  staged draft is visible. **The refactor that made it possible is the
  reusable part:** the four authored fields were lifted OUT of the wizard into
  the page, because the wizard is unmounted while closed and a target that can
  only be written while its own editor is open is a target no agent can reach
  — the wizard's inputs now call the page's `patchNewServerDraft`, so a staged
  value and a typed one are indistinguishable and the draft survives Escape
  and reopen. Slug, endpoint URL, transport, auth strategy, docs/website URLs,
  the official badge and provisioning itself stay human — and note the
  deliberate divergence from `cms`, which DOES stage a new site's slug: the
  campaign brief ruled the slug out here because it becomes the `mcp.<slug>`
  executor name agent definitions reference, and that ruling is honoured
  rather than argued with. Nothing on an EXISTING server is writable, matching
  the manifest's standing rule that a refresh, a connection test or a config
  save is the admin's own button click).

## Surface client tools — page actions offered to bound agents (v1, 2026-07-29)

The ACTION tier of the 360 loop: a surface declares client-side TOOLS an agent on that surface can call to do live things on the page. Same registration the platform's internal tool families use (war-room / scribe: inline specs + `tool_delegated` routing) — never a parallel system.

- **Declare** — `SurfaceManifest.clientTools` (`SurfaceClientTool` in [types.ts](./types.ts)): `name` (lower_snake, globally unique per conversation — prefix with the surface domain), `label`, `description` (model-facing prose), `inputSchema` (the generated `CustomToolInputSchema` wire shape), `mode?: ui | draft | entity` (same meanings as write targets). Mirrored to `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` by manifest sync (see "Mirror" below).
- **Register** — `useSurfaceClientTools(surfaceName, handlers)` in [runtime/SurfaceRuntimeContext.tsx](./runtime/SurfaceRuntimeContext.tsx) (exact ref-proxy/module-registry pattern of `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`; any depth; latest closure always wins). A handler `(input: unknown) => Promise<unknown> | unknown` runs the action and returns the tool output the agent receives.
- **Execute** — ONE seam: `executeSurfaceClientTool(name, input, opts?)` in [runtime/surface-client-tools.ts](./runtime/surface-client-tools.ts). Never throws (envelope `{ok} | {ok:false,error}`), deepest declaring mounted surface wins (same stack walk as `applySurfaceWrite`), LOUD on every contract break (toast + `captureError` source `surface-writeback`): tool undeclared, declared-but-unwired, handler threw. `listLiveSurfaceClientTools()` mirrors `listLiveWriteTargets` (declared+mounted+hasHandler).
- **Reach the agent (WIRED)** — `buildToolInjection` (`features/agents/redux/execution-system/utils/build-tool-injection.ts`) reads `listLiveSurfaceClientTools()` fresh every turn and emits each declared+mounted+handled tool as a `ToolSpecInline` (`kind:"inline"` — aidream's supported path for client-delegated tools with no server registry entry; the server schema-validates args and emits `tool_delegated`). No per-conversation arming needed; skipped under the disable-injection brake; declared-but-unwired tools are NOT offered (console.warn). Name collisions with already-present specs are skipped.
- **Resolve the call (WIRED)** — `surfaceDelegatedToolCall` routes any name declared by ANY registered manifest (`isDeclaredSurfaceClientToolName` — manifest-wide so an unmounted page still yields a precise error, not `unsupported_client_tool`) to `dispatchSurfaceClientTool` (`thunks/dispatch-surface-client-tool.thunk.ts`), which runs `executeSurfaceClientTool` and posts through the single `submitToolResult` funnel — the hard-suspended loop always resumes.
- **Policy** — the dispatcher is run-immediately (like scribe). A tool whose effect needs a human gate should route its handler through `applySurfaceWrite` (which carries `applyPolicy`/`write_policies`) or gate itself.
- **Mirror (DB)** — `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` is the action-tier twin of `ui_surface_write_target`: one row per declared tool (`surface_name`, `name`, `label`, `description`, `input_schema` jsonb, `mode`), written by `manifest-sync.service.ts` (and its SQL twin `scripts/emit-surface-sync-sql.ts`), so a SERVER-side agent can see a surface's action vocabulary — the inline specs are assembled on the client, so without this nothing server-side knew these tools existed. Code is truth; never hand-edit. A row means DECLARED, not live: the page must also be mounted with a registered handler before the tool is offered on a run. Columns mirror `SurfaceClientTool`, not `SurfaceWriteTarget` (no `value_type`/`updates_value`/`group_key`/`sort_order` — the type declares none); the FK cascades on UPDATE as well as DELETE (surfaces can be renamed) and `name` carries the same lower_snake CHECK `ui_surface_value` does.
- **Enforced (drift)** — `check:surface-drift` validates `clientTools`: `name` regex, unique per surface AND **globally across every manifest** (the tool namespace is per CONVERSATION, so a cross-surface collision is a real defect, not a style nit — it is silently skipped at injection time), `label`/`description` present, optional `mode` enum, and `inputSchema` conforming to the `{type:"object", properties, required}` wire shape (including `required` naming only declared properties). Every message names the fix.
- **Live adopters include:** `matrx-user/files` — `files_reveal_row`, `files_clear_column_filters` (initial adopter, 2026-08-13). Handlers live in `features/files/components/surfaces/useFilesSurfaceClientTools.ts` and are registered from `FileTable` / `FileGrid`, not from the page's provider — see the Change Log entry.
- **THE DUPLICATION RULE (learned on the first adopter — read this BEFORE declaring a tool).** A surface's `writeTargets` with `mode: "ui"` ARE page actions. Check whether a `ui` target already does the thing: if one does, a client tool for the same effect is a parallel path — the "two targets covering the same fields is a defect, not a merge" rule, applied across tiers. It is worse than redundant when the existing target is `applyPolicy: "ask"`: this dispatcher is run-immediately with NO dialog, so a duplicate tool SILENTLY STRIPS a human gate the manifest deliberately placed. A client tool earns its place only where the effect is an IMPERATIVE ONE-SHOT with no settable value behind it — something "set value X to Y" structurally cannot express (scroll/reveal/focus, clear-everything, replay, re-run).
- **Register where the STATE that proves the effect lives.** The failure mode of this tier is a tool returning `ok` while the page does not visibly move; the handler must be able to REFUSE when its effect would be invisible. That usually means registering from the component holding the rendered set, not from the provider mount — which also gives a legitimate per-mount split, since mounts that don't render that component leave the tool declared-but-unwired and `listLiveSurfaceClientTools()` correctly stops offering it.
- **Live adopters** — `matrx-user/content-plan` (2026-08-13, the reference implementation): `content_plan_focus_node`, `content_plan_switch_view`, `content_plan_expand_tree`. All `mode: "ui"`; handlers in `ContentPlanWorkbench` (focus/switch) and `PlanTree` (expand) via `useSurfaceClientTools`.
- **What that adopter established (copy this shape):**
  - **A tool is not a write target.** These three MOVE THE VIEW; the surface's data half stays on `writeTargets` and the child node surface. Where a tool and a target genuinely overlap (`content_plan_focus_node` vs the `select_node` target), they share ONE resolution function rather than becoming two ways to do the same thing.
  - **Selecting is not seeing.** `content_plan_focus_node` switches to a view that actually renders a node panel before selecting, because a selection the user cannot see is not a focus. A tool that returns `ok` without visibly moving the page is the failure mode of this tier.
  - **Accept the identifier the agent naturally has.** Focus takes a UUID *or* a route; an agent discussing `/services/roof-repair` should not have to go find a UUID first.
  - **Register the handler where the state lives.** The tree's collapse set is local to `PlanTree`, so `PlanTree` registers `content_plan_expand_tree` itself — `useSurfaceClientTools` works at any depth. It therefore registers only while the tree is mounted, which is the intended reading of declared-but-unwired: on the other views the tool is not offered at all (one `console.warn`), not offered-and-broken.
  - **Validate and THROW.** Every handler checks its input against the real vocabulary constant (`PLAN_VIEWS`, exported for exactly this) and throws; the seam turns the throw into a loud, safe envelope. Proven live: a bogus route came back as `client_tool_error: content_plan_focus_node: no node in this plan has route "…"`, which the agent quoted verbatim, and the very next tool call succeeded — the hard-suspended loop never wedged.
- **Not built yet (honest gaps):** server tools that write + refresh the page are a later tier; aidream's surface manifest endpoint does not yet feed `ui_surface_client_tool` to the model alongside `<surface_write_targets>`, so the mirror is written but not yet read.

## Header Surface Context windows

The universal Agents header dropdown exposes two ephemeral WindowPanels for the current route/runtime surface:

- **Surface Context** (`surfaceContextWindow`) is available to every user. It samples the matching `SurfaceRuntimeProvider.getScope()` while open, labels live/snapshot/unavailable state, and presents declared values beside their exact current values plus any undeclared runtime keys.
- **Surface Context Admin** (`surfaceContextInspector`) is gated by Redux `selectIsAdmin` both in the dropdown and inside the window. Its Live Values view uses the same runtime sampling; Manifest & Settings embeds the canonical admin editor and exposes code-owned provenance plus every DB-owned editable surface setting.

Both panels keep the standard thin WindowPanel chrome: friendly manifest-derived surface labels, icon-only header actions, and the canonical compact Copy for AI control. Runtime/manifest status and technical identifiers belong in panel content, footers, or the copied AI payload rather than the user-facing title bar.

Sampling is intentionally view-only and ephemeral: it reads the provider callback every 400 ms while a panel is open and never copies surface data into Redux or changes the agent execution scope.

## Architecture

- **Service**: [features/surfaces/services/surfaces.service.ts](./services/surfaces.service.ts)
  - `listSurfacesWithStats()` — single round-trip that fans out parallel reads
    (`ui.ui_surface` + `tool.surface_defaults` + `tool.bundle` +
    `agent.menu_surface` + `ui.ui_surface_value`) and joins counts in JS.
    Cheaper than sequential queries; cheaper than a server-side RPC for a
    table that admins read at most a few times per session.
  - `bulkSetSurfacesActive(names, active)` — one `UPDATE ... WHERE name IN (...)`.
  - `tierFor(sortOrder)` + `SURFACE_TIERS` — convention-driven grouping (see
    "Sort_order tiers" below).
- **Component**: [features/surfaces/components/SurfacesAdminPage.tsx](./components/SurfacesAdminPage.tsx)
  - Client tabs (matrx-admin / matrx-user / matrx-public / chrome-extension /
    All) drive the primary filter.
  - Status filter (active / inactive / all) and free-text search refine.
  - Body groups by tier (Pages, Specialized, Overlays, Editor variants, Debug).
  - Per-row controls: select checkbox, inline description edit (click to
    expand), tool-count badge, agent-count badge, active toggle, delete with
    FK-cascade-aware confirm.
  - Bulk control bar appears when ≥1 row is selected: activate / deactivate /
    clear selection.
  - "New surface" dialog with client picker, local-name input (validates
    `^[a-z0-9-/]+$` so multi-segment names like `debug/state-analyzer` are
    allowed), tier picker (auto-assigns sort_order = tier.min + 50), and
    description.

## Sort_order tiers (convention)

There's no `kind` or `category` column on `ui_surface`. Tiering is done via
`sort_order` ranges, and the UI groups + labels each band:

| Sort range | Tier label      | What goes here                                             |
| ---------- | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0–99       | Reserved        | (intentionally empty — reserved for future / pinned items) |
| 100–299    | Pages           | Top-level routes / primary destinations                    |
| 300–999    | Specialized     | Power-user surfaces, secondary tools                       |
| 1000–1999  | Overlays        | Modals, sheets, popout windows                             |
| 2000–8999  | Editor variants | Editor and authoring surfaces                              |
| 9000+      | Debug           | Admin-only debugging overlays                              |

The "New surface" dialog uses the tier picker to assign `sort_order = tier.min + 50`,
so new surfaces land in the middle of their band and don't collide with seeded rows.

## Seed (current production state)

After migration `seed_matrx_frontend_surfaces_expanded` (2026-05-05):

| Client             | Active | Total   |
| ------------------ | ------ | ------- |
| `matrx-user`       | 46     | 59      |
| `matrx-admin`      | 18     | 33      |
| `matrx-public`     | 5      | 8       |
| `chrome-extension` | 2      | 2       |
| **Total**          | **71** | **102** |

The inactive 31 are placeholders for emerging surfaces (beta UIs, debug
overlays not yet wired, etc.) — they're seeded so tools/agents can opt-in
to gating against them without admin work, but not activated by default.

## Conventions baked in

- `confirm()` from `@/components/dialogs/confirm/ConfirmDialogHost` for
  destructive actions.
- No barrel files; direct imports.
- No `useMemo` / `useCallback` / `React.memo` (per CLAUDE.md, React Compiler
  handles memoization).
- Bulk delete is intentionally NOT supported — single-row delete with
  cascade-warn is enough; bulk delete is a footgun for FK-target tables.
- Hard delete is allowed (no `is_active=false` "soft delete" alternative
  needed since `is_active` already exists). The confirm message warns when
  the surface has tool or agent references.

## v2.1 — full enrichment (2026-05-05 second pass)

After the user-requested "go all in" pass, the page picks up:

- **Per-surface detail drawer** ([SurfaceDetailDrawer.tsx](./components/SurfaceDetailDrawer.tsx)) opens on row name click or chevron. Shows:
  - Identity edit (active toggle, description edit, **rename**)
  - "Tools on this surface" — resolves `tool.surface_defaults` names against `tool.definition`, click-through to the tool admin
  - "Agents visible here" — joined `agx_agent_surface ⋈ agx_agent`
  - "Custom tool UI components" — `tl_ui` rows scoped to this surface
- **Rename support** so a rename propagates to every dependent row. The propagation is the DATABASE's, not this code's: `renameSurface()` issues a single `UPDATE` and every FK onto `ui.ui_surface(name)` carries its own rows across via `ON UPDATE CASCADE` — `tool.surface_defaults`, `tool.ui`, `agent.shortcut`, `ui.ui_surface_value`, `ui.ui_surface_agent_role`, `ui.ui_surface_config`, `ui.ui_surface_write_target`, `ui.ui_surface_client_tool`, and the `parent_surface_name` self-FK. **A child table that omits `ON UPDATE CASCADE` does not degrade gracefully — Postgres rejects the whole rename with `SQLSTATE 23503`, and no code path catches it** (this is exactly what `ui_surface_write_target` did to 108 of 185 surfaces until 2026-08-13; see the Change Log).
- **Bulk delete** in the bulk action bar. The confirm aggregates tool/agent reference counts across all selected rows and warns explicitly that DELETE is non-cascading (FK behavior on delete is `NO ACTION`).
- **"Add from candidates" dialog** ([SurfaceCandidatesDialog.tsx](./components/SurfaceCandidatesDialog.tsx)) — a curated catalog ([data/surface-candidates.ts](./data/surface-candidates.ts)) of ~70 plausible-but-unseeded surfaces (window-panel overlays, second-tier admin pages, agent embedding widgets, etc.) discovered via codebase inventory. Filter by client / kind / search, multi-select, optionally force-active on insert, bulk insert in a single round-trip.
- **"New client" dialog** inline (NewClientDialog at the bottom of the page file). Avoids round-tripping to `/admin/lookups` to add a `ui_client`.
- **Keyboard shortcuts**: `/` focuses the search input; `Esc` closes the open drawer / dialog / clears selection (in that priority order).
- The candidate-count badge on the "Candidates" button shows how many catalog rows aren't yet seeded — naturally trends to 0 over time.

## Readiness tracking + overlay surfaces (2026-07-24, Arman-ruled)

- **`SurfaceManifest.readiness`** (`verified | partial | stub`, REQUIRED) + `readinessNote` — the campaign tracker for "verified correct and complete". Mirrored to `ui_surface.readiness`; NULL = unregistered (no manifest). Code-owned; the admin board at `/administration/ui/surfaces` rolls up the four states. `verified` is earned via the full surface-authoring checklist, never aspirational.
- **Overlay surfaces**: every window-panel overlay gets its own surface, identified by `SurfaceManifest.overlayId` → `ui_surface.overlay_id` (the overlay twin of `url_pattern`). Emitters mount `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` inside the window component — nested providers out-depth the page, so the window's scope wins while open.
- 11 zero-reference stale `ui_surface` rows hard-deleted 2026-07-24 (ai-voice, browser-workbench, code-workspace, multi-file-smart-editor, news, notes-beta, prompt-apps, applets, voice-pad-advanced, voice-pad-ai, custom-apps — all had 0 values/roles/bindings/tool-defaults).

## Surface writeback — read/WRITE manifests (v1, 2026-07-29)

Surfaces are no longer read-only. A manifest may declare **`writeTargets`** (`SurfaceWriteTarget` in [types.ts](./types.ts)): named write paths INTO the page, beside the read-only `values`.

- **One apply seam:** every caller goes through `applySurfaceWrite(target, value, opts?)` in [runtime/surface-writeback.ts](./runtime/surface-writeback.ts) — never a bespoke callback. It never throws (envelope `{ok} | {ok:false,error}`), resolves **deepest-first** over the live `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` stack (`getSurfaceRuntimeStack`), validates the target against the resolved manifest, and is LOUD on every contract break (toast + `captureError` source `surface-writeback`): undeclared target, declared-but-unwired handler, handler throw.
- **Pages register handlers on their provider:** `SurfaceRuntimeProvider getWriteHandlers={() => ({ target: (value) => … })}` — fresh closure per call, no stale state. A handler validates its input and throws on bad values; the seam converts that to a safe error envelope.
- **Three modes**, declared per target: `draft` (stages into the page's editor — the USER reviews and saves; the preferred default), `entity` (persists through the page's canonical write path immediately), `ui` (ephemeral state — selection/focus/navigation; no success toast).
- **`updatesValue`** links a target to its read twin (the evidence loop: read the value, write the target).
- **Kind components reach it via the action registry:** `apply_surface_write` in `features/content-ir/react/actions/handlers/apply-surface-write.ts` — a rendered agent-result component's button calls `runAction("apply_surface_write", {target, value})`; the component never touches supabase/redux/page internals.
- **Validated AND mirrored:** `writeTargets` are validated by `check:surface-drift` and mirrored to `ui.ui_surface_write_target` by `manifest-sync.service.ts`, so server-side agents can see what a surface accepts. (This line previously said "NOT yet mirrored to the DB" — that was stale from before the mirror shipped.) The mirror is diffed both ways by the drift report — see **Mirror drift is reported for all four tables** below. First live consumer: the content-plan surface family (`content-plan-node` is the reference — field drafts + `save_node`).

### Mirror drift is reported for all four tables (2026-08-13)

`computeDriftReport` diffs the code registry against **every** mirror it writes
— `ui_surface_value`, `ui_surface_agent_role`, `ui_surface_write_target` and
`ui_surface_client_tool` — and `ManifestDriftDialog` renders all four the same
way. Each contributes the same triple the values half has always had:

| Mirror | manifest-only | db-only | field diffs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ui_surface_write_target` | `writeTargetManifestsMissingInDb` | `dbWriteTargetsNotInManifest` | `writeTargetDiffs` |
| `ui_surface_client_tool` | `clientToolManifestsMissingInDb` | `dbClientToolsNotInManifest` | `clientToolDiffs` |

**Why this needed to exist.** Both write halves were mirrored to the DB and
diffed by nothing, so a stale row was invisible: the report showed a green
"Everything is in sync" while `ui_surface_write_target` carried rows for
manifests that were never merged. The only lever an admin had was
`deleteStale: true` — a GLOBAL sweep that cannot distinguish "removed target"
from "another agent's unmerged work" — so the correct move was to run nothing,
and therefore to see nothing. **Reporting is the remedy; deleting is not.** The
`db_only` sections say so in the UI copy.

**Comparator discipline.** Each comparator normalizes the DB-side column
defaults before comparing, exactly as `diffSurfaceValue` does — write targets:
`apply_policy` → `manual`, `group_key` → `general`, `sort_order` → `1000`,
`updates_value` → `null`; client tools: `mode` → `ui`. Skipping this would
report every declared target as drifted the moment one optional is omitted.

**`input_schema` is compared as canonical JSON** — object keys sorted
recursively, **array order preserved**. Key order can never be evidence
(Postgres `jsonb` physically reorders keys on storage, so a raw
`JSON.stringify` would report every tool as drifted forever — the same trap
`value_groups` already documents). Array order is deliberately NOT sorted:
`enum` / `oneOf` / `anyOf` / `prefixItems` are order-bearing or order-visible to
the model, so sorting would trade a harmless false positive (a reordered
`required` shows as drift; a sync fixes it) for a false negative (a genuinely
changed enum ordering never surfaces). A silent drift report is the failure
mode worth avoiding.

**One shared count.** `countDriftIssues`
([utils/drift-report-count.ts](./utils/drift-report-count.ts)) sums the lengths
of every array-valued field of the report, so it is exhaustive by construction
and both `ManifestDriftDialog` and `ManifestSyncDialog`'s "Remaining drift"
line use it. The two used to hand-maintain separate sums and both had already
gone stale — the sync dialog was omitting label, value-group, write-target and
client-tool drift, under-reporting against the very report the operator had
just read.

`/api/admin/surfaces/drift-report` returns `computeDriftReport` verbatim, so it
needed no change — verified by reading the route, not assumed.

### The `manual`-holdout backlog (verified 2026-08-11)

A sweep for surfaces still worth making agent-writable went stale within a day
— `quick-tasks`, `scanner`, `data-tables`, `canvas`, `image-studio`, `lists`,
`working-document` and `scratchpad` all shipped write targets while it was
being written, so it is not reproduced here. Read the Change Log instead; it is
the live record, and several entries below record RULED-OUT surfaces too, which
is the part that saves the next agent a trip.

One concrete item does survive, and it now has a proven recipe:

- **`content-plan-node`'s `node_primary_keyword_id`** is the campaign's one
  deliberate `manual` holdout, for a documented reason — "the surface exposes
  no keyword options, so an agent has no legitimate way to produce a valid
  `seo.keyword` UUID; revisit if keyword options are declared."
  `content-plan-entities` has now done exactly that for its sibling field:
  declare the vocabulary as a read value, validate against it in the handler,
  and mark it `autoContext: true` so the agent can actually read it. The
  inventory already exists — `listSiteKeywordValues(siteId)` returns the site's
  `seo.site_keyword_value` rows and `listKeywordLabels(ids)` maps ids →
  phrase, both in `content-plan/data/service.ts`. Declaring a `keyword_options`
  value is the whole job; the target then moves to `ask`.

### The remainder, swept (2026-08-12) — and where the campaign goes next

A chip assigned `mermaid-editor`, then four named alternates, then "scout your
own". By the time that scout finished, **every surface it evaluated had either
shipped write targets already or failed the judgment bar**, so this records the
remainder instead of adding another adopter. Depth is honest: manifest value
sets for all of them, plus live-component checks on the ones named below. Treat
it as a starting point, not a per-surface deep audit — and `git fetch` first,
because this list went stale twice while it was being assembled.

The un-adopted manifests fall into five categories, none of which clears the bar:

- **Read-only reports over immutable evidence** — `marketing-analysis`,
  `marketing-audit`, `marketing-coverage`, `marketing-crawl`, `marketing-links`,
  `marketing-ranks-hub`, `marketing-site-pages`, `marketing-sitemaps`,
  `agent-run-history`, `ai-results`. Their value sets are rollups, queue counts,
  coverage matrices and table query state. Status readouts are not inputs.
- **Viewers and previews** — `canvas-viewer`, `file-preview`, `image-viewer`,
  `rag-viewer`, `context-preview`, `assistant-message`. A rendered record, not
  an editor; rewriting one fabricates history (the `matrx-user/chat` ruling).
- **Lists and hubs** — `podcast`, `education`, `dashboard`, `gallery`. List +
  filter + search state only, which is the `education-flashcards` /
  `crm-manager` precedent. `gallery`'s one text field is an Unsplash query.
- **Admin readouts and infrastructure** — `admin-cx-dashboard`,
  `admin-server-logs`, `admin-sandbox`, `admin-users`, `admin-mcp-servers`,
  `observational-memory`, `agent-gate`. Identity, credentials, connection
  configs and gate timings — all explicit NOs.
- **Opener-payload windows** — `image-uploader`, `share`. Their "editable"
  fields come from the caller's payload, not state the mount owns (the
  `quick-note-save` precedent), and `share` is permissions, which the bar names
  directly.

Three more, individually: `image-annotate` emits file ids, a `save_folder` and
an `is_saving` flag — markup is baked onto a flat PNG on save, so the only
writable thing there touches saved files; `education-flashcards`' home mount is
list + filters; and `_baseline` / `_conversation-document` are shared manifest
fragments, not surfaces.

Already carrying their own documented verdicts, and **not to be re-assigned**:
`messages` (evaluated twice; its one candidate duplicates `matrx-user/chat`'s
`input_draft`), `scopes`, `admin-official-components`.

**The next campaign is the ACTION tier, not more write targets.**
`clientTools` is built end to end — declare on the manifest, register with
`useSurfaceClientTools`, execute through `executeSurfaceClientTool`, injected per
turn by `buildToolInjection` and routed back by `surfaceDelegatedToolCall` — and
as of this sweep **zero manifests declare a single one**. Every seam a chip would
need already exists and is proven by the write-target campaign's twin plumbing;
what is missing is adopters. The honest gaps listed in the client-tools section
above (no DB mirror, no `check:surface-drift` validation of tool name
regex/uniqueness) are the second and third chips of that campaign, not blockers
on the first.

## Change Log

- **2026-08-13 — `matrx-user/agent-run-history` gets its first emitter and goes `stub` → `partial`: 3 declared values become 15, and the completeness pass is the substance.** The manifest had been authored from a code audit with `createAgentRunHistoryScope` at ZERO call sites — nothing emitted the scope. `AgentRunHistoryWindow` now mounts `<SurfaceRuntimeProvider>` (nested inside the overlay ON PURPOSE: providers resolve by depth, so while the window is open its scope out-depths the page behind it — verified live, the Agents popover names `matrx-user/agent-run-history` while sitting on top of `/agents/[id]/run`). Derivation lives in [`agent-run-history-scope.ts`](../window-panels/windows/agents/agent-run-history-scope.ts). **(1) The three declared values described the window's two useState fields and a count; they did not describe the thing the window is FOR — the roster.** `run_history` (every past run with status, timestamps, message count, agent version, model, origin), `run_versions` (the sidebar's grouping), `latest_run_at`, `history_load_status` / `_error` / `_fetched_at`, and the selected run's row + transcript now emit. **(2) Split state, one publisher.** The roster lives in `RunHistorySidebar`, the selection in the window. The sidebar publishes a FRAGMENT into a ref the window holds rather than mounting its own provider — a child provider would out-depth the parent's and replace its scope wholesale, silently dropping the selection. One slot per publisher, per the `matrx-admin/mcp-servers` precedent. **(3) "Not loaded" stays distinct from "loaded, and empty" — twice.** Roster keys are OMITTED (never `[]`) until the fetch resolves, so a `loading` list never reads as "this agent has never run"; `selected_run_transcript` is omitted while loading and `[]` only when the roster row's server-side `message_count` agrees the run has none. Both facts were already in hand, so neither costs a fetch — `getScope` is polled every 400ms and stays fully synchronous over live store state. **(4) NO WRITE TARGETS, argued rather than skipped.** Run history is a RECORD; derived evidence is read-only by nature. The row menu's rename / favorite / archive were considered and DECLINED in the manifest header: they are the conversation-row action registry's canonical operations, identical in five lists, and re-exposing them here would advertise a per-surface contract over a write path this surface does not own. The agent definition and raw tool-call payloads are documented exclusions too. **(5) `partial`, not `verified`** — the emitter is verified live (Surface Context: Live, 14/20 supplied, contract honored, no "Undeclared (runtime only)"; the 6 absent are the 5 unemitted baselines and `history_load_error`, which is correctly absent when nothing failed), but no agent has been bound to this surface, so the non-matching-name and Matrx-vs-matrix checks have not run and the DB manifest sync has not been applied for the new values.

- **2026-08-13 — The `clientTools` tier gains an initial adopter: `matrx-user/files` declares two tools — and the assignment's four "obvious" candidates were REFUSED as duplicates, which is the reusable finding.** `files_reveal_row` and `files_clear_column_filters`, declared in [`files.manifest.ts`](./manifests/files.manifest.ts), handlers in [`useFilesSurfaceClientTools.ts`](../files/components/surfaces/useFilesSurfaceClientTools.ts). **(1) The chip proposed navigate-to-folder, set-kind-filter, set-view-mode and select-a-file. All four already existed** as `mode: "ui"` write targets (`active_folder_id`, `kind_filter`, `view_mode`, `active_file_id`), driving PageShell's own activation callbacks. Declaring tools for them would be a second name on the same dispatch — and for the two NAVIGATION targets it would have been actively harmful: both are deliberately `applyPolicy: "ask"` because activation moves the user, fires `loadFolderContents` and rewrites the URL, while this dispatcher is run-immediately with NO dialog. A `files_navigate_to_folder` tool would have silently stripped a gate the manifest argues for at length. For the `auto` targets it would have added literally nothing, since the agent already applies those with no dialog. **The general rule now sits in the client-tools section above: a `ui` write target IS a page action; a client tool earns its place only where the effect is an imperative one-shot with no settable value behind it.** **(2) The two that survive are the two the write tier structurally cannot reach.** `files_reveal_row` — `active_file_id` opens the PREVIEW PANE and nothing else (`handleSelectFile` dispatches `setActiveFileId` alone), so a file deep in a paginated list can be "active" while its row is never scrolled to and, past the infinite-window cap, never even mounted. Revealing is a different mechanism entirely — `setFocusedId` → `ensureIndexVisible` grows the window → the row's `scrollIntoView` fires — there is no "focused row" value to write (focus is an event, not a setting), and it works on FOLDER rows, which `active_file_id` cannot touch. `files_clear_column_filters` fills a gap the write tier refused ON PURPOSE: exclusion #4 declines a `column_filters` target because the per-column vocabulary is not enumerable, but that objection is about SETTING and evaporates for CLEARING, which takes no arguments at all. **(3) Registered from `FileTable`/`FileGrid`, not from the provider — because the failure mode of this tier is returning `ok` while the page does not move.** Only those two components hold the `buildRows` output (the set actually rendered after section, search, chip, kind and column filtering), so only they can REFUSE a reveal whose effect would be invisible; PageShell's `searchScopedFiles` is `buildRows`' INPUT, not its result. They are mutually exclusive (`viewMode === "grid"`), so exactly one registers at a time. **(4) The sharp line held:** nothing here creates, moves, renames, deletes, uploads or downloads a file, nothing spends money, and checkbox selection stays out for the reason exclusion #1 gives (it arms `BulkActionsBar`) — `files_reveal_row` is the deliberate non-arming alternative, so pointing at a row can never pre-build a bulk mistake.
