Rows are addressed by durable UUIDs only, per the surface's FILE DOCTRINE. **(5) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/files/all`, and "the page actually moved" was MEASURED, not assumed.** Asked to point at `google-logo.png` (row 53 of 56, below the fold), the agent called `files_reveal_row`; the scroll container went `scrollTop 0 → 2344`, rendered rows grew `56 → 84` (proving `ensureIndexVisible` fired), and the row's viewport top went `3287 → 943`, in view and ring-highlighted. **Sampling the DOM the instant the focus class appeared showed `inViewport: false` — the smooth scroll was still animating; a single-frame probe would have reported a false FAIL.** `files_clear_column_filters` cleared an armed `Name contains "sample"` filter with rows going `5 → 51` and the Filters bar disappearing. A forced `row_id: "not-a-real-id-12345"` returned the handler's throw verbatim as an `<error>` (`...not-a-real-id-12345 in the loaded listing. Pick an id from visible_files or visible_folders.`) and the loop resumed normally — a follow-up message answered fine. **(6) The declared-but-unwired contract was tested in-repo rather than argued.** `/files/requests` mounts PageShell but renders a placeholder instead of the table, so both tools are declared with no handler there: `build-tool-injection` `console.warn`ed each by name and the agent's own tool list on that route showed `apply_surface_write` but NEITHER client tool. That is the per-mount split working, and it is the same posture the mobile `MobileStack` and `PickerShell` get for free. **(7) Error Inspector on a CLEAN load: Red 0, Orange 0, and the 2 yellows are the AGENT's own `tool:sql` lookups** (tiered SILENT as "NORMAL agent operation"), zero `surface-writeback` captures; the forced-throw load is reported separately because a throw IS a capture by design. **(8) Honest friction worth knowing:** `visible_files` is `autoContext: false`, so the agent does not get row ids for free — in one run it tried SQL lookups (those are the two yellows) and in another it narrowed with `search_query` first. Both reached a correct id and a working reveal, but the id-acquisition hop is real, and a future `autoContext` or id-bearing composite would smooth it. **(9) TWO infrastructure siblings landed mid-flight, and the collision that mattered was the DOC one, not the manifest one.** No manifest declared `clientTools` at any point during this work (checked at start, before writing code, and immediately before commit — `main` moved three times), but the two entries directly below rewrote this file's entire client-tools section while the browser verification was running: the DB mirror `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` shipped, and `check:surface-drift` gained `clientTools` validation — so **this entry's original claim that "the author is still the check" was obsolete before it was written.** Both sides were reconciled BY HAND rather than taking either wholesale, which is what that section's "Mirror"/"Enforced" bullets and this adopter's bullets now sitting together means. Drift was re-run AFTER rebasing onto the new validator: both tools pass its name-regex, per-surface and GLOBAL uniqueness, `label`/`description`, `mode` and `inputSchema` checks (including `required` naming only declared properties), so the two declarations are machine-verified rather than eyeballed — `check:surface-drift` green and reporting **`145 surfaces, 3667 values, 361 write targets, 2 client tools declared`**, the first non-zero client-tool count the checker has ever printed. `type-check` clean. **The `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` mirror was deliberately NOT written**, following the convention every entry above uses and the explicit warning in the entry below: the sync's delete-stale sweep is GLOBAL and would wipe rows belonging to siblings' unmerged manifests, and hand-inserting rows would violate the mirror's own "code is truth, never hand-edit" rule. Until a sync runs after merge, the drift report will correctly show these two as `manifest_only` — which is what an unmerged declaration is SUPPOSED to look like, and is now visible precisely because the report shipped hours earlier. The tools work either way; the mirror is for server-side advertisement, and the inline specs are assembled on the client.
- **2026-08-13 — `clientTools` gains an initial adopter: `matrx-user/content-plan` ships three `ui` tools, and the tier's "no live adopter" gap is closed.** The ACTION half of the 360 loop was wired end to end since 2026-07-29 with zero surfaces declaring it; this is the reference implementation. Shipped: `content_plan_focus_node`, `content_plan_switch_view`, `content_plan_expand_tree`. **(1) The judgment bar for TOOLS is not the write-target bar, and the difference is the whole tier.** A tool DOES something on the page; it does not write data. Everything shipped here moves the view and nothing else — the surface's data half already lives on `writeTargets` and the child `content-plan-node` surface, and none of it was duplicated as a tool. Candidates deliberately rejected: anything that creates/deletes/reparents a node (data, and delete stays human), Generate plan and Deepen briefs (they spend an agent run — the dispatcher is run-immediately with NO ask dialog, so a tool that spends money must not exist at this tier), and the site picker (navigating away from the page). **(2) `select_node` already existed as a `ui` write target, and the fix for the overlap was NOT to skip the tool.** FEATURE.md's own worked example is `content_plan_focus_node`, and the tool is a strict superset: it resolves a **route** as well as a UUID, and it switches view. Both now call ONE `requirePlanNode` function in `ContentPlanWorkbench`, so there is still exactly one way to open a node and the target's error strings are unchanged. Two paths that select a node would have been the defect; a shared core is the answer. **(3) SELECTING IS NOT SEEING — this is the failure mode the tier is most exposed to.** A tool that returns `ok` without visibly moving the page passes every test you write against its return value. `selectedNodeId` is honoured only by the tree and map views: `table`'s detail panel is driven by MatrxDataTable's OWN row selection, and `setup`/`entities`/`ai-runs` render no node panel at all — so on four of six views the naive implementation sets state nothing displays. `content_plan_focus_node` switches to `tree` first unless the current view is already node-showing (`NODE_PANEL_VIEWS`). Assert on the DOM (`[role="treeitem"][aria-selected]`), never on the envelope. **(4) Register the handler where the STATE lives, and let declared-but-unwired mean something.** The tree's collapse set is local `useState` in `PlanTree`, so `PlanTree` registers `content_plan_expand_tree` itself via `useSurfaceClientTools` (any depth, latest closure wins) rather than lifting state into the workbench. Consequence, and it is the intended reading rather than a defect: `PlanTree` mounts only on the tree view, so on the other five views the tool is declared-but-unwired and `buildToolInjection` does not offer it — **verified from both ends**, one `console.warn` ("no registered handler — not offered to the agent this turn") and the agent's own unprompted reply on the map view: *"I don't have a tool for that"*, followed by an accurate enumeration of only the two wired tools plus `apply_surface_write`. **(5) Vocabulary constants, not re-typed literals.** `PLAN_VIEWS` is now exported from `usePlanWorkspaceParams` and the handler validates against it; the manifest's `enum` is typed `readonly PlanView[]` behind an `import type` (erased at build, so the manifest registry stays free of client-component imports) — a renamed view breaks the build in the manifest. **(6) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/marketing/content-plan/…?view=tree`, no mocks, asserting on the PAGE.** Focus by route → `aria-selected` landed on `/blog/article-1`; "collapse to just the pillars" → tree went **20 rows to 8**; "switch to the map view" → `?view=map` and the tree unmounted. A forced bad route returned the handler's throw as a safe envelope the agent quoted verbatim — `client_tool_error: content_plan_focus_node: no node in this plan has route "/definitely-not-a-real-route" — read the routes from plan_tree, they are database-derived.` — and **the very next call still worked** (`/services/service-2` focused), so the hard-suspended loop never wedged. Error Inspector: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures on a load with no forced failure; on the load that forced one, exactly the by-design pair (contract-break capture + user-facing toast). `check:surface-drift` green and `type-check` clean — post-rebase the drift run reports **145 surfaces, 3661 values, 360 write targets, 3 client tools**, the first non-zero client-tool count the checker has printed. **(7) THE INFRA TIER LANDED ON `main` MID-BUILD, and the predicted docs conflict was real.** `claude/client-tools-infra` merged as `e9dcca0e` while the live verification was still running. Nothing about the DESIGN collided — it touches no manifest — but `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` conflicted in two places and was reconciled **by hand, keeping both sides**: their Mirror + Enforced bullets and this adopter's entries, with the shared "Not built yet" bullet reduced to what is genuinely still missing. Taking either side wholesale would have silently reverted shipped docs — this is the campaign-wide hotspot the chips keep warning about, and it fired. Consequence for this entry: the global-uniqueness check the author had to do by grep is now **automated**, and `check:surface-drift` passes on all three tools post-rebase. **The DB mirror is deliberately NOT synced for this surface yet:** `manifest-sync` writes `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` via `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests`, but the infra entry directly below warns its `deleteStale` sweep is GLOBAL and would wipe sibling branches' unmerged rows (it counted 364 write-target rows against main's 357). Left pending rather than risking another agent's data — the tools work either way, because the inline specs are assembled on the client.
- **2026-08-13 — `matrx-admin/mcp-servers` reaches `verified`: the five per-tab values the entry below listed as still missing now emit, three declared values were CORRECTED rather than faked, and a dead fetch was fixed. Purely additive on top of `new_server_draft`, which landed mid-task and is kept untouched.** **(1) Closing the stated gap.** The emitter that landed hours earlier covered list + search + selection + the new-server draft and named its own remainder honestly: `server_tools`, `server_configs`, `server_connected_user_count`, `selected_server_active_tab` and `latest_test_result` were fetched inside ToolsTab/ConfigsTab/ConnectionsTab with no path up to the provider. All five emit now. Each tab reports what it loaded UP into a ref the provider's `getScope` reads at Run — the `messages.manifest.ts` `onLoadedMessageCountChange` shape, for the same reason: lifting per-tab data into page state would remount the detail pane mid-fetch, and a ref sampled at Run is exactly as fresh as state at no re-render cost. **`null` ("that tab has not loaded") stays distinct from `[]` ("loaded, none exist")** — the builder OMITS the key rather than emitting an empty array, so an agent can tell "no tools" from "not looked". `ServerDetail` clears the ref on unmount, so a deselect cannot leave a stale server in the scope. The row→value derivation moved out of the page into `features/tool-registry/mcp-admin/mcp-servers-scope.ts` (the `marketing-page-scope.ts` pattern), preserving the sanitization posture the inline version established. `readiness` `partial` → `verified`; **DB mirror sync deliberately left for merge — see (7).** **(2) THREE values could not be emitted honestly and were corrected, not faked** — the `current_conversation_message_count` precedent applied three more times. `selected_server_active_tab` had NO holder at all: `<Tabs>` was uncontrolled (`defaultValue="tools"`), so the page genuinely did not know its own answer; it is controlled now. `selected_server` promised sync status only, while the header also renders a PERSISTED connection-test badge off `last_test_*` — without those five columns an agent reading an absent `latest_test_result` would conclude a server was never tested while the page was showing a result from a previous session; `website_url` came along, since the row carries it and the wizard collects it. `server_configs` gained `env_schema` / `notes` / `min_node_version`, which the Configs tab renders and the intro explicitly promises — the schema declares KEYS and their required/secret flags, **never env var values**, and a malformed hand-edited blob projects to an empty schema rather than to something the agent will reason about wrongly. **(3) A real bug fell out of wiring `server_tools`.** `ToolsTab` passed the SLUG to `listServerTools`, which filters `tool.definition.managed_by_server_id` — a uuid, and a leftover from the pre-2026 `${slug}:%` name-pattern signature. The tab could only ever error or return empty. A value that cannot load cannot be emitted honestly either. **(4) The verification lesson is the first attempt's own test design.** Run one instructed the agent to use no tools and concluded several values were ABSENT — but `selected_server` had been handed over as a DEFERRED object it was forbidden to fetch, and it correctly counted its 17 fields while declining to guess at them. **A "do not use any tools" instruction silently converts every deferred value into a false ABSENT; never verify an emitter that way.** Run two allowed the fetch: `mcp_search: post` (as typed), `mcp_server_count: 38` (matching the badge), `selected_server_slug: postgres`, `selected_server` fully resolved and matching the Metadata tab's raw JSON, `selected_server_active_tab: configs` (DOM agreed), `server_connected_user_count: 0`. The values still reported ABSENT were exactly the three declared `autoContext: false` — bindable-only by design, not a gap. Error Inspector on a clean load with all four tabs exercised, search typed and cleared, and selection changed: **0 captures of any kind**. **(5) Radix tabs do not respond to a bare `.click()`** — `TabsTrigger` activates on mousedown/focus, so a harness that clicks a tab and then asserts on the tab it *meant* to open will silently verify the wrong pane. Dispatch `mousedown` + `focus`, then assert `[role="tab"][data-state="active"]` before trusting anything downstream. **(6) The collision landed MID-FLIGHT and the landed design won, as it should.** STEP ZERO was clean — no branch, no `agent.review_queue` row, no `writeTargets` on `main`; a complete competing implementation appeared while phase 1 was being live-verified and merged before this branch pushed. No competing target was declared (two targets over the same four fields is a defect, not a merge); this branch reset onto the landed version and re-applied only the additive half. Worth recording that the remote `claude/*` set turned over COMPLETELY during one task — three branches at the start, three different ones at the end, all merged and deleted. **(7) The DB mirror was synced, reverted twice, and is deliberately left alone — which is the finding.** The value descriptions, intro and `readiness` were synced from this branch and verified by re-query; within minutes `readiness` was back to `stub`, and by the end of the task the whole row had reverted to its PRE-write-target state (intro back to 1037 chars, the corrected `selected_server` description gone). Nothing malicious — other agents run `sync-manifests`, and a sync writes whatever manifest the runner has, so a branch-authored mirror row survives only until the next sync from `main`. **Re-applying it was rejected on purpose:** advertising `verified` and a value contract that is not on `main` yet would make the mirror describe a manifest nobody can run. The mirror becomes correct when this branch merges and any sync runs; the client reads the manifest directly and is unaffected either way, which is why every live run above passed against a stale mirror. **The general rule: never claim a DB mirror is synced from a branch — it is only as current as the last sync from the last-merged manifest.**

- **2026-08-13 — Surface RENAME was broken for 108 of 185 surfaces; `ui_surface_write_target` was missing `ON UPDATE CASCADE`. Fixed at the DB, plus the confirmation dialog that was hiding the blast radius.** `renameSurface()` is a single `UPDATE` that relies **entirely** on DB-level cascade — it hand-updates nothing. Nine FKs point at `ui.ui_surface(name)`; eight cascaded on update, and `ui.ui_surface_write_target` (added 2026-07-29) was the lone holdout, declared `ON DELETE CASCADE` only. `ON UPDATE` therefore defaulted to `NO ACTION`, which does **not** orphan rows — it **rejects the parent UPDATE outright** with `SQLSTATE 23503`. Every surface carrying a write target simply could not be renamed: 108 of 185 surfaces, 368 rows. The DELETE dialog was deliberately left alone — both mirrors cascade on delete, so counting them there would wrongly imply the delete will fail. `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` was verified live and was **already correct**, exactly as the entry below intended. **The standing rule: any new child table of `ui_surface` MUST declare `ON UPDATE CASCADE`, or it silently breaks rename again — there is no code-side fallback to catch it.**

- **2026-08-13 — `matrx-user/education-practice-oral` authored AND made agent-writable (2 ask targets) — and it collided MID-FLIGHT with a sibling's competing design that exists only in the shared DB mirror.** A two-step job like `education-planner`: `route-to-surface.ts` mapped `/education/practice-oral` at this name but no manifest existed, so every agent run there resolved a surface with no values, no targets and no intro. **(1) Read half.** 23 values in four curated groups (`practice_mode` / `session_setup` / `practice_run` / `practice_results`), emitted from `SpokenPracticeSurface.tsx` — the one component that spans all four screens — with the setup form publishing its slice into `spoken-practice/setupSnapshot.ts` so `getScope` stays synchronous (the Surface Context window samples it every 400ms). `runner_phase` + `selected_mode` together decide which screen is on display, which is what makes a one-tree, four-screen surface legible. **(2) Write half: TWO targets, and the composite is the point.** `practice_setup` is one `{focus?, difficulty?, count?, deck_id?, source_text?}` patch because that is one thought; splitting it would have pretended the fields are independent when the SOURCE KIND genuinely gates them — deck grounding exists only in the two modes whose `offersDeckGrounding` is true, and the paste box is hidden while a deck is chosen. `setupWrites.ts` therefore validates the RESULT of the patch (deck + replacing text in one call is fine; both set is refused) and skips the deck rule entirely when the mode renders no picker, because a deck chosen under an earlier mode survives the switch in component state, is hidden, and is already ignored by `handleStart`. `practice_mode` stays a separate `ui` target only because it is the one write reachable from the home screen. **(3) Vocabulary promoted so the three copies cannot drift.** `COUNT_OPTIONS` was a local literal in `PracticeSetup.tsx`; `DIFFICULTY_OPTIONS` and `MODE_CONFIG` sat behind `lucide-react` imports that have no business in the registry's server-side graph. The icon-free half moved to `spoken-practice/vocabulary.ts`, `constants.ts` re-exports every name (zero importer changes), and the manifest builds its enum PROSE from those constants while the handler validates against the same ones. **(4) What is deliberately NOT writable, and why it is most of the surface.** Starting the session spends an agent run, opens the microphone and meters `education.spoken_practice` — stage the command, never run it. Every grade, verdict, transcript, pronunciation rollup and review is derived from grading a real recording; an agent writing its own score forges the measurement the feature exists to produce. `session_prompts` carries reference answers and rubrics for questions not yet asked, so it is `autoContext: false` and the intro forbids leaking it. Handlers are registered on the PRE-SESSION mounts only — `SpokenPracticeSurface` returns `{}` once `phase !== "idle"` — so `listAgentWritableTargets()` offers an agent on the runner or summary no tool at all, rather than a target for a form nobody can see. **(5) Verified with real agent runs, not a type-check.** Popover header confirmed as `matrx-user/education-practice-oral` (that is how you know the authoring step took). One message changed mode + four setup fields: both ask dialogs rendered the manifest description verbatim, Apply landed every value visibly (heading → Debate, resolution + pasted material + level + count in the real inputs) and nothing started. A second run declined everything — form unchanged, agent acknowledged gracefully, no error. An undeclared target (`session_scorecard`) was refused and the agent enumerated exactly the two real targets. A deliberately invalid `{count: 7, difficulty: "Expert"}` returned the handler's throw verbatim (`client_tool_error: practice_setup.difficulty must be exactly one of …`) with NOTHING staged — validate-then-apply. Error Inspector: **0 captures on the valid-path load**; the invalid load showed exactly 2 (contract break + its toast) BY DESIGN, which is the distinction several older entries here get wrong. `check:surface-drift` and `type-check` green. **(6) The collision, unresolved on purpose.** STEP ZERO was clean — no manifest on main, no `claude/surface-write-education-practice-oral` on the remote — and a complete competing design was synced to the shared DB at 00:12 UTC while this one was being verified: values `setup_open` / `practice_focus` / `practice_calibration` / `practice_phase`, and FOUR separate draft targets (`practice_focus`, `practice_calibration`, `practice_deck`, `practice_material`). Those are four of the twelve stale `db_only` rows the drift entry below counts. The doctrine's tiebreaker did not fire: nothing landed on main and no sibling branch exists, so there was nothing to yield to and nothing to force-push over. **No manifest sync was run from this branch**, so the sibling's mirror rows are untouched — reporting the divergence is the job, overwriting it is not. Two target sets covering the same fields is a defect, not a merge: a human picks ONE before either lands.
- **2026-08-13 — The drift report now covers BOTH write halves, and the stale-row count it exposes is larger than anyone had measured — and still growing.** `SurfaceDriftReport` diffed values and agent roles only, so `ui_surface_write_target` and `ui_surface_client_tool` were mirrors that nothing compared. **(1) What shipped.** `SurfaceWriteTargetDrift` / `SurfaceClientToolDrift` types mirroring the value/role pair, six new report arrays (the `manifest_only` / `db_only` / `diff` triple for each table), `computeDriftReport` populating them from two added reads, and six new `ManifestDriftDialog` sections following the existing ones. **(2) The comparators, and the one judgment call.** `diffSurfaceWriteTarget` normalizes the DB defaults `manifestWriteTargetRowFor` writes (`apply_policy`→`manual`, `group_key`→`general`, `sort_order`→`1000`, `updates_value`→`null`); `diffSurfaceClientTool` normalizes `mode`→`ui`. `input_schema` had no precedent in this service, so: **canonical JSON with recursively sorted object keys and PRESERVED array order.** Key order is never drift — `jsonb` reorders keys on storage, so a raw `JSON.stringify` would flag every tool forever (the trap `normalizeGroups` already documents for `value_groups`). Array order is deliberately not sorted: `enum`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/`prefixItems` are order-bearing, so sorting buys a false NEGATIVE on a real reordering in exchange for suppressing a harmless false positive on a reordered `required`. A drift report that stays silent about real divergence is the worse failure. **(3) The "Remaining drift" tally was fixed by deleting it.** Both dialogs hand-maintained their own sum over the same object and both had gone stale — `ManifestSyncDialog` omitted label, value-group AND the new categories. Replaced by ONE `countDriftIssues` helper that sums every array-valued field of the report, so it is exhaustive BY CONSTRUCTION and a future category is counted with no second edit. **(4) `/api/admin/surfaces/drift-report` needed no change — verified by reading the route** (it returns `computeDriftReport` verbatim), not assumed. **(5) Proven LIVE, because a passing type-check is not evidence for a DB feature.** Drift report loaded at `/administration/ui/surfaces` as admin. `ui.ui_surface_write_target` held **372 rows against main's 360 manifest-declared targets**, and the report resolved that exactly: 0 manifest-only, **12 stale `db_only` rows**, 1 diff. Named: `content-plan-node::node_primary_keyword_id`, four on `education-practice-oral`, two on `education-mind-maps`, `marketing-competitors::competitor_domains`, and four on `education-learn-authoring` **synced at 00:16 UTC — minutes before the report ran**. The prior entry measured 364-vs-357 and called it 7; it is 12 now and rising, because siblings keep syncing unmerged manifests. That growth IS the argument for the feature. **`deleteStale` was NOT run** — reporting is the job; those rows belong to other agents' in-flight work. **(6) The client-tool comparators were proven with a temporary fixture**, since the table is empty and zero manifests declare tools (correctly zero drift, which proves nothing). Three fixture `clientTools` on `mermaid-editor` + three DB rows exercised every branch: `manifest_only`, `db_only`, and a `diff` reporting `label` + `mode` + `inputSchema` together. The decisive case: `fixture_tool_match` was stored with its `input_schema` keys DELIBERATELY reordered, Postgres reordered them again on write (`{type,required,properties}` with `properties:{beta,alpha}` vs the code's `{type,properties:{alpha,beta},required}`), and it correctly reported NO drift — canonical JSON verified against a real jsonb round-trip, not a unit test's idea of one. A write-target `sortOrder` fixture proved `writeTargetDiffs` fires on a real change while the other 359 stayed silent, which is the default-normalization working. **Fixture code and all four DB rows reverted; `ui_surface_client_tool` is back to 0 rows.** **(7) Corrected stale docs:** the writeback section still claimed `writeTargets` were "NOT yet mirrored to the DB" long after the mirror shipped.

- **2026-08-13 — The two documented infrastructure gaps in the `clientTools` tier are closed: `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` (the DB mirror) and `check:surface-drift` validation. Infrastructure only — no manifest declares `clientTools`, deliberately.** **(1) Drift validation shipped FIRST, because it is pure client-side and unblocks adopters with no DB dependency.** `check:surface-drift` now validates `clientTools` the way it already validated values and write targets: `name` regex, uniqueness within a surface, `label`/`description` present, optional `mode` enum, and the `inputSchema` wire shape. **The one check that is NOT a copy of the write-target block is global uniqueness**, and it is the one most worth having: FEATURE.md's own contract says a tool name is global per CONVERSATION, and `buildToolInjection` SKIPS a spec whose name collides with one already present — so two surfaces declaring the same tool name is a defect that manifests as a tool silently missing from a run, with no error anywhere. It is tracked across every manifest in one pass. Safe to do because `clientTools` (like `writeTargets`) is NOT merged through surface inheritance — `resolveManifest` merges values/roles/namespaces/evidence and passes the rest through via `...m` — so a child never inherits a parent's tools and cannot false-positive. `inputSchema` validation goes past "is an object": `required` entries must name declared `properties`, and a non-empty `required` with no `properties` block is an error, because both ship a tool the server will reject for arguments the model was told to send. Every message names the fix rather than the rule. **(2) The mirror.** `ui.ui_surface_client_tool` follows `ui_surface_write_target`'s conventions (composite PK, FK to `ui_surface`, world-readable + super-admin-write RLS, `surface_name` index) but its COLUMNS mirror `SurfaceClientTool`, which is a different type: no `value_type`/`updates_value`/`group_key`/`sort_order` — the type declares none of them, and adding columns code can never populate would be inventing structure — plus `input_schema` jsonb, which write targets have no analog for. **Two deliberate departures from `ui_surface_write_target`, both toward the older `ui_surface_value` convention:** the FK is `ON UPDATE CASCADE` as well as `ON DELETE CASCADE` (surfaces CAN be renamed from the admin page and `ui_surface_value`/`ui_surface_agent_role` both cascade it — `ui_surface_write_target`'s missing `ON UPDATE` is an inconsistency, not a rule to copy — that call was right, and the inconsistency was fixed later the same day; see the entry above), and `name` carries the lower_snake CHECK its two older siblings carry. Wired into `manifest-sync.service.ts` (upsert + delete-stale + `clientToolUpserted`/`clientToolDeleted` in the result), `ManifestSyncDialog`, and `scripts/emit-surface-sync-sql.ts`. **(3) Verified live, both directions.** `check:surface-drift` green on a clean tree (144 surfaces, 3645 values, 357 write targets, 0 client tools) and failing loudly on a deliberately-broken fixture — 10 issues across duplicate-within-surface, bad regex, missing label, missing description, bad mode, `type:"array"`, a non-object property spec, a non-string `required` entry, a non-object `inputSchema`, and a cross-surface name collision, plus the two `required`-vs-`properties` branches exercised separately. `type-check` clean. The mirror was proven by the CANONICAL path, not a migration file: a temporary `lookups_focus_tab` declaration on `matrx-admin/lookups`, `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests` from the logged-in admin browser → `{clientToolsUpserted: 1}`, and the row read back out of the database with `input_schema` intact (`{type, properties:{tab:{type,description}}, required:["tab"]}`). Fixture and row both reverted; the tree ships zero declarations. **(4) `deleteStale` was deliberately NOT run, and that is a finding about the shared database.** The delete-stale sweep is global, and `ui.ui_surface_write_target` currently holds **364 rows against main's 357** — 7 rows synced from sibling branches whose manifests are not yet merged. Running it to exercise my own delete path would have silently deleted another agent's live-verified rows. The fixture row was removed with a targeted DELETE instead. **So: the client-tool upsert path is live-verified; the delete-stale path is a structural copy of the write-target path and is NOT live-exercised.** Anyone running a full `deleteStale` sync during this campaign should expect to wipe sibling rows — check the counts first. **(5) `pnpm db-types` cannot run in an agent shell** (no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`; it fails AFTER truncating `types/database.types.ts` via shell redirection, so `git checkout` the file if you try it). The `ui_surface_client_tool` block was hand-written into the generated file in the generator's exact format and position; the next real `db-types` run should reproduce it byte-identically. `scripts/schema-check/current-schema.json` was deliberately NOT refreshed — it is a whole-DB snapshot and refreshing it would have pulled in every unrelated change other agents made.
- **2026-08-13 — `matrx-admin/mcp-servers` is agent-writable (ONE draft target) + its first surface runtime. This REVERSES two prior sweep rulings, and the reversal is the part worth reading.** Twice in this log (the `matrx-admin/email` handoff and the `messages` sweep) this console was ruled a NO on the same premise: "its lone authored field is a new server's description, one target below the floor and credential-adjacent." **That premise is factually narrower than the page, and both rulings were made from the manifest docblock without opening the components** — the exact failure the `messages` entry itself names when it says scouting notes "made from manifest/route shape" are stale by construction. Two corrections. **(1) The authored field count is four, not one.** `AddMcpServerDialog`'s identity step authors `name`, `vendor`, `category` AND `description`, all consumed by ONE `provisionMcpServer` call — over the ~2-YES floor, and the composite case rather than four dialogs. **(2) "Credential-adjacent" describes the page, not those fields.** The credential- and connection-shaped state (endpoint URL, transport, auth strategy, `tool.mcp_config` rows and their env schemas) is real and is excluded to the last field; the four authored ones are naming and summarising work. Note also that the manifest carried NO "deliberately not declared" ruling — it said the opposite, that an agent here may "help the admin draft a new server's description" — so by this log's own rule that **a surface's own manifest is the authority on whether it has been ruled out**, this surface was never actually ruled out at the source. It was also re-assigned deliberately by a human. **The scouted target does not exist, and finding that out is most of the work.** The chip asked for description / category / tags on the SELECTED server, copying the sibling `/administration/agents/mcp-tools` shape. There is no such editor: `ServerDetail` renders `server.description` as a `<p>`, the Metadata tab is a read-only `<pre>` of `serverMeta(server)`, and `mcpAdmin.service.ts` exposes no `updateServer` at all — its one row-level mutator, `setServerStatus`, is exported and called by nothing in the repo. With no canonical write path to an existing `tool.mcp_server` row anywhere in the feature, that target would have had to invent one, which is the declared-but-unwired defect the skill forbids. **So the target is `new_server_draft`, the `cms` `new_site_draft` design applied unchanged:** partial keys, validate-EVERYTHING-then-apply, `mode: "draft"` / `applyPolicy: "ask"`, the modal opened so the staged draft is visible, and the human still presses Provision server. **The plumbing lesson generalises beyond this surface: a draft target whose state lives inside a modal is unreachable until that state is lifted out.** `AddMcpServerDialog` is unmounted while closed, so its `useState` did not exist at the moment an agent wrote to it — and because the wizard is a modal, the header's "Agents for this page" button sits in an `aria-hidden` subtree whenever it IS open, so every agent-originated write necessarily arrives with the dialog shut. The four authored fields therefore moved to `McpServersAdminPage` and the wizard's own inputs now call the page's `patchNewServerDraft`; slug, transport, auth, URLs and the official badge stayed local, because none of them are writable. `provisioning` was lifted the same way so the handler can refuse a write against a form whose insert is already in flight. **`category` is validated against `MCP_SERVER_CATEGORY_VALUES`, derived from the wizard's own `CATEGORY_OPTIONS`** — the vocabulary the Select renders and the vocabulary the handler accepts cannot drift, and the enum is spelled out in the target description so the agent is told the same list. **This surface's FIRST `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`**, feeding list, search, selection and the new draft; readiness `stub` → `partial`, with the note naming exactly what is still missing — `server_tools`, `server_configs`, `server_connected_user_count`, `selected_server_active_tab` and `latest_test_result` are fetched inside the child tab components and never travel up, and all five are `alwaysAvailable: false`, so they read as absent rather than wrong. Endpoint URLs and OAuth ids stay out of the scope, matching `serverMeta`'s sanitized posture. **Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs, no mocks.** One message asking to stage a Linear server produced ONE confirm dialog carrying the target description **verbatim**; Apply opened the wizard with all four fields filled (`Linear` / `Linear Orbit, Inc.` / `Productivity` / a real agent-facing paragraph), and the draft **survived Escape and reopen**, which is what "persists" means for a target whose whole point is that nothing is written until a human acts. **"Keep as is" declined cleanly** — no error, all four fields byte-identical, and the agent acknowledged and offered its rewrite as text to paste instead. **The undeclared ask was refused loudly and accurately:** told to run a connection test on Context7 and change its endpoint URL, the agent raised NO dialog and explained the boundary in the manifest's own terms — that tests and refreshes are the admin's button clicks, that its one target stages a NEW server and "can't edit an existing, already-provisioned server like Context7", and that the endpoint URL is not writable even in the draft. **The forced-invalid value returned the handler's throw word for word** — `client_tool_error: new_server_draft.category expects exactly one of: productivity | communication | … | other. Got "design-tools" — pick one of those, it is not mapped or corrected.` — and **nothing was staged, not even the valid `name` in the same object**: validate-then-apply proven, not claimed, since the wizard never opened. Error Inspector on the CLEAN load (valid apply + decline, no forced invalid): **"No errors captured", 0 distinct · 0 total**. The invalid-value run was deliberately a SEPARATE page load because a handler throw is a `surface-writeback` capture by design. `check:surface-drift` green (144 surfaces, 3646 values) and `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) sync still pending, per the convention every entry above follows — the client tool works either way, which is why every run passed. **What would reopen the bigger prize here:** the moment this console grows an EDITOR for a registered server — a description field and a `updateServer` service to save it — that is a straightforward second target on a canonical path, and the sibling `mcp-tools` console is the shape to copy. Until then, an existing server's description is genuinely read-only on this page, and the manifest intro now tells the agent to say so plainly and offer to draft the text for pasting instead of pretending it can save it. **CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — ZERO chips fired, and here is the scan rather than an assertion.** 143 non-shared manifests; **33 lack `writeTargets` on `main`, and 110 are already claimed on some `claude/*` branch** (measured by fetching every remote `claude/*` head and grepping its manifests, not by reading branch names — branch names do not say what a branch claims). Subtracting the claimed set leaves 33, and every one fails: the read-only reports and viewers (`ai-results`, `agent-run-history`, `assistant-message`, `context-preview`, `file-preview`, `image-viewer`, `gallery`, `rag-viewer`, `dashboard`, `admin-cx-dashboard`, `admin-server-logs`, and the eight `marketing-*` report surfaces), the browse/filter hubs (`education`, `education-flashcards`, `podcast`, `scopes`), the governance and identity consoles (`admin-users`, `admin-sandbox`, `share`, `agent-gate`), `admin-official-components` (its `componentList` is a STATIC in-repo registry with no write path at all), and `messages`, whose manifest carries a standing "do not re-assign" ruling. **Four were never judged before and were checked HERE against their components rather than their docblocks** — the mistake this entry is otherwise about — **and all four fail:** `image-annotate` looks like the jackpot (markup on an image, the `pdf-analysis` re-label shape) and is not, because marker.js 2 is imperative and class-based, so the arrows, boxes and callout TEXT live inside a `useRef<unknown>` MarkerArea instance, never in React state — `AnnotateModeShell`'s own state is `saving` and `aiBusy`, and a target there would have to drive a third-party canvas API rather than a canonical write path; `observational-memory` holds exactly `copied` and `selectedConversationId` (a read-only diagnostics inspector); `canvas-viewer` holds `tokenInput` / `activeToken`, a share-token paste box that is one field and an identifier an agent would be guessing at; `image-uploader` holds only its upload `result` and has no text concept at all, as its own docblock says. **The honest state is the one the 2026-08-12 sweeps reached, minus this surface:** what remains is reports, viewers, hubs, and identity/credential consoles, and reopening the board needs new editable surfaces to be built. **The one genuinely worthy follow-up this task found is not a write-target chip at all** — it is the feature request at the top of this entry: give this console an edit form for a registered server (description + category through a new `updateServer`), which would then earn its second target on a canonical path.

- **2026-08-12 — The write-target field is swept: a chip for `mermaid-editor` produced a rule-out, an alternate that collided, and the finding that the campaign's remaining surfaces are reports, viewers, lists and admin readouts. No new adopter; see "The remainder, swept" above.** **(1) The assigned surface was ruled out, and the rule-out was PARTLY wrong — which is the useful part.** `mermaid-editor` already had a bespoke agent-edit path (`useMermaidAgentEdit` + `AgentEditRail`: pick an agent, type an instruction, watch a proposed diagram render, Apply or Discard), so the chip's own gate asked whether a declared seam ADDS anything. The scout said no, on three legs: the workbench owns exactly ONE authored field (`source` — `title` is DERIVED via `extractMermaidTitle`, and mode/selection/theme are view toggles), that one target reproduces the rail's outcome with a WORSE review affordance (raw DSL in a confirm dialog versus a live-rendered proposed diagram), and the surface had **no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` anywhere**, so mounting one inside the global canvas side sheet — a SIBLING of page content in `AdaptiveLayout`, registering at depth 1 and winning `getSurfaceRuntime()`'s recency tiebreak because the canvas opens after the page — would hijack the header Agents popover away from whatever route the user was actually on. **Another agent shipped it hours later** with two `mode:"entity"` targets (`diagram_source`, `diagram_title`) and the provider mounted in `MermaidWorkbench` itself. So the ADDITIVE leg was right — a canonical, policy-gated path any header-popover agent can drive is worth having beside a one-shot rail that scrapes streamed text — and the "fewer than 2 targets" leg was wrong, because a title written INTO the source's frontmatter is a real second target. **The transferable lesson: "the field is derived" is not the same as "the field is unwritable"** — derived-on-read can still be authored-on-write through the source of truth. **(2) The alternate collided, and the collision is worth naming.** `agent-apps` was scouted as the jackpot — `app_name` / `app_tagline` / `app_description` (draft, the Identity tab's own field state) plus `app_category` / `app_tags` (entity, because those two pickers have NO draft layer and save on select). It was built, type-checked and drift-checked; then `git fetch` showed another agent had landed **the identical five targets with the identical modes and policies**, plus shared validators in `agent-app-entity-writes.ts` registered on the LAYOUT mount too, so category and tags stay writable from the other sub-routes. Nothing local was additive, so it was discarded rather than merged. Convergent design across independent scouts is a good signal the bar is legible — but **`git fetch origin main` immediately before writing code, not only before committing**, or you will build something that already exists. **(3) The sweep.** With the assignment ruled out and the alternate taken, the four named fallbacks were checked and all failed: `crm-manager` is `CrmListPage` in window chrome (server-side sort/filter/paging plus destructive row actions — the `education-flashcards` precedent), `marketing-audit` is a deterministic rollup dashboard, `marketing-discovery` is a human-verdict review inbox whose confirmed facts are human-owned by the `marketing-brand` doctrine, and `education-tutor`'s only settable fields are two teaching-style preference enums on a surface where the agent IS the tutor. Every remaining manifest was then categorised; that list is the section above. `podcast-run` is called out because it looks writable and is not — `episode_title` / `episode_description` are read-only in `MetadataHero`, and its companion content already has bespoke generation (`podcast_blog_writer` / `podcast_show_notes_generator`), the same "bespoke path already exists" shape as `war-room-thread`. **(4) The pointer forward.** `clientTools` — the ACTION tier of the 360 loop — is fully wired and has **zero adopters**. That, not more write targets, is where the next chips go.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/scopes` evaluated for write targets and DECLINED, for the second time and by an independently-scouted assignment. No targets added; the ruling is now recorded IN the manifest so a third assignment does not re-derive it.** **(1) The prior NO existed but was invisible.** A 2026-08-11 agent assigned this same surface concluded it had no editable state, moved to the skill editor instead, and recorded the call ONLY in its `agent.review_queue` row — where it explicitly asked Arman to sanity-check the judgment ("tell me if you disagree"). Nothing landed in the manifest, so this surface still read as an un-scouted gap and got assigned again. **That is the `messages` lesson repeating: a judgment that lives only in a queue row is not documentation.** The ruling now sits in `scopes.manifest.ts`'s docblock in the standard dated form, and the `readinessNote` says read-only BY DECISION, not by omission. The prior agent's request is answered: independently re-derived from the live components, and it stands. **(2) The surface genuinely has no editable state — swept, not assumed.** All three mounts: `ScopesHub.tsx` renders cells as truncated read-only `summarizeContextCell` text with a `title` tooltip and rows that `router.push`; `TemplatesGalleryPanel.tsx` and `ScopesSettingsPanel.tsx` contain **zero** `<input>`, `<textarea>`, `<select>`, Save, `useState` or `dispatch` between their 349 lines — one `refresh()` button each. The only stateful control on the whole surface is the hub's `query` filter. **(3) Both central claims of the scouting brief were factually wrong, and both corrections point the same way.** The brief named `ContextItemsHub.tsx:210` as a mount of this surface — it mounts `matrx-user/context-items`, which **already ships five write targets** via `ContextItemsWriteTargets.tsx`, so the context-ITEM editor half of the brief (`EditContextItemSheet` → `ContextItemSettingsForm`) is shipped work on a different surface rather than missing work on this one. The brief also named `scopesService.setContextValue` as the canonical write; that function is **never called from any of this surface's three mounts** (its only production callers are `kgSuggestionsService.ts:372` and the `setContextValueWindow` overlay), and the route that actually edits a scope's cells — `/organizations/[orgId]/scopes/[typeId]/[scopeId]` — **mounts no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` at all**. **Generalizing: a scouting brief's file:line citations are a hypothesis, not a finding.** Grepping the surface-name constant took one command and overturned the assignment's premise; doing it first would have saved the 2026-08-11 agent its scout too. **(4) Why declaring the write anyway would have been actively harmful, not merely unearned.** It is the transcripts `tags`/`folder` anti-pattern in its most dangerous form: a write the user cannot see staged or correct in place, landing in the one part of an agent's context users author BY HAND, surfaced on the hub only as a truncated summary cell among every org and scope type they own. A wrong value would be near-invisible and would silently reframe every downstream invocation — the `FEATURE.md` Liquid Facelift example is precisely this blast radius. The correct fix, if that write is ever wanted, is to mount a surface on the org-scoped detail route where the editor already lives; **the missing piece is an emitter, not a target**, which prior entries put outside this campaign. **(5) Sub-candidates rejected individually rather than waved off.** `search_query` is the closest and still fails: cosmetic (the emitted summaries are explicitly the FULL set, not the filtered one, so the agent gains nothing it does not already hold) and one target against the skill's ~2 floor. The heavy-hitter inbox on the hub DOES write via `create_scope` + `setContextValue`, but **the human accept/reject IS the review gate on machine-generated proposals** — handing it to an agent means one machine rubber-stamping another's guesses into hand-authored context; it is also hidden when the detector finds nothing and its rows are undeclared here. The `active_context` group is permanently ineligible rather than rejected: it mirrors `appContextSlice`, and a target that moved the user's active context is the #1 bug the scopes module exists to kill. **(6) Board re-swept with BOTH scan methods, because the documented fix for the mount-scan trap has an inverse nobody has recorded. ZERO chips fired.** 34 non-`_` manifests still lack `writeTargets`. The `matrx-user/marketing-competitors` entry above correctly says to match the QUOTED surface name (`"matrx-user/podcast"`) to kill substring collisions, and to exclude `route-to-surface.ts` and the manifest itself. **That fix introduces a false NEGATIVE, and this surface is the proof:** manifests that export a name CONSTANT are mounted by the constant, not the literal, so a quoted-name scan reports `matrx-user/scopes` as having ZERO mounts when it has three (`ScopesHub`, `TemplatesGalleryPanel`, `ScopesSettingsPanel`). Run BOTH — the quoted literal AND the exported `*_SURFACE_NAME` const — and union them, or the board looks smaller than it is in exactly the case where a real candidate would hide. With both run: the const scan surfaces `scopes`, `image-annotate`, `image-viewer`, `file-preview`, `context-preview`, `gallery`, `share`, `admin-cx-dashboard`; the literal scan surfaces the marketing report/table set (`analysis`, `audit`, `coverage`, `crawl`, `links`, `ranks-hub`, `site-pages`, `sitemaps`), plus `messages`, `podcast`, `dashboard`, `education`, `education-flashcards`, `assistant-message`, `rag-viewer`. **The union is the same set the entries below already ruled out, decision for decision** — reports, viewers, previews, dashboards and hubs, chat surfaces, `share` and `admin-users` (permissions and identity), with `podcast` additionally `isEditable={false}` and its sibling `podcast-studio` already shipped. `observational-memory`, `admin-mcp-servers`, `admin-official-components`, `admin-sandbox`, `admin-server-logs` and `image-uploader` have no mount at all to register a handler on. **Firing chips at these would spend a session each to re-derive an existing NO** — and this task is itself the proof of that cost, having spent a session re-deriving one that was never written down. **(7) No live agent run, and nothing is claimed from one.** No code path changed, so there was nothing to verify: this task added a docblock, a `readinessNote` and two Change Log entries. `check:surface-drift` and `type-check` were run and are reported in the commit; the manifest's own `readiness` stays `"partial"` and its pre-existing live-verification gap is untouched and still recorded.
- **2026-08-12 — Scraper re-verified independently; two campaign-wide verification corrections.** Re-ran the full write matrix for `matrx-user/scraper` against latest `main` (and against the pre-merge two-target build) with a real Badass Agent run — ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose with enum and bounds interpolated from `scrape-command.ts`, Apply staging Deep + keyword + 6 pages and normalizing a scheme-less `example.com` to `https://example.com`, "Keep as is" declining cleanly, an undeclared results-tab/result-index write refused with the agent naming only the real targets, and a forced-invalid `scrape_page_limit: 99` returning `client_tool_error: scrape_page_limit expects an integer from 1 to 20.` with nothing staged. Two things this campaign has been recording wrong, both reproduced on two separate builds. **(1) "Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures" is a claim about the VALID path only.** A deliberately-invalid call — which this skill REQUIRES you to make — captures a `Surface writeback contract break` row plus its user-facing error toast, carrying `{targetName, surfaceName}`. That is the seam working as designed: a refused write is meant to be visible. Roughly 25 adopter entries now assert a flat "zero captures", which invites the next agent to see its own required invalid-value test appear in the inspector and go hunting for a regression that does not exist. State it as "zero from the valid path; the invalid test captures by design". **(2) Verifying a FLOATING-WINDOW surface has a trap worth an hour.** Where a surface's handlers live only on an overlay (here `scraperWindow`, opened via the sidebar **Windows** popover → **Dupes** → **Web Scraper**; `?panels=scraper` does NOT work because `UrlPanelManager` is mounted nowhere), the shell header's Agents popover still names the ROUTE surface — `matrx-user/dashboard` — never the window's. It reads as a failed mount and tempts you to go "fix" surface resolution. It is correct: `getSurfaceRuntime()` resolves ONE winner by React depth for the popover's label, but `listAgentWritableTargets()` walks the ENTIRE mounted stack, so the window's targets are offered to a run started from any route while it is open. Verify overlay surfaces by opening the window and running the agent from wherever you are; do not wait for the popover to rename itself. **On the collision protocol:** `main` lacking `writeTargets` is NOT proof a surface is unclaimed — this one had TWO complete competing implementations sitting on `claude/surface-write-*` branches while main was still clean. Run `git ls-remote origin 'claude/surface-write-*'` before writing anything.

- **2026-08-13 — `matrx-admin/sandbox` was DECLARING nine values and MOUNTING nothing; the emitter now exists, and write targets are ruled OUT with reasons.** Assigned from the observation that the write-target pool is essentially exhausted and the remaining leverage is surfaces that declare values but mount no provider — an agent on such a page sees *nothing at all*, which is strictly worse than a page with no manifest, because the chrome names a surface that turns out to be empty. **STEP ZERO confirmed the shape: `createAdminSandboxScope` had ZERO call sites outside its own manifest file** on latest `main`, no branch existed on this surface, and `readiness` was `stub` with a `readinessNote` explicitly deferring the provider. That grep — "does the scope helper have a call site?" — is the cheap, reliable unmounted check, and it is worth running before any read-side chip. **The failure mode this surface had is specifically nasty, and worth naming: the route IS mapped.** `utils/route-to-surface.ts` already pointed `/administration/compute/sandbox` at `matrx-admin/sandbox`, so the header Agents popover said "Sandbox Management", listed Badass Agent, and ran it — with an empty scope, because `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl`'s `hasLiveScope` needs a REGISTERED runtime, not a route match. A mapped route with no provider looks exactly like a working surface right up until the agent answers from nothing. **(1) THE MOUNT.** `AdminSandboxManagementPage` now wraps its tree in `<SurfaceRuntimeProvider>` with a **synchronous** `getScope` over live render state. That is a load-bearing constraint, not a style choice: `useLiveSurfaceScope` polls `getScope` **every 400ms** for as long as a Surface Context window is open, so an async emitter that re-read `/api/admin/sandbox` to "freshen" itself would hammer a fleet-wide admin endpoint continuously behind a debug panel that looks idle. The page's own 15s interval remains its only fetch and the emitter just reads what that already put in state, so the values are by construction what the admin is looking at. **(2) VALUES ARE PROJECTED, NOT SPREAD — the completeness audit found two things.** A `SandboxInstance` is structurally assignable to the manifest's declared entry type, so `instances` could have been passed straight through; it would have compiled and silently shipped `metadata`, `labels`, `organization_id`, `project_id` and the rest into agent context under a description promising eleven fields. Both list and detail values go through hand-written projections instead, so the declaration and the payload are the same object. The audit also found the `instance_detail` group promising "its full detail (container, TTL, paths, config)" while declaring only `expanded_sandbox_instance_id` — and since `sandbox_instances` is deliberately `autoContext: false` (fleet-wide per-user infrastructure data), an agent knew a row was open and nothing about it. Added **`expanded_sandbox_instance`**, the detail panel field for field. Two things were checked and deliberately NOT declared, recorded in the docblock so the next agent does not re-derive them: `viewerUserId` is ambient session identity that exists only to decide which rows link to `/sandbox/[id]` (no manifest in the registry declares a viewer id), and `isRefreshing` / `stoppingIds` / `deleting` / the SSH dialog toggles are transient in-flight chrome that would only flicker through the 400ms sampler. SSH credential material stays undeclared, as it already was. **(3) WRITE TARGETS: a documented NO, which is the honest answer here.** The page renders **zero `<input>` and zero `<textarea>`** — verified in the live DOM, not just by reading: all 8 inputs present are AppShell chrome checkboxes (`shell-sidebar-toggle`, `menu-group-admin`, …), none inside the page. Its complete interactive set is seven filter chips, a refresh button, a row-expand toggle, per-row Stop / Delete / SSH, two `CopyButtons` and two dialogs. Nothing on it is *authored*, so the bar's "which fields could an agent draft better?" has no candidates. Its only write paths — `handleStop`, `handleDelete`, `handleRequestSsh` — are excluded twice over: by the bar itself (destructive stays human; credentials are never agent-drivable) and by the settled `sandboxes.manifest.ts` ruling that starting/stopping/extending/destroying spends real compute and the human press is the gate. **That ruling was made for a user acting on their OWN sandbox; these same buttons act on OTHER USERS' running machines, fleet-wide, so every argument against it there argues harder here.** The two non-destructive candidates (`sandbox_status_filter`, `expanded_sandbox_instance_id`, both `ui`) are view toggles over data the agent already receives whole, would move the admin's view out from under them mid-read, and are two targets with nothing authored behind them — under the ~2 floor. The docblock names the one condition worth revisiting on: genuinely authored state (a labels editor, an editable TTL, a per-instance admin note). A new button that destroys something is explicitly not a reason to revisit. **(4) LIVE VERIFICATION, and it produced a finding worth propagating.** Real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/compute/sandbox`. The Agents popover names **Sandbox Management / `matrx-admin/sandbox`** (reachability confirmed — route surface and runtime surface are the same name here, so the runtime wins cleanly); the Surface Context window reports **`Live · 9/15 supplied · contract honored`** with `sandbox_list_error` correctly grey/absent and the five baselines unemitted. Asked to answer only from context, the agent returned the **non-default** filter (`running`, then `ready` on a second run — a value that cannot be guessed), the four counts matching the stat cards, `sandbox_list_loading: false`, and it correctly reported `sandbox_list_error` as absent rather than inventing one. **THE FINDING: an `object` value over ~200 chars does not arrive inline.** `expanded_sandbox_instance` stringifies to ~600 characters, past the platform's default `max_inline_chars` of 200, so it arrives as a **deferred** context item the agent retrieves on demand. The first run — instructed not to call tools — reported it as "deferred … not inlined" and listed every field as missing, which reads exactly like a broken emitter and is not one. Allowed to retrieve, the agent returned the whole object correctly: `sbx-7520dde5030e`, container `69ba973d…be57`, tier `hosted`, `ttl_seconds` 31536000, `/home/agent`, `/data/cold`, expiry `2027-08-04`, each labeled with its source field, plus "no admin actions (stop/delete/SSH) were taken — this is a read-only report", which is the manifest `intro`'s read-only instruction landing. **Anyone verifying a composite object value on any surface should expect this and test WITH retrieval allowed; the manifest description now says so explicitly.** A third run also exercised the emitter's own guard: with the filter narrowed to `ready` the expanded row left the loaded set, so `expanded_sandbox_instance_id` stayed set while the detail object was correctly withheld — documented behaviour, and the agent said "not available" instead of inventing it. `pnpm check:surface-drift` green (144 surfaces, 3646 values) and `pnpm type-check` clean. **(5) `readiness` moved `stub` → `partial`, deliberately NOT `verified`.** The emitter is wired and live-proven, but no agent role is declared for this surface (nothing here plugs an agent into a named slot) and no deliberately-non-matching-name binding test has been run against it. `verified` is earned, and those two are the remaining work. **One PRE-EXISTING defect found and left alone, reported rather than fixed:** `instances.map` returns a keyless `<>` fragment wrapping the row and its detail row, so React logs "Each child in a list should have a unique key prop … `TableBody` … `AdminSandboxManagementPage`" on every render of this page. It is on `main` untouched by this change; it is a one-line fix (`<Fragment key={instance.id}>`) for whoever next has reason to edit that block.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/education-planner` EXISTS now, and is agent-writable: a manifest authored from scratch for a surface the router had been pointing at for weeks, plus 4 ask-policy targets.** **(1) The route pointed at nothing.** `utils/route-to-surface.ts` mapped `/education/planner` → `matrx-user/education-planner`, and the `ui_surface` row was seeded — but **no manifest file existed**, so `getManifest()` returned null and every agent run on that page resolved a surface with no values, no targets and no intro. This was a TWO-STEP chip and it is worth naming the shape, because five sibling education routes (`/education/memory`, `/progress`, `/learn`, `/game`, `/mind-maps`, `/audio-study`) are in exactly the same state: **a route mapping is not evidence a surface exists.** Grep `manifests/` for the file before scouting one of these, not `route-to-surface.ts`. **(2) The judgment, and it is the skill's explicit YES.** Study goals are planning fields derivable from context: "I have a biology midterm in three weeks covering cell respiration" carries a title, a date and a topic, and `createGoal` is the decomposition action the bar blesses. Shipped: `create_goal`, `update_goal`, `goal_status` (ENTITY — a goal is a small, visible, one-click-reversible row, and staging it as a draft would mean inventing an editor state the page does not have) and `plan_setup` (DRAFT — it fills the generation form and stops). **(3) Coordinates travel WITH the value.** `update_goal` and `goal_status` take `{goal_id, …}` because which goal is selected decides where the value lands; the read twin `study_goals` carries the ids, and a wrong id throws an error that lists the CURRENTLY active goals — fetched at call time inside the throw, not from a render closure that may have gone stale while the confirm sat open. Proven live: on a fresh run the agent read a goal's id out of `study_goals` and addressed both targets by it, so the evidence loop closes. **(4) One canonical write path, and a vocabulary constant that cannot drift.** The goal writes were inline in `StudyPlanner.tsx`'s editor dialog; they were extracted into `planner/goalWrites.ts`, which THROWS instead of toasting — the dialog catches and toasts, the agent handler lets the throw reach the seam. `studyService` never throws (it returns `{data, error}`), so the throw is added deliberately at that one shared layer rather than duplicated. `GoalStatus` was a bare TS union with no runtime twin, so `GOAL_STATUSES` is now a `const` array in `study/types.ts` with the type DERIVED from it — the status validator checks the real vocabulary, never retyped literals. It is deliberately narrower than the DB check constraint, which also permits `paused`: nothing in the app authors or renders a paused goal, so it is not offered. **(5) WHERE a handler registers decides whether it is honest, and this surface needed both seams.** Both tabs are conditionally rendered, so neither child is always mounted. Goal handlers went on `PlannerWorkspace`'s own provider and the goal LIST was lifted there with them, so goals stay readable and writable from BOTH tabs; each write then switches to the Goals tab and reloads, so the learner SEES what landed instead of being told it did. `plan_setup` went the other way — registered from `PlanGenerateForm` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, because that component owns the form state and `listAgentWritableTargets()` filters on `hasHandler`, so the target is offered only while the form is genuinely on screen and never resolves into a form nobody can see. The Surface Context window reports it as "1 write target unwired" when an active plan is showing; that is the design, not a defect. **(6) THE EMITTER TRAP, and it would not have shown up in a single agent run.** `getScope` cannot fetch. `useLiveSurfaceScope` polls it **every 400ms** for as long as a Surface Context window is open, so an async emitter that queried goals and the plan would have hammered the database continuously behind a debug panel that looks idle. `getScope` here is synchronous over live render state plus a module snapshot store (`planner/plannerSnapshot.ts`) that the plan view and the form publish into and **clear on unmount**, so an unmounted tab never emits stale data as if it were live — which is exactly why every value fed from it is `alwaysAvailable: false`. **(7) Deliberately NOT writable.** Every mastery, struggle and progress number is DERIVED EVIDENCE — an agent that could write a mastery percentage would forge the measurement the planner exists to produce. Deleting a goal stays human, and `goalWrites.ts` exposes no delete helper for a future target to reach. Marking a plan BLOCK done/skipped is the learner's own record of what they actually studied and it feeds mastery, so the agenda is read-only: an agent ticking it off is a lie about their behaviour, not a convenience. Generating, re-planning and archiving spend an agent run and rewrite a schedule, so those buttons stay human-pressed. **(8) Live-verified with five real Badass Agent runs on `/education/planner`, no mocks.** The popover header read **"Study Planner / matrx-user/education-planner"** before anything was trusted, so the route-vs-runtime trap did not apply. Each ask dialog carried the manifest description VERBATIM. Apply on `create_goal` landed the goal in the list AND in `education.study_goal` with the exact title, `target_date 2026-09-18` and `metadata {topic: "Cell respiration", itemType: "fc_card"}`. A second, FRESH run (scope is sampled when the user presses ▶, so a follow-up in the same thread carries the first run's snapshot — start a new run to prove a read twin) applied `goal_status → achieved`, and the goal visibly left the active list. "Keep as is" on `update_goal` declined with **no error** and a graceful acknowledgement ("You declined this change in the approval prompt, so the date stays at 2026-09-18"). The undeclared ask — "set my average mastery to 95%" — raised NO dialog and staged nothing; the agent refused it in the manifest's own terms, that mastery is computed from real performance and has no write target. A forced-invalid `create_goal` returned the handler's throw word-for-word — `target_date must be a calendar date formatted YYYY-MM-DD (e.g. "2026-09-14"); received "next Tuesday".` — with **no goal created**, confirming validate-then-apply. `plan_setup` staged all five fields into the real form, **read back out of the DOM rather than taken from the agent's summary**: title `Spanish midterm`, date `2026-10-02`, "Minutes per day: 45", Sun+Sat highlighted, cap `40`, and nothing generated. **(9) Error Inspector: zero.** Checked on a load carrying only valid Applies and a decline — **"No errors captured · 0 distinct · 0 total occurrences"**. The forced-invalid run's capture was manufactured by the test and checked on its own load, per the skill. A decline produces no capture, as designed. **(10) Test data restored through the app's own UI**, which also re-proved the human delete path after the `StudyPlanner` refactor: both test goals were removed via the trash button and its confirm dialog, and `education.study_goal` is back to zero live rows — the state it was in before this task. One harness note: the goal cards were first targeted with a loose `div.rounded-xl.border` selector that matched an unrelated card elsewhere on the page; identify a row by its EXACT title and the trash button by its `text-destructive` class inside THAT card, or you will click something you did not mean to. `check:surface-drift` green (144 surfaces, 3626 values) and `type-check` clean — the 13 files with pre-existing errors are byte-identical on `origin/main` at `a0fcc8b1`, proven by detaching and re-running. **DB sync APPLIED** via the canonical `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests` from the logged-in admin browser: `ui_surface_value` 17 rows, `ui_surface_write_target` 4 rows, label/readiness/url_pattern/intro mirrored. `readiness` stays `"partial"` — no agent roles, no `data-surface-value` Locate anchors, and the absence/staleness banner prose is deliberately not emitted (it is derived from the heavy `collectPlanSummary` snapshot; the underlying facts — `plan_progress.overdue`, `last_study_session_at`, the agenda dates — are).

Here the domains and the budgets are NOT independent: `max_competitors` truncates the domain list, so naming five rivals against a stale budget of one silently discards four. The bundling rationale also rests on a mechanical claim that **checks out in code** — `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler map via `getWriteHandlers()` at `surface-writeback.ts:286`, raises the confirm at :299, and only calls the handler at :308, so interdependent fields split across targets genuinely can resolve against different snapshots, and the ref-at-apply-time pattern both entity targets use is the correct fix rather than defensive boilerplate. A divergent brief is not a mandate to override merged work; verify whose reasoning holds first. **(1) DUPLICATE REVIEW ROW — actionable, and nobody has recorded it.** `agent.review_queue` carries **two** rows for `/marketing/competitors` (`57356eeb` at 05:00:13Z, `459592e2` at 05:13:48Z), both `source: ai-matrx`, describing the same three targets. Neither is deleted here — removing another agent's row is not this run's call — but Arman will otherwise review the same surface twice, and **the queue is a collision signal the skill already tells you to dedup against, so a duplicate row degrades the very check that is supposed to prevent duplicate work.** Dedup by `url`, not by title: these two titles share no distinctive substring. **(2) THE MOUNT-SCAN TRAP, which inflates the candidate board.** The standard scouting trick — cross-reference manifests lacking `writeTargets` against those that MOUNT a provider — reports false positives two ways, and both bit this run before being caught. **Substring collision:** grepping the bare surface name matches longer names, so `matrx-user/podcast` "mounts" in `GeneratorForm.tsx` when that file actually mounts `matrx-user/podcast-studio` (already shipped) — the generator form's rich run-command state looks like a textbook candidate and is not one. Match the QUOTED name (`"matrx-user/podcast"`). **Route maps are not mounts:** `features/surfaces/utils/route-to-surface.ts` names nearly every surface and imports the provider, so it satisfies a naive two-grep filter for almost all of them; exclude it and the manifest file itself. But that manifest is scoped to the LIBRARY LIST in its own header, and all 14 of its surface-specific values are list vocabulary (`sets_loaded`, `visible_sets`, `folders`, `search_query`, `visibility_filter`), four of them `alwaysAvailable: true` and therefore REQUIRED by `createEducationFlashcardsScope`. Mounting it on the edit route would have forced the emitter to promise a folder taxonomy, a visibility facet and a set list that page does not have — the COMPLETENESS LAW inverted, a UI lying by construction — while the set and its cards showed up as "Undeclared (runtime only)". **The trap is that `/education/flashcards/[setId]/edit` ALREADY RESOLVED to the list surface** via the `/education/flashcards` prefix, so a manifest-shaped or route-shaped scan sees a mapped route with a surface and moves on. A prefix mapping is not a surface; it is only a name lookup. Fixed with a regex special-case above the prefix list, following the `analysis-studio` (`/files/f/[id]/studio`) precedent for a dynamic segment mid-path. **Judgment:** card front/back and the set header are YES; `deleteCard` and set deletion stay human, `reorderCards` is mechanical, share visibility is a permissions decision, and everything in `card_mastery` is derived FSRS review history — writing it would fabricate the learner's past, so it is declared `autoContext:false` read-only and named in the manifest as never a target. **Two things the live run taught that are worth copying.** (1) **A saved row is not a visible write.** Each `CardEditor` seeds `front`/`back` into local state ONCE, so the first `card_content` apply persisted correctly and left the old text on screen. Fixed with a per-card remount token (`key={id:rev}`) bumped only for the card actually written, re-seeding that one editor from the row the service returns while leaving the user's in-progress typing in every other card untouched — a blanket refetch-and-remount would have thrown away unsaved edits. (2) **`add_cards` does not append.** `fcService.addCards` defaults `startPosition` to 0, so new cards sort to the FRONT — and the "Add card" button does exactly the same thing, so the handler is at correct parity and the DESCRIPTION was what needed fixing. It now says new cards take the same placement the button produces rather than promising "the end". **Verified live end-to-end on `/education/flashcards/[setId]/edit`** with Badass Agent: the popover header resolved to "Flashcard set editor / matrx-user/education-flashcard-editor" (confirmed BEFORE trusting the run, since the panel prefers the route-derived surface); one message asking for three changes raised three ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose verbatim, and Apply landed all of them visibly — topic and description into the inputs, `Chloroplast` → `The chloroplast` in the card body, two new Calvin-cycle cards on the page; "Keep as is" on a later topic change declined with no error and the DB still read `Photosynthesis`; a deliberately invalid `card_id` came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`client_tool_error: card_content: no card with id "not-a-real-card-id" is in this set. Read the \`cards\` value for the ids actually on this page.`) with nothing written; and a clean load with only a valid apply produced **zero** `surface-writeback` captures ("No errors captured yet"). `check:surface-drift` green (144 surfaces, 3625 values); `type-check` shows the same 40 pre-existing errors as pristine `origin/main`, proven by detaching and re-running. All test data restored to its exact pre-run state, the text fields through the app's own UI so the human typing + autosave path was re-proven after the refactor. **A NEW surface needs its `ui.ui_surface` row seeded before any agent run will work, and the emitter script will not do it for you.** The first live run was rejected outright — `Request rejected: Surface 'matrx-user/education-flashcard-editor' is not registered in ui.ui_surface` — because `scripts/emit-surface-sync-sql.ts` only UPDATEs that row (its first statement is a guard SELECT telling you exactly this) and `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests` defaults `createMissingSurfaces:false`. Seed the row, then sync; 16 values and 3 write targets mirrored after that. Budget a step for it — this is invisible when you ADD targets to a surface that already exists, which is what every prior entry here did. **One honest non-finding, recorded so nobody re-reports it as a defect:** asked to delete a card and flip visibility to public, the agent raised NO ask dialog — the write layer correctly offers neither — but it then did both anyway through its own general-purpose database tools and reported success (the visibility change was real; the "deleted" card was soft-deleted with its membership edge removed). **Undeclared-target refusal is a property of `apply_surface_write`, not a sandbox around the agent**: an agent holding direct DB tools is not fenced by what a surface declines to declare, so "destructive stays human" is a statement about this seam only. Both were restored.

Temporarily forcing `runtimeIsOverlay = false` in `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` — i.e. the pre-fix rule — puts "Dashboard / matrx-user/dashboard" back in the popover for **all four**, then restoring it flips all four back. So every one of these was unreachable from the header control on any mapped route, and each surface's original verification must have run somewhere the route mapped to nothing. That matters most for `smart-code-editor`, whose only real caller (`MultiFileCodeEditor`, a chat code block) opens it over `/chat/[id]`. **CORRECTION 1 — the "overlay surfaces cannot READ" finding above is narrower than it reads, and does not apply to these.** All three surfaces whose values could be exercised read correctly over a mapped route: `smart-code-editor` quoted its exact Monaco buffer and `language`; `markdown-editor` read a **34,045-character** document accurately (counted its 150 `## Section` headings and returned a token planted near the end); `voice-pad` quoted its `content` verbatim. No run produced "is not attached to the current turn". The values arrive as INLINED context, and that path is fine — so the earlier finding should be read as being about the server `context` tool specifically, not about overlay surfaces in general, and "ask only for writes it can compose without reading the page" is over-cautious advice for these four. **CORRECTION 2 — the trap that actually looks like a read failure is TIMING, and it burned an hour here.** `SurfaceRuntimeValue.getScope` is documented as "called only when the user hits ▶ — never on mount", and it means it: the scope is snapshotted ONCE per run.
