Ask an agent to write, apply the write, then ask it in the SAME conversation what the field now contains, and it truthfully answers "empty" — it is reading the pre-write snapshot, not a broken bridge. Starting a fresh run re-reads correctly. Any future verification that reads *after* writing must start a new run, or it will file a platform bug that does not exist. **`transcript-studio` is reachable but its two targets are NOT usable today, for a reason outside this feature.** The studio cannot load any session: `features/transcript-studio/service/studioService.ts` and `transcriptBridge.ts` filter `studio_sessions` on `is_deleted` / `user_id`, while the live `transcripts.studio_sessions` has `deleted_at` / `created_by` and neither of those columns — PostgREST returns 42703 `column studio_sessions.is_deleted does not exist`, the list comes back empty and the window offers only "Start a new session". Both targets address a row by an id drawn from `concept_items` / `cleaned_segments`, so with no session there is nothing to address, and `concept_item` refuses correctly with `No session is open — select or start a transcription session before editing its concepts.` The contract is sound and guarded; it is gated on a schema-drift repair that belongs to the transcript-studio data layer, not here, so it was reported rather than rewritten. **Fully usable today: `markdown-editor` (3/3), `smart-code-editor` (3/3), `voice-pad` (2/2).** Each was exercised live: per-target ask dialogs carried the manifest description verbatim; Apply staged into the visible editor with the surface's own controls (the pad's record/copy buttons, the editor's Save) untouched; "Keep as is" returned a decline the agent acknowledged in prose with no error (`pipeline_processor` on markdown, `draft_text` on the pad); and undeclared requests were refused with the agent enumerating the real targets — `file_path`/`language` on the code editor, "delete the transcript entries and start recording" on the pad, and `replace_selection` correctly refused with nothing selected. **Two smaller findings, recorded not fixed.** (1) `SURFACE_ROUTE_MAPPINGS` maps `/markdown-editor`, `/smart-code-editor` and `/voice-pad`, but **none of those routes exists** — there is no page behind them, so these three surfaces are overlay-only in practice and the "it has both a route and an overlay id" framing does not hold. Harmless (a prefix that never matches never fires) but misleading to the next scout. (2) `UrlPanelManager` is not mounted anywhere in the app, so the `?panels=` deep links its registry advertises (`voice`, `markdown_editor`, …) do nothing; the working launcher is the Windows tools grid (`[aria-label="Manage floating windows"]`). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.

- **2026-08-12 — The header Agents panel could never resolve an OVERLAY surface on a mapped route. One-line fix; it unblocks write targets that were already shipped and already documented as live.** `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` picked its primary surface as "the registered runtime, but only if it matches the route" — so on any route with its own mapping, a floating window's surface LOST to the route even while the window was open on top of it. That contradicts the overlay doctrine three sections up ("nested providers out-depth the page, so the window's scope wins while open"), which was true of the runtime STACK (`getSurfaceRuntimeStack`, what `applySurfaceWrite` walks) but never of the panel's own `primaryName`. The panel now also accepts the deepest live runtime when its manifest declares an `overlayId`. **Why this is not cosmetic:** `matrx-user/quick-note-save` opens ONLY from a chat message's `Save as > Note`, so the Quick Save Note window is ALWAYS over `/chat/[id]`. Before the fix the popover read "Chat / matrx-user/chat", listed the CHAT's bound agents, and ran them against the CHAT's scope — so the `note_draft` target that landed 2026-08-10 (entry below) was unreachable from the one control the campaign's own verification protocol tells you to use. Verified live before and after on the real path (chat message → Options → Save as → Note): the popover flipped from "Chat / matrx-user/chat" to "Quick Note Save / matrx-user/quick-note-save", and only then did Badass Agent get offered `apply_surface_write` at all. With it, a single message asking for a title and a folder raised one ask dialog per target carrying the manifest prose verbatim, Apply staged into the visible form with Save unpressed, "Keep as is" declined with no error, and "switch this window to overwrite my existing note called Thread 1 and press save for me" produced no tool call at all — the agent answered that saving and choosing what gets overwritten are the user's. Zero `surface-writeback` captures. **Same class, worth re-checking: `matrx-user/task-create`** and every other `overlayId` manifest whose window opens over a mapped route — their live-verification runs may have been done on an unmapped route and would not have caught this.
  **Second finding, NOT fixed here, and it limits every overlay surface: an overlay's declared values never reach the agent as readable context.** With the fix above, the agent can WRITE every target but cannot READ them — it answers "the content field is empty" and the server `context` tool returns "'available_folders' is not attached to the current turn." The client side is provably correct: the scope logged at `handleRun` carried all of the window's live values including the real nine-folder list, and a control run on `matrx-user/markdown-studio` (a ROUTE surface, same agent, same panel, same launch path) read its `content` back verbatim — so the divergence is route-vs-runtime, not the manifest, and syncing the values to `ui.ui_surface_value` did not change it. Prime suspect: `buildToolInjection` computes the client `surface` the server resolves against as `perConversation?.surfaceOverride || detectActiveSurface()`, and `detectActiveSurface()` reads `window.location.pathname` — `matrx-user/chat` — with no way for the launch's own `surfaceName` to win. Until that is addressed, an overlay surface's agent should be handed the values it needs in the message, or asked only for writes it can compose without reading the page.
  **Process note for the campaign, because it cost this session a full duplicate implementation.** This chip was scouted as "quick-note-save has NO writeTargets"; that was true in the agent's own checkout and false on `main`. A `git clone --depth 1` through the session git proxy came down **9,453 commits behind `origin/main` on the same branch**, so the surface, its `note_draft` target and its whole judgment write-up were all invisible locally — and `git ls-remote --heads origin 'refs/heads/claude/*'` under-reports because merged campaign branches are deleted. STEP ZERO only works if the checkout is real: run `git fetch origin main && git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main` BEFORE scouting, and dedup against `agent.review_queue`, not branch names. (See the standing row "Campaign root cause: agents clone a STALE main".) The duplicate quick-note-save implementation from this session was discarded rather than pushed; only the two findings above are new.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/email` assigned a THIRD time. No targets added and none were needed; what this run contributes is a STALE-DOCS CORRECTION that was quietly understating a shipped surface, plus a third independent live verification on a newer `main`.** The chip arrived with a full scouting brief — mount the surface's first provider, weigh one composite `{subject?, body?}` against two micro-targets, refuse recipients / From / Send. **STEP ZERO found every one of those calls already made and merged** (`email_draft` landed 2026-08-10; the entries below had already closed the surfaces-side docs gap and re-verified it), so per the collision rule nothing was reimplemented. Worth one line on its own: the brief's predicted design and the shipped one agree decision for decision, which is evidence the judgment bar reproduces across agents who cannot see each other's work. **(1) THE ADDITIVE FIND — a `readinessNote` that was wrong about its own surface, in the SAFE direction, which is exactly why it survived two prior passes.** Both the manifest and the entry below state there is "no DB mirror of `writeTargets`", i.e. that aidream cannot advertise `email_draft` server-side. **That stopped being true on 2026-08-11.** `ui.ui_surface_write_target` holds the row, and it is not a partial or drifted sync: `label`, `value_type`, `mode`, `apply_policy`, `group_key`, `sort_order` and `updates_value` all match this file, and the mirrored `description` is **1547 characters against the manifest's 1547** — byte-exact, interpolated constants and all, checked by importing the manifest rather than by eye. The note is corrected; the two genuine gaps (`data-surface-value` anchors, the un-audited read values) are kept. **Generalize this, because the campaign has a reflex for the opposite case only:** the sandboxes and Agent Builder entries warn about docs that OVERCLAIM, but a note that UNDER-claims is just as costly and much quieter — it tells the next reader that a shipped capability is missing, and the natural response is to go build it again. **A "still pending" claim inherited from an earlier entry is a claim, not a fact; re-checking one against the DB costs a single query.** **(2) Third independent verification, on `main` at `5d9ee64f8`** — several merges newer than the run below (messages, sandboxes, the doc reconcile), so this is a regression check rather than a repetition. Four real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/users/email`, each on its own page load. One message asking for an announcement subject AND body raised **exactly ONE** ask dialog rendering the manifest description **verbatim** — the composite paying off, since a split target would have put two dialogs over one piece of copy — and Apply landed both fields with **real newlines preserved between paragraphs, no escaped `\n`, no stray quotes**: the parsed-argument trap stays closed because the target takes an OBJECT. The SAME message also asked to add `engineering@example.com` to the recipients; **no dialog was raised for it and the recipients box stayed empty**, the agent explaining in the manifest's own terms that the list has no write target. "Keep as is" left both fields untouched and drew a clean acknowledgement ("Staged nothing — you declined the draft change"), no error raised. A deliberately forced two-line subject returned the handler's throw verbatim — `client_tool_error: email_draft.subject must be a single line — it is an email header, and line breaks are not allowed in one.` — **with both fields still empty afterwards**, which is validate-then-apply holding: the confirm is answered BEFORE the handler runs, so Apply was pressed and the throw landed after it. **No email was sent at any point; Send was never pressed and no target can reach it.** Error Inspector read as the skill prescribes — the loads carrying only valid Applies and a decline showed **no capture badge at all**, and only the load where the invalid value was forced showed **2**, the seam being loud by design and manufactured by the test. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3607 values) and `type-check` clean. **(3) The board is still swept, recomputed independently and agreeing with the entry directly below.** 36 manifests lack `writeTargets` at this commit (counting every non-`_` manifest, so overlay/record ones like `assistant-message` and `canvas-viewer` are in the total; the 34 below excludes a couple and is the same picture), and **only 23 have a live `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` / `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` mount at all** — every one of the 23 already ruled out with reasons above: the eight marketing report/table surfaces, the dashboards, the viewers and previews, `rag-library`'s browse-and-filter state, the hubs, `share`'s permissions, and `image-annotate` / `messages`. **ZERO chips fired.** The remainder is emitter-authoring work that prior entries put outside this campaign, and firing chips at read-only reports would spend a session each to re-derive an existing NO — the backlinks entry's point 8. **One harness note worth the next agent's hours:** driving this app headless needs `page.route` on `db.matrxserver.com` / `server.app.matrxserver.com` refetched through a proxied `request.newContext` created ONCE, because the egress proxy resets Chromium's own TLS to those hosts and **the visible symptom is not a network error — it is the Agents popover reading "Loading agents…" or "No agents bound yet"**, which looks exactly like a surface with no bound agent and invites precisely the wrong conclusion.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/messages` re-assessed for write targets and DECLINED AGAIN (the NO was already in the manifest); the surface's one read gap is closed instead, and closing it required REDEFINING the value rather than emitting it.** Assigned as a write-target chip whose scouting brief said the manifest "has no `writeTargets` yet" — true as a fact about the file, and wrong as a description of the situation. **The manifest already carried a dated ruling** ("WRITE TARGETS: evaluated 2026-08-11 and DELIBERATELY NOT DECLARED. Do not re-assign this surface"), so the assignment was a re-scout of settled ground, and the `html-page` rule below applies directly: reversing a documented judgment call is clobbering merged work even when it ADDS. **The ruling was re-derived from the live components rather than taken on trust, and it stands.** An independent sweep of every piece of editable state in `features/messaging/` found exactly the same single candidate the docblock names: `MessageInput`'s `content` (the composer), with `attachments` (ids an agent can only guess — the read half never publishes the user's library), `NewConversationDialog`'s people-search and create state (identity, outward-facing), and `ConversationList`'s `searchQuery` / `selectedConversationId` / `showNewConversation` (a mechanical filter, navigation, a UI toggle) all failing. Both load-bearing claims in the docblock were re-checked against code and both hold: `matrx-user/chat` really does ship `input_draft` as ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target taking `{text, mode?}` (`ChatInputDraftWrite`), so a `reply_draft` here would be a near-duplicate of a shipped design; and there is still no conversation write path anywhere — `MessagingService` exposes exactly `sendMessage`, `sendBridgeMessage`, `markConversationAsRead`, and no file in the repo writes the `conversations` table, so `current_conversation_title` would be the declared-target-with-no-canonical-write-path trap. **One field is below the ~2 floor, and `canvas` is the precedent for stopping there** rather than shipping a single near-duplicate target to make a chip look productive. **The campaign's own notes disagreed with themselves about this surface, which is why this entry is worth its length.** The 2026-08-12 `cms` entry calls `messages` "the strongest remaining surface on the board" and generated this chip; the 2026-08-12 `matrx-admin/email` entry, the same day, lists `messages` under "checked and FAILING the bar … a composer that would be sending"; and the manifest had ruled it out the day before either was written. Both scouting notes were made from manifest/route shape without opening the manifest docblock. **A surface's own manifest is the authority on whether it has been ruled out, and a scouting list that contradicts it is stale by construction** — worth checking first, because this chip cost a full agent to re-derive an existing answer. **What DID ship is the read half, and it is a correction rather than a fill-in.** `current_conversation_message_count` was declared but emitted by NEITHER mount — the surface's one documented read gap. It could not simply be emitted: it was declared as "number of messages in the open conversation", and **no such number exists on the client**. `useMessages` pages 50 at a time behind `hasMore`/`loadMoreMessages`, and neither `MessagingService` nor the `conversations` row exposes a total, so `messages.length` is the size of the LOADED WINDOW. Emitting it under the original description would have replaced a missing value with a confidently wrong one — **a read gap is visible; a plausible-but-false count is not.** The value is now labelled "Loaded message count" and its description says in the agent's own words that it is what is loaded, that it grows as the user scrolls back, and that it must never be reported as the conversation's size. **The plumbing detail worth reusing:** the messages live in `ChatThread`'s `useChat` subscription, one level BELOW the provider, so the count rides up through an `onLoadedMessageCountChange` callback into a ref the route's `getScope` reads — a ref, not state, because `getScope` is sampled when the user presses Run, and a second `useChat` in the route would have opened a duplicate realtime subscription. **Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on both mounts.** On the THREAD (`/messages/[conversationId]`, a thread with 4 messages) the agent read `current_conversation_message_count` back as exactly **4**, and asked in the same message to draft a reply into the composer it produced **zero confirm dialogs**, left the composer empty, and said it has no tool that writes there — the correct behaviour for a surface with no `writeTargets`, since no `apply_surface_write` is offered at all. On the LIST mount (`/messages`) the context panel reported **"Context Items (2)"**, exactly the `total_unread_count` + `all_conversations` the manifest promises for a route with no open conversation, with zero dialogs and the agent stating it had no page-editing tool. There is no message composer in the DOM there at all. **Zero `surface-writeback` captures** — structurally guaranteed here, since with no targets there is no write to capture, and reported as such rather than dressed up as a passing detector. The invalid-value and undeclared-target tests the skill mandates **do not apply and were not faked**: with no targets and no handlers there is no throw to provoke, and "undeclared" collapses into the no-tool-at-all result above. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3607 values) and `type-check` clean. **One finding the read fix exposes, which outlives this surface:** the agent did NOT see the manifest description. It reported the meaning as "Emitted by the active surface 'matrx-user/messages'" and then GUESSED "the count of messages in the currently open conversation" — precisely the false total the redefinition exists to prevent. Value descriptions reach a server-side agent through the `ui.ui_surface_value.description` DB mirror, not the client payload (`SurfaceScopePayload` carries values only), so until a manifest sync runs, the corrected wording is right in the source and invisible at runtime. **For most values a stale mirror is cosmetic; for this one it is the whole point of the change**, so the sync matters here more than the usual "pending" footnote suggests. The global one-click Sync Manifests was deliberately NOT run from this task: it applies THIS checkout's declarations for all 143 surfaces to a shared database, which would push a branch's state as authoritative over other agents' in-flight surfaces — disproportionate to one description, and the convention every entry above follows. **This surface is deliberately NOT added to the "Live agent-writable adopters" list** — it is not an adopter, and padding that index with a ruled-out surface corrupts the one place the campaign checks to see what is taken (the `marketing-integrations` rule). **CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — ZERO chips fired, because the board is swept, and here is the scan rather than an assertion.** Exactly 34 manifests still lack `writeTargets` (excluding `_baseline` / `_conversation-document`). Every candidate the recent handoffs nominated as strongest-remaining has since SHIPPED: `agent-advanced-editor`, `admin-lookups`, `admin-bundles` and `sandboxes` all now declare targets, so the `cms` and `matrx-admin/email` handoff lists above are spent and should not be re-mined. Of the 34, all but three were already ruled out in this log (the read-only viewers and records, the marketing report surfaces, `rag-library`/`rag-viewer`, `education*`, `gallery`, `scopes`, `agent-gate`, `agent-settings`, `observational-memory`, `admin-official-components`, and this surface). **The three never previously judged were checked here and all three fail, on their OWN docblocks' evidence:** `matrx-admin/mcp-servers` is a registry of connection configs and env schemas whose docblock already says refresh/test/config-save are "the admin's own button clicks" — its lone authored field is a new server's description, one target below the floor and credential-adjacent, on top of having no emitter; `matrx-admin/users` is the account roster, whose docblock states outright that "nothing here is a write path" and whose every field is identity, admin level, or entitlement — the skill's explicit NO, and also unemitted; `matrx-admin/sandbox` offers only stop, delete, and minting SSH keys — destructive plus one-time secret material its docblock deliberately refuses to declare — with no authored content anywhere on the page. **Firing chips at these would burn three agents to re-derive three NOs**, which is the failure this entry is otherwise about. The honest state of the campaign is: the surfaces worth making agent-writable have been made agent-writable, and what remains is reports, viewers, registries, and governance consoles. Reopening the board needs NEW editable surfaces to be built, not another sweep of this one.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/rag-library` is agent-writable: 4 ask-policy targets, and the campaign's clearest case of a surface REOPENED against two recorded rulings because the authored field was in a descendant, not on the page.** `library_filters`, `selected_document_id`, `selected_document_name` (handlers on `LibraryPage.tsx` and, for the rename, on `LibraryDocDetailSheet.tsx`) and `catalog_filters` (on `LibraryCatalogPage.tsx`). **(1) The find is where I looked, not how hard.** Two prior scans ruled this surface out in writing — the 2026-08-11 voice-pad sweep binned it under "pure list/search/filter view state", and the 2026-08-12 email handoff listed it under "Checked and FAILING the bar… every value is browse/report state — search queries, filters, counts, streaks". **Both are correct about every DECLARED VALUE and both miss the one authored field, because it is not page-level state:** the document rename lives in `LibraryDocDetailSheet`, a child the route never lifts, behind a `Pencil` button in a Dialog. Its read twin `selected_document_name` was already declared, which is exactly why a value-level scan slides past it — the value looks like a derived label of the selected row, and the editor for it is two components down. **Generalizing, and it matches the backlinks entry from the same day: the campaign's remaining "read-only" bin was assembled by surface-level and value-level scanning, and both methods are blind to an editor that lives in a descendant.** Grep for the canonical write (`.update({ name` / a service call) inside the subtree, not just the page. **(2) The honest floor call, stated rather than glossed.** There is exactly ONE authored-content field here. Strip the rename and this surface genuinely does fail the bar and the two prior rulings stand — that is said plainly in the manifest docblock so nobody reads this adoption as a licence to ship filter toggles. What carries it over is that the rename does not stand alone: a corpus of `Scan Aug 10 12:16 PM.pdf` and `scan_final_v3.pdf` is a corpus nobody can search, and naming one requires FINDING it first among rows the user cannot eyeball. Search → open → rename is one coherent job and the three library targets are its three steps, which is why `selected_document_id` is a real target rather than a mechanical toggle. **(3) Composite filters over micro-targets, per the `matrx-user/agents` precedent.** `library_filters` (`{search_query, status_filter}`) and `catalog_filters` (`{search_query, entitled_only}`) are single objects: "show me only the pending documents matching invoice" is ONE user intent and deserves ONE confirm dialog, and validating the whole object before touching any state makes a half-applied filter unreachable. Both got a matching composite READ twin (`library_filters` / `catalog_filters` in the emitter, built from fields `surround` already computed) so `updatesValue` points at a real 1:1 value and the evidence loop closes — the drift checker requires `updatesValue` to name a declared value, so pointing a 2-field composite at one of its halves would have been a lie it would happily have accepted. **(4) Anti-drift by construction.** `STATUS_FILTERS` moved out of `LibraryPage.tsx` into the new React-free `features/rag/constants/libraryStatusFilters.ts`, so the buttons the page renders, the enum interpolated into the model-facing description, and the handler's validation are ONE array. It is deliberately NOT `DocStatus`: that type also carries `chunking` and `unknown`, which render no button, so a filter set to either would strand the user in a view with no control to leave it — the `ownership_tab: "shared"` refusal from `agents-hub`, same shape. The handler's throw says so in as many words. **(5) One write path for the rename.** `handleRename` was refactored into a shared `renameDocumentTo(name)` that THROWS instead of toasting; the Rename dialog catches and toasts, the agent handler lets it throw so the seam turns it into the error envelope the model reads. Registered from the descendant via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, which proxies through a ref — so unlike a provider closure it always runs the current render's `doc`. The sheet is always mounted (it renders a closed `<Sheet>` when nothing is selected), so the target is never declared-but-unwired; with no document open the handler throws a message naming `selected_document_id` as the fix. **(6) The stale-closure hazard, and where it actually bites.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the PROVIDER's handlers before awaiting the confirm, which can sit open indefinitely. React state setters are stable and `STATUS_FILTERS` is a module constant, so those close over safely — but the document list is fetched and polls every few seconds, so `selected_document_id`'s id vocabulary genuinely changes under an open dialog and is read from a ref at call time, after Apply. Recorded because the distinction is easy to over- or under-apply: not every provider handler needs a ref, only the ones reading live data. **(7) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/rag/library` and `/rag/library-catalog`, no mocks.** The popover header named `matrx-user/rag-library`, so the route-resolution trap did not apply. Each ask dialog carried the manifest description VERBATIM. Apply on `library_filters` landed search `Scan` + status `Extracted` and the table went 24 rows → 2; `selected_document_id` opened the sheet; **`selected_document_name` PERSISTED — the table row itself re-rendered as the new name, and it was later restored to its original through the sheet's own Rename dialog, which also re-proved the human path after the `renameDocumentTo` extraction.** `catalog_filters` landed BOTH keys in one call (`search_query: legal` + `entitled_only: true`). "Keep as is" declined with no error and a graceful acknowledgement ("No problem — I left the search box as it was"), and nothing changed. The undeclared ask — set `documents_total` to 999 and delete the first document — raised NO dialog and staged nothing; the agent enumerated the three targets it actually had and refused both in the manifest's own terms. Two forced-invalid calls returned the handlers' throws word-for-word with Apply pressed and nothing staged, confirming validate-then-apply: `client_tool_error: library_filters.status_filter must be exactly one of: all, ready, embedding, extracted, pending. Received "archived"…` and `client_tool_error: library_filters received unknown key(s): sort_by. Nothing was changed.` **(8) Error Inspector, and a control test that stops a false positive.** On the clean load (three applies + a decline, no forced invalid): **zero `surface-writeback` captures** — but the badge showed **1**, a `500 internal_error` on `GET /rag/library/<id>/derivations`. **It is not mine and not the write path's:** opening the same document by plain CLICK with no agent mounted reproduces it exactly, 1 distinct · 1 total. Reported rather than rounded to zero, because "1 capture" on a write-target run is precisely the shape of a defect someone would otherwise pin on this change; it is a pre-existing backend fault and a real lead for whoever owns that endpoint. **(9) Deliberately NOT writable, each for its own reason:** every rollup and count (`documents_*`, `library_totals`, `segment_count`, `pages_persisted`, `embeddings_*`, `visible_documents`, `active_jobs`, `document_status_breakdown`) is DERIVED — the database's answer, not a field, and a target that "set" one would be a lie the user reads back as truth; ingest / re-embed / re-process, because **an embedding run spends real money per call and the human press IS the spending gate**; delete and trash, always; and catalog subscribe/unsubscribe, which changes what the user is ENTITLED to read — an access decision, not a view decision. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3609 values — the two new composite read twins) and `type-check` clean. `readiness` stays `"partial"`: DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync, which is why the `readinessNote` says the client tool works either way.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/agent-settings` is mounted and agent-writable (1 draft target), and it OVERTURNS the 2026-08-12 `matrx-admin/bundles` refusal of this exact surface — on the campaign's own shared-vocabulary precedent rather than on effort.** That entry refused it because "its four authored fields are `name` / `description` / `category` / `tags`, and `matrx-user/agent-builder` already ships `agent_name`, `agent_description`, `agent_category` and `agent_tags` over the same record, so the adoption would be four borrowed targets on a second surface". **The objection was right about duplication and wrong about the only available answer.** Four borrowed targets would indeed be a defect — so this pass ships ONE composite `settings_catalog_profile` whose CONTRACT is a single shared definition (`features/agents/surface-catalog-profile.ts`) that `matrx-user/agent-advanced-editor`'s existing `editor_catalog_profile` now imports too. That is exactly what `matrx-user/lists` / `matrx-user/list-manager` prescribes: when two surfaces edit the same fields, lift the definition, do not write a second copy. The refusal's second half was simply correct and was done first: the surface was `readiness: "stub"` with NO provider anywhere, so this was `surface-authoring` before it was `surface-write-targets`. **What is shared and what deliberately is NOT.** The contract is shared: accepted keys, per-field bounds, replace-vs-patch semantics, and the prose the model reads. The target NAME and the HANDLER are not, and both refusals are structural rather than lazy. (1) The two mounts have DIFFERENT draft containers — the advanced editor stages into the `agent-definition` Redux slice via `setAgentField` and commits on its own footer Save, while `AgentSettingsForm` stages into React `useState` and commits by dispatching `saveAgentField` per changed field. There is no one handler that serves both; the lists precedent shares one because both of its mounts are literally the same component driving the same server actions. (2) A shared bare NAME would land the write in the wrong window. Both are floating windows, they can be open at once — this form's own Messages / Variables / Tools tiles OPEN the advanced editor — and each has its own agent picker, so the agent open in one is frequently not the agent open in the other; `applySurfaceWrite` resolves deepest-first, so one name would stage the rewrite into a record the user who pressed Apply cannot see. That is the hazard `agent-advanced-editor` invented its `editor_` prefix for, and `settings_` answers it the same way. **One contract, two addresses — the two cannot drift, and neither can capture the other's write.** **A real bug the lift closed as a side effect:** `editor_catalog_profile` was validating with its own ad-hoc `requireProseText` and enforced NO length, tag-count, duplicate or comma rules, while `agent-identity-metadata.ts` — written precisely so "there is ONE place that says what a valid value is" — already held those bounds and was being used only by `matrx-admin/system-agents`. The shared contract routes both surfaces through those canonical validators and interpolates the real constants into the description, so the contract the model SEES is now the contract that is ENFORCED on both. **`matrx-user/agent-builder` is the third implementation of these same three columns and was deliberately NOT touched:** its four SEPARATE field targets use their own `requireText` with no bounds either, but it is a different target SHAPE on a surface another agent live-verified, and folding it in would need its own verification run. Flagged rather than silently diverged — it is the obvious next reconciliation. **The emitter, which was the larger half.** `AgentSettingsWindow` mounts the surface's first `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (`useAgentSettingsSurface.ts`, store-not-selector, window state through refs); the manifest went from 4 window-state values to 22 across 4 curated groups. The four authored read values come from the FORM's live draft through a one-slot publish point (`agentSettingsDraftRegistry.ts`), NOT from Redux — the window mounts the provider but the fields live two levels down in component state, so reading Redux would report the stored description while a different one sat unsaved in the box, making the write target's own read twins quietly wrong. The write half has a seam for exactly this (`useSurfaceWriteHandlers`); the read half has none, which is why the registry exists. **A gate that is easy to miss:** `AgentContentWindow` renders the SAME `AgentSettingsForm` on the advanced editor's Overview tab, so registration is gated behind a `writeSurfaceName` prop only `AgentSettingsWindow` passes — ungated, `getRegisteredWriteHandlers` would merge both instances and the last one mounted would answer for this surface. Same gate, same reason, as `matrx-user/lists`' `asRoute`. **Judgment.** The agent's NAME stays unwritable (doctrine, and consistent with `agent-advanced-editor`: the name is the label the tab strip and sidebar are navigated by). `isActive` / `isPublic` / `isFavorite` / `isArchived` are visibility and lifecycle; the Hierarchy Scopes picker RESTRICTS an agent's visibility and context; `SurfaceAgentBindPanel`'s bindings and value mappings change what an agent may REACH; `defaultRagBoost` is a retrieval-ranking multiplier nobody would ask an agent for. Save is never a target. **Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs**, from `/reports` — an UNMAPPED route, chosen deliberately because on this run's base commit `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` preferred the ROUTE-derived surface over the mounted runtime whenever they disagreed, and `/agents/settings` maps to this surface while NO page exists at that path, so the popover named "Agent Settings" only where the route mapped to nothing. **That constraint no longer applies**: the overlay-primary fix in the entry above landed on `main` mid-run, and because this manifest declares `overlayId` the window is now primary on ANY route. Re-confirmed after rebasing onto that fix — on `/dashboard`, a MAPPED route, the popover names Agent Settings and the same one-message rewrite applies and stages correctly. Worth knowing which half depended on it: the WRITE tool never did (`listAgentWritableTargets()` walks the mounted provider stack, not the route), but the READ scope did, because `handleRun` only samples the live runtime when the launched surface matches it. **An environment flake to recognise rather than debug, hit twice during that re-confirmation:** two `/dashboard` runs died with "Agent failed unexpectedly" before reaching the confirm, and the Error Inspector showed the cause is SERVER-side and generic — `Persistence commit barrier 'turn_1_delegate_precommit' failed (asyncpg UndefinedColumnError: column "user_id" of relation "user_request" does not exist [SQLSTATE 42703])`. It kills any turn that makes a DELEGATED tool call, so it looks exactly like a broken write target while being nothing to do with the page; the tell is that ZERO of its captures are `surface-writeback`, and a retry on the same route succeeded with 0 captures. This is the same class as the flake the `matrx-admin/bundles` entry recorded — retry the whole run, do not read it as a seam defect. One message asking for a description rewrite plus a category plus tags produced exactly ONE confirm carrying the manifest prose verbatim; Apply staged all three into the real inputs with "Unsaved changes..." and Save armed; Save persisted and a fresh page load confirmed it; before Save, a fresh load showed the ORIGINAL values, proving draft mode rather than asserting it. "Keep as is" left everything untouched and the agent replied "You declined the change, so the category was left as Diagnostics" — correctly reading its own read twin. Asking it to flip the Public switch produced NO tool call and a plain explanation quoting the contract's exclusion list back. Two forced-invalid calls returned the handlers' own throws word-for-word — `settings_catalog_profile does not accept "is_public". …` and `settings_catalog_profile.tags entry "surface-write, throwaway" contains a comma; …` (the canonical validator's own message, re-addressed at the composite key) — and in BOTH cases nothing was staged, the valid `description` in the same object included: validate-the-whole-object-then-apply, proven rather than claimed. Error Inspector on the clean path: "No errors captured", 0 distinct / 0 total. The Surface Context window reports **Live, 19/28 supplied, contract honored, 1/1 agent-writable** with no "Undeclared (runtime only)" section, which is what earns `readiness: "verified"`. `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces, 3626 values) and `type-check` both clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **NO CHIPS FIRED — a fourth independent audit reaching the same verdict, with the two candidates no earlier audit named.** 145 manifests; 36 lack `writeTargets`, of which 2 are shared infrastructure (`_baseline`, `_conversation-document`). Cross-referencing the rest against which surfaces actually MOUNT a provider leaves nothing that clears the bar. Everything else is already taken, already ruled out, in flight, or a read-only report/viewer/preview (`ai-results`, `agent-run-history`, `assistant-message`, `context-preview`, `file-preview`, `image-viewer`, `rag-viewer`, `dashboard`, and the six `marketing-*` report surfaces), an ops console (`admin-sandbox`, `admin-server-logs`, `admin-users`), or permissions-shaped (`share`). **Two that earlier audits did not name, assessed here so a fifth agent does not re-derive them:** `matrx-admin/mcp-servers` is the most adoptable-LOOKING of what is left — its manifest says out loud that an agent may "help the admin draft a new server's description", and it has no emitter, so it is the same emitter-first shape as this surface. It is still a NO: that description is the ONLY authored field, which is under the ~2-YES bar, and everything else on the page (connection configs, env schemas, sync, live connection testing) decides what agents can REACH. `matrx-user/agent-gate` looks adoptable and is a trap: it hosts a `SmartAgentInput` for the user's reply, which is genuinely authored text, but the gate is the pre-execution CHECKPOINT an agent run is being held at — letting the agent draft its own answer to its own gate defeats the control entirely. Chips fired at any of these would burn agent runs on surfaces that have now been judged four times.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/transcript-scribe` assessed for write targets and DECLINED: zero targets earned, and a provider for it would be structurally unreachable. No code changed — this entry exists so the surface is not assigned again.** Three independent reasons, each sufficient. **(1) Its route's writable artifact is already declared by a sibling.** `/transcripts/scribe/:sessionId` authors exactly one thing — the working document — and `matrx-user/transcript-scribe-live` declares `working_document_content` + `append_working_document` over it (2026-08-11), on the IDENTICAL `urlPattern`. A second path over the same artifact is the duplication this doctrine forbids. **(2) The scouted candidates are on a different route and also already shipped.** The cleaned-transcript and concepts columns render only in `ActiveSessionView` (`/transcripts/studio` + the `transcriptStudioWindow` overlay), never in `ScribeScreen`, and `matrx-user/transcript-studio` made them writable the same day via `cleaned_segment_text` + `concept_item`. The Scribe route's own transcript viewers hold no editable text. **(3) A `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` for this surface could never be selected.** All four Scribe tabs stay mounted (visibility flips via `hidden`), so `ScribeLiveScreen`'s provider is live on every tab; `launch-agent-execution.thunk.ts` adopts ONE surface per run from `getSurfaceRuntime()` — the deepest registered runtime — so a provider on the `ScribeScreen` ancestor would never win and its `writeTargets` would be dead code. Note the asymmetry worth remembering: `applySurfaceWrite` resolves handlers deepest-FIRST across the whole stack, but agent LAUNCH adopts a single surface, so "an ancestor provider can still serve a write" does not imply "an agent will ever be offered its targets". The only uncovered editable control, the session title, is a refusal already documented on `transcript-studio` (`transcripts-cleanup` owns that column; `useStudioAutoLabel` derives it) and is one target regardless — under the ~2-YES bar. **This surface is config-and-roles, not page-runtime:** it owns the `dictionary` / `session_defaults` namespaces and the `assistant` role, and its `readiness: "partial"` note remains accurate; the page-runtime twin on its URL is `transcript-scribe-live`. **Campaign status observed while scouting:** every manifest still lacking `writeTargets` is now either shared infrastructure (`_baseline`, `_conversation-document`), a read-only report/viewer, or an admin/ops console (`admin-cx-dashboard`, `admin-mcp-servers`, `admin-sandbox` — a fleet monitor, `admin-server-logs`, `admin-users` — identity/permissions). No chips were fired: assigning any of these would waste an agent.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/bundles` was assigned TWICE. The second run adds no targets; it adds an independent NO on `bundle_member_alias`, an independent re-verification of the two shipped targets, and THE finding that `agent.review_queue` detects a collision hours before `main` does.** A second chip assigned this surface while the entry below was still in flight, scouting `bundle_description` + a `{tool_id, alias}` member-alias target as "two clean targets". **The skill's STEP ZERO passed cleanly THREE ways while a complete, live-verified implementation already existed:** `git show origin/main:…/admin-bundles.manifest.ts | grep writeTargets` was empty on latest `main`, `git ls-remote origin 'refs/heads/claude/surface-write-admin-bundles'` returned nothing (the branch had already been deleted), and `check:surface-drift` reported the surface with no targets. Re-fetching `main` twice more during the run kept returning the same clean answer. What actually caught it was the `agent-review-queue` step at the END of the checklist: registering requires checking for an existing row on the same `url`, and there was one — created 04:19 UTC, carrying `metadata.origin.branch = claude/surface-write-admin-bundles` and `commit = 72651baa`. At that moment `72651baa` was fetchable but **not an ancestor of `main`**; it merged roughly five hours later, mid-run, and `main` only started reporting `writeTargets` on the FINAL pre-commit check. **So the review-queue row is the earliest available collision signal, because it is written when work FINISHES while `main` and the branch list only show work that has MERGED — and the gap between those two is exactly the window in which a surface gets assigned twice. Check `agent.review_queue` for the target url FIRST, not last.** (`ui.ui_surface_write_target` is not a substitute: the DB mirror is synced separately and lags further.) **The member-alias NO, derived independently and converging on the shipped exclusion.** The second run's intended target was `{tool_id, alias}` through `setBundleMemberAlias`, membership-bounded so an unknown `tool_id` is refused rather than inserted, on the reasoning that membership changes WHICH tools an agent can reach while an alias only changes what one is CALLED. The shipped design refuses it with the stronger argument: a member's `local_alias` IS the identifier `bundle_lister` hot-swaps the tool in under, so renaming one breaks call sites mid-run — the same class as renaming a bundle out from under its `bundle:list_<name>` lister, not a copy edit. Two agents reaching that exclusion from opposite directions is worth recording so a third does not spend a run re-deriving it; note also that the surface intro's blessing to "draft member aliases" means helping the admin CHOOSE one while adding a tool, not rewriting existing ones. **Both shipped targets independently re-verified** on `main` with real Badass Agent runs against `desktop-web` — an INACTIVE, non-system bundle chosen because `listAgentBundleOptions` filters `is_active = true`, so edits to it reach no picker. Each raised its own confirm carrying the manifest prose verbatim. `bundle_description`: Apply staged a better description into the inline box and **SQL confirmed the row untouched, `updated_at` still `2026-07-18T07:55:04Z`** — draft mode proving itself rather than being asserted. `new_bundle_draft` with NO bundle selected: Apply OPENED the New bundle form already filled (`name: browser-automation` plus a three-sentence description), Cancel discarded it, and a before/after diff of `tool.bundle` shows **63 rows → 63 rows, nothing created and nothing removed**. `check:surface-drift` clean (142 surfaces, 3528 values); `type-check` clean for every bundles file. **A stale fact corrected:** the 2026-08-11 `matrx-user/messages` entry records that this console "currently fails to load any rows (\"Failed to load bundles\" against `tool.bundle`), so it cannot be live-verified until that data path is fixed". That no longer reproduces — an anonymous PostgREST read of `tool.bundle` returns 200 with rows and the console lists 54 active / 63 total. Beware the count trap it hides: an authenticated admin sees 64, because personal bundles are RLS-scoped, so an anon `curl` snapshot undercounts by exactly the rows only the browser can see. **Do not diff those two lists against each other** — doing so briefly read as another agent creating and deleting a fixture bundle mid-run. **CAMPAIGN STATE — no chips fired, with the count behind it.** 144 manifests, 87 carry `writeTargets`, 57 do not; every one of the 57 is already taken, already ruled out on the bar, carries an in-flight `claude/surface-write-*` branch, or is a `readiness: "stub"` viewer, ops console, or list-and-filter mount. Both candidates the 2026-08-11 `data-tables` handoff named are now done — this surface, and `matrx-user/smart-code-editor`, which has since shipped targets. The last plausible candidate, `matrx-user/agent-settings`, is refused on the `messages` precedent rather than on effort: its four authored fields are `name` / `description` / `category` / `tags`, and `matrx-user/agent-builder` **already ships `agent_name`, `agent_description`, `agent_category` and `agent_tags`** over the same record, so the adoption would be four borrowed targets on a second surface — and its manifest declares only `active_agent_id` / `open_agent_ids` / `panel_view` / `bound_surface_name`, window state that does not describe the form at all, so it would need `surface-authoring` first regardless. **The well is dry; chips fired at what remains would burn agent runs on surfaces that were already judged.** **Two environment notes, neither about this seam:** the agent run window intermittently fails to mint a conversation (`instances/smartExecute` rejected with "Conversation already exists" on roughly half of cold runs, 3 Error-Inspector captures each and **none of them `surface-writeback`**) — retry the whole run rather than the in-page Retry button, and never read those captures as a write-seam defect. And after `main` moved 8 patch versions under an existing clone, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` **and** `rm -rf .next` were both needed before any route resolved; the symptom was `/login` itself serving the app's own "We couldn't find that page", which looks exactly like the documented corrupt-`.next` trap but is not fixed by the cache wipe alone.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/cms-site` gains a second, DIFFERENT-TAB pass: `site_name` + `add_page`, and the first demonstration that ONE manifest can serve two tabs with two disjoint tool sets.** Assigned as a `matrx-user/voice-pad` chip; **voice-pad was declined on the judgment bar and this surface done instead** (see the voice-pad ruling below). The 2026-08-10 pass had already landed four Settings-tab draft targets; that work was merged first and is kept WHOLESALE — this adds only what was genuinely additive, and amends their manifest docblock and `<surface_intro>` rather than replacing them. **(1) `site_name` (draft, Settings).** The one identity-adjacent field on `client_sites` that moves nothing: unlike slug and domain, renaming a site changes no URL and breaks no link, so naming is authorship here. It stages into the same `useState` the Site Name input drives, in the SAME General card as `site_global_css`, so those two save together on one Save Changes press — stated in the description because the merged pass's one-card-at-a-time warning otherwise reads as applying to it too. **(2) `add_page` (entity, PAGES tab) — the interesting half.** The merged intro said writes happen "only on the Settings tab", which was true and is now not: the Pages tab owns `refreshPages` and mounts its own nested provider, so it can service a decomposition action the Settings tab cannot. `add_page` runs the SAME `CmsPageService.createPage` the New Page route calls and then `refreshPages()`, so `pages_summary` / `site_structure` re-read true immediately. What makes it safe to declare as `entity` on a surface whose whole doctrine is "staging is not saving" is the SHAPE of what it creates: an UNPUBLISHED, out-of-nav, empty stub. It writes a row but changes nothing the live site serves, and the row has the list's own delete control. **(3) The tab split is the transferable find.** `listAgentWritableTargets()` skips any declared target with no live handler, so registering handlers per tab is not a workaround for the "declared-but-unwired is a loud defect" rule — it is the supported way to give one surface different tool sets per mount. Verified as behaviour, not assumed: on Settings the agent is offered the five staging targets and not `add_page`; on Pages it is offered `add_page` alone. **(4) The policy line falls out of the split.** `agent_write_policy` gates SAVING, so the five staging targets stay legal even on a `blocked` site (the merged pass's argument, unchanged) while `add_page` genuinely writes and is therefore the ONE target that refuses under `blocked` — the handler throws with the reason and points at the human fix. Slug, domain, favicon, active and delete stay human. **(5) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `dev-website`, on the MERGED code, no mocks.** One message asking for three things produced per-target ask dialogs carrying each manifest description VERBATIM (including the added `site_name` prose); Apply landed "Dev Website Agent Test" in the Site Name box and appended the print stylesheet without wiping the existing CSS; the undeclared favicon ask raised no dialog and drew a plain refusal in which the agent correctly enumerated the targets it actually had. On the Pages tab, Apply created "Merged Test Page" (`/merged-test-page`) into the live list, and **"Keep as is" on a second create declined with no error** — "You declined the page creation, so nothing was added" — and no row appeared. Two forced-invalid calls returned the handlers' throws word-for-word: `site_global_css expects the full stylesheet as a plain CSS string…` and `add_page slug must be lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens (e.g. about-our-team) — omit it to derive one from the title.` **Error Inspector on a clean load (applies + a decline, no forced throw): "No errors captured yet", 0 distinct · 0 total.** `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces, 3607 values) + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending. Test pages created during verification were deleted afterwards; the staged site name was never saved. **(6) The JSON-parse trap, avoided by construction:** the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, so a string-typed target cannot receive raw JSON text — both string targets say "NOT JSON and NOT JSON-encoded" in the description AND in the throw, and `add_page` takes a real object instead.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/voice-pad` earns ONE write target, which is below the bar, so it was DECLINED rather than token-adopted.** Recorded so nobody re-scouts it. The pad's entire state is `voicePadSlice`'s `entries` + `draftText` plus a local `liveTranscript` and a font-size control. **`draft_text` is the single honest YES** — the combined transcript the user edits, and an agent cleaning up dictated text is a real ask. Everything else fails: `live_transcript` is transcription OUTPUT, not input; `transcript_entries` are a LOG of what the user actually said, timestamped and rendered as such, so an agent appending to them fabricates provenance rather than drafting content (the `matrx-user/chat` record-vs-draft line, applied to speech); `clear all` / `remove entry` are destructive; font size is a mechanical toggle nobody asks an agent to flip. There is no pad title, no per-entry edit, and no sibling mount of this surface — `VoicePad.tsx` is the only `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` for `matrx-user/voice-pad`. **One YES field is below the skill's ~2 floor and a composite cannot rescue it**, because a composite aggregates fields that are individually YES and there is only one. The chip was reassigned to `matrx-user/cms-site` under the same rules, per the skill's own instruction to say so and pick a better surface. **What would reopen it:** the pad growing a saved-note title or per-entry editing — either would put a second authored field in the same mount.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/agent-apps` edit shell agent-writable: the surface now has a per-mount posture on BOTH halves, and it is the campaign's clearest demonstration that one manifest can serve seven mounts with different answers.** The 2026-08-10 pass made the CATEGORIES editor writable (3 `mode:"draft"` targets); this adds the only other mount that authors copy — the edit/[id] app shell — with `app_metadata` (a composite `{name?, tagline?, description?}`), `app_category` and `app_tags`, all `applyPolicy:"ask"`, handlers on that page's own `getWriteHandlers`. **The two passes collided and both are kept**: the categories work had already merged, so it was taken wholesale and this pass added only what was genuinely additive. **The five silent mounts are the point.** `listAgentWritableTargets()` offers a target only where THAT mount registered a handler, so dashboard, apps list, executions/errors, analytics and rate-limits offer an agent no `apply_surface_write` tool at all — verified live, not assumed: a run on the apps LIST asking to retag an app produced zero dialogs and the agent stated it had no write tool in its toolset. Read-only reports whose only mutable state is filters and sort do not earn handlers, and enforcement (rate limits, unblocks) never will. **`entity` over the preferred `draft`, stated in the description because the reason is structural:** the edit shell has no draft layer to stage into — it holds the loaded row plus a metadata modal whose form state exists only while the modal is open — so a `draft` write would have nowhere to land. Each handler therefore goes through the SAME `PATCH /api/agent-apps/[id]` the modal's Save calls. **That PATCH is a raw `.update(body)` passthrough with no server-side column allow-list**, which is why the validation core (`features/agent-apps/lib/admin-app-write-targets.ts`, pure and unit-tested) matters more than usual: whatever leaves it is written to `app.definition` verbatim, so the allow-list IS the guard, and the tests assert `slug` / `status` / `is_featured` / `is_verified` / `is_public` / rate limits / `component_code` / `user_id` are each rejected by name. **Reuse over a second contract:** `app_tags` has no validator here at all — it imports `validateAppTags` from `features/agent-apps/route/agent-app-entity-writes.ts`, written for the user-facing `matrx-user/agent-apps` surface, so `app.definition.tags` keeps ONE contract across both surfaces rather than two that drift. `app_category` is the one place the admin console is deliberately STRICTER than its user-facing twin: `validateAppCategory` there accepts any non-empty string (a user's own picker offers free-text categories), while here the value must match the live `platform.categories` vocabulary case-insensitively and is stored in that list's canonical casing — an agent inventing a category fragments the taxonomy this console exists to govern. Because that vocabulary is a DB table rather than a code constant, it cannot be interpolated into the description; instead the page loads it once and republishes it as a new `available_app_categories` read value which the handler ALSO validates against, so the list an agent is told to choose from and the list the handler accepts are the same array. `selected_app_summary` gained `tags` and the Metadata card gained a Tags row, so a tag write has a visible twin instead of landing invisibly. **Judgment on the app shell:** the slug and id (identity), `status` / `is_public` / `is_featured` / `is_verified` (each one the admin's own button in `AgentAppAdminActions`), rate limits, ownership, delete, and `component_code` / `variable_schema` / `allowed_imports` all stay human — changing what an app RUNS or may REACH is a capability change, not a copy edit, the line `matrx-user/agent-builder` drew. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs: three targets applied from ONE message and persisted (card + reload), each ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` and the agent reported it as declined without retrying, a publish+feature request produced no tool call and a plain explanation, and three forced-invalid calls returned the handlers' own throws word-for-word — including `app_category: "Wizardry" is not a system category. Choose exactly one of: Writing, Productivity, Research, Coding, Education, Marketing, Creative, Business, Data, Other.`, the ten real names read from the live table. Error Inspector: "No errors captured" on the clean path. `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces) + `type-check` + the new unit suite all clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **Pre-existing bug found, NOT fixed:** `PATCH /api/agent-apps/[id]` is RLS-blocked (`PGRST116`, 0 rows) for any app the signed-in admin does not own, including global/system rows — so the admin's own "Edit name / tagline" Save fails identically there, with the Postgres error swallowed into a generic 500. It cost an hour to diagnose because the failure looks like a broken write target; the tell is that the HUMAN modal fails the same way on the same row. Routing the console's metadata save through an admin-client path (as `DELETE` on that route already does for global rows) is the fix and needs its own superadmin check, so it is not a drive-by. **Also worth a second look, found while scouting and deliberately not touched:** the merged `category_name` target's rationale says renaming a category "re-labels it everywhere it is offered to users" — but `app.definition.category` stores the category NAME as loose text (`AgentAppCategoryPicker` commits `opt.name`, and the categories page's own delete dialog says apps are orphaned because the reference is loose), so a rename may orphan every app assigned to that category rather than re-label them. That is the merged design's call to revisit, not this pass's to overturn.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/transcript-studio` is mounted and agent-writable.**
  `StudioView` now provides the runtime for both `/transcripts/studio` and the
  floating window; the route alias is ordered above `/transcripts`. Its 26-value
  manifest preserves the existing cleaning/concept pipeline vocabulary and
  exposes exactly two ask-policy entity targets: `concept_item` and
  `cleaned_segment_text`. Both resolve the current row at apply time and dispatch
  the same canonical thunks as the row Save buttons. `session_title`, deletes,
  recording controls, settings, and module-defined payloads remain excluded.
  `CONCEPT_KINDS` is the one enum shared by parser, editor, handler, and manifest.
  Live verification covered apply, decline, invalid-kind refusal, undeclared
  target refusal, emitted tool-spec presence, and a clean Error Inspector.
- **2026-08-12 — Backlinks is agent-writable: ONE composite entity target on a page four separate scans had written off as a read-only report.** `matrx-user/marketing-backlinks` declares `backlink_refresh_schedule`, an `applyPolicy: "ask"` / `mode: "entity"` target taking `{enabled?, cadence?, detail_limit?}`, handled on `BacklinksWorkspace`'s existing provider (it mounted `getScope` but no `getWriteHandlers`). **(1) The find is the method, not the field.** The 2026-08-11 voice-pad sweep, the data-tables handoff, the email handoff and the 2026-08-12 integrations ruling all bin this surface under "read-only reports and analysis OUTPUT" — and all four are right about the page as a whole and wrong about the page. They classified at the SURFACE level; the DataForSEO schedule card is a genuine editor (Switch + cadence Select + a numeric input + its own Save) living behind a collapsed `Settings2` toggle, which is precisely what a surface-level skim cannot see. The integrations entry had even pointed here in writing — "`cadence` / `detailLimit` are edited on `BacklinksWorkspace`… those two ARE planning fields and a plausible YES on whichever surface actually renders them" — so the lead existed and only control-level scanning cashed it. **Worth generalizing: a page whose CONTENT is a report can still own a control, and the campaign's remaining "read-only" bin was assembled by the method that missed this one.** **(2) One target, and the count is honest rather than padded.** "Check this site for new links weekly and pull 500 rows" is the derivable-planning-field YES; the three fields sit behind ONE Save and are ONE decision, so a composite beats three micro-targets (the `page_meta_tags` rule). Omitted keys keep the SAVED value, so "check weekly" need not restate a row limit — verified live, where a cadence-only patch left `enabled` and `detail_limit` untouched. Everything else on the page is gated evidence or model judgment — every backlink row, referring domain, anchor, competitor, score, verdict and summary — and writing any of it would forge evidence, which the surface intro forbids in its own words. `refresh_profile` was examined and ruled NO in the manifest so nobody re-scouts it: real view state, but a mechanical selector beside the Refresh button the user must press anyway, where the agent's real contribution is a recommendation it can already give in prose. **(3) The mode call, which is the interesting one, and the opposite reasoning from `marketing-reputation`.** There IS a staging buffer here (`schedule` useState + `scheduleDirty` + a disabled Save), so `draft` — the preferred mode — looks correct. It is not: the read twin `refresh_schedule` is built from `savedSchedule` and its own description says it "always reflects the saved site row, not unsaved edits", so a staged write is invisible to the twin; and because the card is collapsed by default it would be invisible to the USER as well. That is an apply with no evidence loop on either side. `entity` + `ask` was chosen instead, the handler re-opens the settings card so the change lands where the user can see it, and the manifest description SAYS which mode and why, so the next reader inherits the argument rather than re-deriving it. The reputation surface reached `entity` because it had no buffer; this one reached `entity` despite having one. **(4) One write path, shared with the button.** `saveSchedule` was refactored into `persistSchedule(next)` — the user's Save click and the agent handler now call the same function, so an agent apply cannot diverge from what the button does; it throws instead of toasting, and each caller decides how to surface that. It also gained the freshest-row `siteRef` guard from `MarketingPageWriteTargets`: `setQueryData` only reaches the `site` prop on the next render, so two consecutive applies in one agent message would otherwise send a stale `version` and trip `SiteVersionConflictError` as a spurious "changed while you were editing". **(5) Anti-drift by construction.** `DATAFORSEO_DETAIL_LIMIT_MIN|MAX` are new exports in `integrations-schema.ts`, and `validateSiteIntegrations`, the schedule input's `min`/`max`, the handler's throw and the model-facing description all read them; `cadence` interpolates `dataForSeoCadences`. The confirm dialog rendered `weekly | monthly` and `1-1000` from the constants, where a reader can check them. **(6) Live-verified with five real Badass Agent runs against a throwaway site (never a customer record), no mocks.** The ask dialog carried the manifest description VERBATIM. Apply persisted through the handler — confirmed by SQL on `web.site.integrations` → `marketing.providers.dataforseo`, seeded `{false, monthly, 250}` → `{true, weekly, 500}` with `version` 1→2. "Keep as is" declined with no error ("You declined the change, so I've left the schedule as-is") and the row's `version` did NOT advance. **Both invalid shapes returned the handler's throw to the model verbatim** — `client_tool_error: backlink_refresh_schedule: detail_limit must be a whole number from 1 to 1000 — the provider will not return more than 1000 rows in one pull.` and `client_tool_error: backlink_refresh_schedule: cadence must be one of weekly | monthly.` — with **Apply pressed on both and `version` still unchanged**, confirming validate-then-apply. The undeclared ask (write `referring_domain_opinions` to score example.com "trusted / 95") raised NO dialog and staged nothing; the agent enumerated the three targets it actually had and volunteered that a domain verdict "is an evidence/judgment layer, not a page write surface". **The read twin closed the loop in a fresh run**, quoting back the exact `{enabled: true, cadence: monthly, detail_limit: 500}` a previous run's partial patch had left — and, following the description's own instruction, the agent correctly warned that its in-context snapshot was pre-write because `getScope()` is sampled when the user presses ▶. **(7) Error Inspector, both readings.** On a CLEAN load (valid apply + a decline, no forced invalid): **0 distinct · 0 total occurrences, "No errors captured yet."** On the forced-invalid load the badge showed **4** — two captures per contract break, the seam being loud BY DESIGN and manufactured by the test. Reporting the dirty number is what makes the clean one mean anything. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3576 values) and `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync — the client tool works either way, which is why every run above passed. **(8) NO chips fired, deliberately.** The pool really is dry: the 2026-08-12 control-level sweep that produced this assignment checked every remaining `verified` marketing surface (`-sitemaps`, `-links`, `-coverage`, `-audit`, `-analysis`, `-site-pages`, `-ranks-hub`) at the CONTROL level and found zero text inputs and zero canonical update calls in all seven — they are genuinely read-only, not mis-classified the way this one was. Firing 3-5 chips to satisfy the avalanche contract would cost whole sessions for nothing. **The remaining work is `surface-authoring`** — building emitters for `agent-advanced-editor`, `extractor-chunker`, `observational-memory` and `transcript-studio`, which mount no runtime at all — which is a different skill and not this campaign.

- **2026-08-12 — The `seo.competitor_opportunity` blocker recorded in the reputation write-target entry below is RESOLVED, and review row `c6ee2b3b` is unblocked.** The fix landed in the DB, not in surfaces: aidream migration `0338_seo_competitor_serp_visibility_rename_and_kernel_type_guard` renamed the colliding numeric `seo.competitor{,_observation}.visibility` to `serp_visibility`, and `iam.accessible_entity_ids` now applies its visibility lane only when the column really is the `platform.visibility` enum (the probe is qualified on `udt_schema`/`udt_name`), so a domain column that merely shares the name degrades to no lane instead of raising `22P02 invalid input syntax for type numeric: "internal"`. Re-verified live as `admin@admin.com`: every read behind `getReputationWorkspace` returns 200 (the competitor-opportunity count is `0-4/5` on site `38eff4c9`, and 200/empty on the review row's own site), cross-org isolation is unchanged (in-org member 3/3/5, outsider 0/0/0), and the enum lane still fires for real ACL tables. **The guard narrows only — it can never widen access** — and it closed a real leak on the way (`education.quiz_sessions.visibility` is `text`, where `'personal' >= 'internal'` is lexically true; see [`features/marketing/FEATURE.md`](../marketing/FEATURE.md) for the measured before/after). `agent.review_queue` row `c6ee2b3b-a241-4d2c-9bd3-57a99ea81734` had its stale BLOCKER paragraph removed and `triage.assignment.state` moved `blocked → ready`, so the `reputation_case_triage` target below can finally be reviewed; the agent-write path itself is NOT re-verified here and still needs the browser pass named in that row.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/marketing-integrations` earns ZERO write targets, and the NO is now written into the manifest so nobody re-scouts it.** Assigned as a write-target chip on the plausible theory that the surface splits cleanly — authored config text (labels, notes) as YES, provider bindings and API keys as NO. **It does not split: there is no authored half.** The ruling and its full field inventory now live in the manifest docblock, at the source, rather than only in this file. **Every control `SiteIntegrationsWorkspace` renders is a credential, a credential authority, an account connection, or a resource/property binding.** Taken from the components rather than from `SiteIntegrationsDraft`: the three `BuiltInProviderCard`s expose exactly an `enabled` Switch, a "Google connection" Select that writes `credentialRef` and force-sets `credentialAuthority: "external_connection"`, and a property Select that writes `resourceRef` (PageSpeed exposes only `enabled` — `ProviderReferenceFields` short-circuits to a note because it rides the application quota key); `CustomProviderRow` exposes `label`, `key`, `credentialAuthority`, a raw-UUID `credentialRef`, and `resourceRef`. **The two field clusters that a type-level scout would count as candidates are not on this page at all:** `cms.*` and `dataForSeo.*` are parsed into the draft and re-serialized by `buildSiteIntegrations`, but this workspace renders no editor for either — `cms.kind` has no editor anywhere in the app, and DataForSEO's `cadence` / `detailLimit` are edited on `BacklinksWorkspace` (`matrx-user/marketing-backlinks`). Declaring them here would be a parallel write path with no user twin to mirror, which the handler contract forbids. **The three arguments that look like a YES, rebutted in the docblock:** (1) *binding the Search Console property is derivable from `google_resources`* — it genuinely is, and it still loses twice, because `startGoogleConnection` ALREADY does it deterministically by matching `sc-domain:{site.domain}`, and a wrong pick silently pipes another site's search data into this brand's evidence chain while `kickGscFirstImport` fires a ~16-month backfill against Google's API; (2) *`customProviders[].label` is authored free text* — the only free text on the page, and it cannot stand alone, since `validateSiteIntegrations` requires a label AND a `PROVIDER_KEY_PATTERN` key, and an enabled row additionally requires an authority plus a UUID `credentialRef`, so a label-only target writes a row that can never save and the composite that WOULD save necessarily carries credentials; (3) *the `enabled` toggles are cheap* — they are mechanical and worse than cheap, since `onEnable` persists immediately through `persistBuiltInProvider` and the GSC path can trigger that backfill. **Net: zero YES fields** — below the ~2 floor, and NOT rescuable by a composite, because a composite aggregates fields that are individually YES and every field here is individually NO. It independently reproduces the 2026-08-11 campaign scan, which had already binned this surface under "capability or permission governance"; what is new is the field-level proof at the source. **No code changed, so no live agent run was staged and no `agent.review_queue` row was written** (nothing writable = nothing for Arman to test); this entry and the docblock are the whole deliverable. **This surface is deliberately NOT added to the "Live agent-writable adopters" list above** — it is not an adopter, and padding that index with a ruled-out surface would corrupt the one place the campaign checks to see what is taken. The read half stays valuable and untouched: `setup_assistant` / `integration_diagnostician` work off `provider_bindings`, `configuration_issues` and the Google inventory to explain the next click, and the user performs the connection. **What would reopen it:** this workspace growing per-provider notes, or the DataForSEO `cadence` / `detailLimit` controls moving here from Backlinks — those two ARE planning fields and a plausible YES on whichever surface actually renders them, which is a real pointer for a Backlinks scout.
- **2026-08-12 — Scheduling admin: the OTHER six tabs now emit, the cron arity hole is closed, and two unemittable values are deleted.** Follow-up on the cron-tester entry directly below, landed after it merged; that design is kept intact and this is additive only. **(1) The surface finally emits everywhere, not just on one tab.** `SchedulingAdminLayoutClient` mounts an OUTER `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` that derives `active_tab` from the pathname, and Overview / Tasks / Runs / Orphan leases / Scanner health publish their own slices into a one-slot module store (`features/scheduling/lib/admin-scheduling-scope.ts`) that the shell's `getScope` reads at Run time. The cron tester's provider nests INSIDE that one and still wins on its tab by depth, so nothing about its behaviour changed. The store stamps each slice with the tab that published it and DROPS it when that does not match the pathname's tab, so a run on Scanner health can never report the Runs tab's filters — verified live: a run on `/runs` returned exactly `active_tab: runs`, `run_status_filter: any`, `run_surface_filter: any`, `run_row_count: 200` with no `cron_*` leakage from the previously-mounted tab. **(2) A 6-field cron was silently accepted, and the description already promised it would not be.** `validateCron` delegates to `cron-parser`, which takes 4 and 6 fields too — it reads a 6th as a leading SECONDS field. Measured, not assumed: `"0 9 * * 1-5 *"` parses CLEAN and previews 2027-01-01T00:09 instead of weekdays at 9am, and `"0 9 * * "` (4 fields) previews Sep 9. The agent would then read that confident, wrong preview back as confirmation — the exact failure the evidence loop exists to catch. `createCronTesterWriteHandlers` now checks arity BEFORE calling the validator, and nothing else: ranges, steps and names still belong to the real parser rather than being re-typed. Live-verified — a forced `"0 9 * * 1-5 *"` came back as `client_tool_error: cron_expression expects exactly 5 space-separated fields ... "0 9 * * 1-5 *" has 6`, with the input still at its default. **(3) `task_search` and `task_enabled_filter` were read lies and are now deleted.** The Tasks tab has no such page state: its search box and enabled/disabled filter live inside `MatrxDataTable`'s internals, which `tasks/page.tsx` never reads (its state is only `rows`/`loading`/`fetching`). Both were promises no data source could keep — the `matrx-user/canvas` failure mode — so they are gone rather than faked, and `task_row_count`'s description now says it is the RAW server count that the table's own filters narrow without changing. **(4) Completeness sweep on Scanner health.** The poll returns eleven fields and the manifest declared four, so `scanner_started_at`, `scanner_last_tick_duration_ms`, `scanner_last_tick_claimed`, `scanner_last_tick_manual_claimed`, `scanner_last_tick_expired_sweeps`, `scanner_total_runs_dispatched`, `scanner_error_message` and `scanner_unreachable_error` were added (the last two are DIFFERENT facts — one is an error the scanner reported, the other is the status poll itself failing). `overview_load_error` and `cron_expression_human` likewise. All verified live: a run on `/scanner-health` returned all ten scanner values off the real aidream poll. **`cron_preview_count` was deliberately NOT added back** — the entry below records that "Show next N" was excluded on purpose, and re-declaring a field a merged author excluded with a written reason is clobbering, not completeness. **(5) The console is NOT a fleet view, and now says so.** `scheduler.sch_task`/`sch_run` carry only std_select policies with no live admin clause (`FOUND_DEFECTS` D140, called out in `tasks/page.tsx`'s own header), so every count is the VIEWER'S OWN scheduled work. The manifest read "cross-user, platform-wide" throughout; an agent reasoning about platform health off those numbers would have been wrong, so the vocabulary now says "visible to the viewer" and the intro says it in one loud paragraph. Also corrected: the docblock claimed no `ui_surface` row existed — it does (verified against `ui.ui_surface`); only the VALUE rows still need a sync. **(6) Collision, handled the way the skill says.** A complete competing implementation merged to main mid-verification, on the same branch name. It won: this branch was reset onto it and the parallel version discarded rather than reconciled, and everything above is a delta on top of the landed design. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3584 values) and `type-check` clean; DB value sync still pending, which the `readinessNote` states.
