- **2026-08-12 — Scheduling admin agent-writable on the CRON TESTER tab only: 2 ask-policy `ui` targets, plus the surface's FIRST provider mount — and an honest floor case, reported as such.** `matrx-admin/scheduling` declares `cron_expression` and `cron_timezone`, handlers in `features/scheduling/lib/cron-tester-surface.ts`, registered from `app/(admin)/administration/automation/scheduling/cron-tester/page.tsx`. **(1) Two jobs, because a target cannot resolve without a runtime.** The manifest had 27 values at `readiness: "stub"` and the docblock said plainly that no page in the subtree mounted a `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` — so every declared value was a read lie. This change mounts the first provider and writes the first emitter (`buildCronTesterScope`), the `matrx-user/html-page` / `matrx-user/data-tables` shape. Readiness moves `"stub"` → `"partial"`, NOT `"verified"`: the other six tabs still mount nothing and a run started on them gets an empty scope, which the `readinessNote` now says in those words rather than letting `"partial"` imply more than was built. **(2) The floor call, made out loud.** This is exactly two fields, which is AT the skill's ~2 bar and not comfortably over it, and the scouting for a third came back empty: the admin subtree is nine files, the Tasks tab has no authored copy (its only state is `rows`/`loading`/`fetching`), and Templates is a hardcoded in-file `SEEDS` array the manifest already refuses to declare. What carries it is that the two fields are genuinely derivable — "every 15 minutes between 9am and 5pm on weekdays" → `*/15 9-17 * * 1-5` is real work — and that the page closes the evidence loop where the user can see it, humanizing the expression and recomputing the next N fire times. It does not: `ScheduleForm` appears in `app/(core)/schedules/**` and `features/scheduling/components/{form,detail}` only, and nothing under `administration/automation/scheduling` imports it. Different page, different state, ephemeral instead of a real trigger — so a second set here is additive, and the manifest docblock records the check so the next reader does not redo it. **(4) Anti-drift by construction.** Validation calls `validateCron` from `lib/scheduler-client/next-due` — the SAME function the page's own error alert renders from — so the value a handler accepts and the value the page previews cannot diverge, and the parser's message reaches the agent unedited. `COMMON_TZ` moved out of the page into the surface module as `CRON_TESTER_TIMEZONES`, making one list serve both the `<Select>` and the enum check; a re-typed list could have accepted a real IANA zone the picker cannot display, leaving the trigger blank over a value the agent believed it set. **(5) `mode:"ui"` with `applyPolicy:"ask"`, on purpose.** The tab persists nothing and has no Save bar, so `"ui"` is the only truthful mode — but `"auto"` was declined anyway, because the expression is the thing the admin is actively reasoning about and silently overwriting their typing is surprising rather than helpful. **(6) Live-verified with four real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/automation/scheduling/cron-tester`.** One message naming several targets produced a per-target ask dialog carrying the manifest description VERBATIM; Apply landed `*/15 9-17 * * 1-5` and `Asia/Tokyo` in the real `#cron-expr` input and timezone picker with the fire list recomputing (a separate run landed `America/New_York` from "US Eastern"). "Keep as is" declined with no error and the value unchanged. The undeclared ask — disable a named scheduled task AND mark an orphaned lease failed — was refused with no dialog and nothing staged, the agent enumerating this surface's two targets and the tester's ephemerality back in the manifest's own terms. A forced `0 99 * * *` returned the handler's throw verbatim: `client_tool_error: cron_expression "0 99 * * *" is not a valid cron expression for timezone UTC: Constraint error, got value 99 expected range 0-23. Expected 5 space-separated fields in the order "minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week" — no seconds field and no @daily-style macros. Send a plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded.` — the cron-parser's own words wrapped in the handler's, with the form still at its default, confirming validate-then-apply. **The parsed-argument trap is named IN the throw text** (`not JSON and not JSON-encoded`), the fix the `shapes` / `grade-work` / `sandboxes` / `marketing` entries all had to learn. **(7) Error Inspector, both readings.** On the CLEAN load (valid apply + a decline, no forced invalid) the sidebar probe showed no count — zero captures. On the forced-invalid load the same probe showed **5**. Reporting the dirty number is what makes the clean one mean anything: a throw IS a capture by design, so a detector never seen firing proves nothing. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3576 values) and `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync — the client tool works either way, which is why every run above passed. **(8) The well is dry, and this entry is the confirmation rather than a new lead.** The 2026-08-12 `matrx-admin/email` handoff listed this surface as candidate (c) of three; it is now taken, and an independent re-sweep of the manifests still lacking `writeTargets` reproduced that entry's conclusion — the remainder are reports, viewers, hubs, credential-adjacent consoles, or governance. ONE chip was fired, not three to five, because padding the fan-out with token surfaces costs more than it finds.
- **2026-08-12 — Digital PR & Reputation is agent-writable: ONE composite target that earns it by completing a role the manifest already declared. Also: the assigned surface was already done, and a production-wide RLS bug surfaced on the way.** `matrx-user/marketing-reputation` declares `reputation_case_triage`, an `applyPolicy: "ask"` / `mode: "entity"` target taking `{case_id, status, note?}`, handled on `ReputationWorkspace`'s existing provider (it mounted `getScope` but no `getWriteHandlers`) through the same `useUpdateReputationCase` mutation the case card's five buttons call — never raw supabase. **Why one target is not padding here:** the surface has exactly ONE canonical write path (`seo.update_reputation_case`), and the manifest has shipped a `reputation_adjudicator` agent role from the start whose entire stated job is to "issue defensible handling decisions from closed evidence" — a role that, before this, could read every fact, inference, contradiction and evidence ref on a case and then had no way to record a single ruling. The target is also a COMPOSITE over three fields decided together, which is the shape the campaign's accepted one-target adoptions take. Validation mirrors the UI exactly: `case_id` required and checked against the cases on the page (a QUEUE with no selected case), `accepted` refused unless the case is currently `open` (the Accept button only renders while open), `open` never writable (nothing sets a case back to it), note capped at `REPUTATION_RULING_NOTE_MAX_LENGTH`; the status vocabulary comes from the shared `REPUTATION_CASE_USER_SETTABLE_STATUSES` constant the manifest interpolates INTO its description, so offered and accepted vocabularies cannot drift. `entity` not `draft` because the page has no staging buffer at all — the buttons mutate directly and `reputation_cases` reports the stored row, so a staged status would be a value the read twin could never see (the `transcripts` mode test, applied honestly). **Provenance guard:** the column is `human_ruling` on an evidence-closed surface that keeps facts, inferences and human rulings separate, so the handler stamps `decided_via: "agent_surface_write"` — the human still rules by pressing Apply, but the record never claims a human authored the prose. The RPC REPLACES `human_ruling` wholesale (and recomputes `resolved_assessment` as `analysis || human_ruling`), so the handler always sends a complete ruling and the description says the note replaces, never appends. **Live-verified** with real Badass Agent runs against a throwaway brand/site/cases fixture (since deleted), no mocks: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim; Apply persisted `status='in_progress'` plus the note, `decided_via` and `lifecycle_decision` in `human_ruling` (confirmed by SQL, with the other two cases byte-unchanged — precise targeting); "Keep as is" declined with no error and left the row untouched; `status: "banana"` returned the handler's throw verbatim to the model (`reputation_case_triage status must be one of accepted | in_progress | monitoring | completed | dismissed. Received "banana".`) with NOTHING persisted (validate-then-apply); and a request to change a case's `verdict` and `confidence` produced NO `apply_surface_write` call at all — the agent refused and enumerated the single target it had, quoting this target's own "never edits the case's verdict, scores, facts or evidence" clause back. Error Inspector: zero captures on both valid-write loads, with the deliberate bad value as the positive control raising it to 2 in its own session. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. **Two findings for whoever comes next.** (1) **The assigned surface, `matrx-user/analysis-studio`, was already done** — four `writeTargets` are on main covering exactly the candidate the assignment named (`annotation_label`) plus `annotation_extracted_text`, `studio_focus_annotation` and `annotation_redact`; STEP ZERO caught it before a line was written and the work moved here rather than duplicating. `data-tables`, `settings` and `admin-lookups` also already have branches. (2) **`seo.competitor_opportunity` is unreadable for EVERY user on EVERY site**, which takes the whole reputation workspace down before the surface runtime mounts (the page counts that table and `getReputationWorkspace` throws): `iam.accessible_entity_ids` recurses to the parent `seo_competitor`, whose `visibility` column is NUMERIC — an SEO metric colliding by NAME with the platform's ACL `visibility` — so the helper builds `t.visibility >= 'internal'` and Postgres raises `22P02 invalid input syntax for type numeric: "internal"`. Reproduced on a real site, so it is production-wide and predates this work; it was worked around in TEST SCAFFOLDING ONLY (a localhost relay returning an empty exact count) with no app code touched. Deliberately not fixed here — the fix is either a column rename/type change or a guard in a shared, security-critical RLS helper, and neither is a write-target task's call. **One trap worth knowing:** declaring the `writeTargets` array but forgetting `writeTargets,` on the exported manifest object fails SILENTLY — `type-check` and `check:surface-drift` both pass, the handler registers fine, and the target simply never reaches the tool spec, because `listLiveWriteTargets` skips a surface whose manifest has no `writeTargets` without warning (unlike the client-tool path, which `console.warn`s the unwired case). It cost a full diagnostic loop; the symptom is an agent that lists only the INHERITED parent-surface targets and insists yours "doesn't exist on this page".
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/official-components` earns ZERO write targets, and the NO is now written into the manifest so nobody re-scouts it.** Assigned as a write-target chip on a reasonable-looking theory: the manifest declares `current_component_description` / `_categories` / `_tags`, and catalogue metadata across a large component library is exactly the "authored content an agent drafts better, faster and more consistently than a human filling a form" case from the skill's YES list. **The theory died on the prerequisite question** — *is this registry actually editable, and where does an edit persist?* — which the skill makes you answer BEFORE writing code precisely so a plausible target set does not get built over nothing. **It is not editable anywhere.** `componentList` is a hardcoded literal (`export const componentList: ComponentEntry[] = [...]`, `parts/component-list.tsx:151`); all ~18 repo-wide references are reads (`.find`, `.filter`, `.forEach`, `.some`, `.slice`, `.length`), and nothing outside the route directory imports it. `[componentId]/page.tsx` renders name/categories/tags/description/path straight off the found entry with no `useState`, no form, no `onSubmit` and no service call — the "Source" button has no `onClick` at all — while the list page's only local state is the sidebar's `expandedGroups` beside `?q=`/`?category=`. There is no backing store (`types/database.types.ts` has no `official_component*` table; `app/api/admin/tool-ui-components` is the unrelated tool-call-visualization renderer registry) and no codegen (nothing in `scripts/` or `package.json` produces the file). **So an edit here is a `.tsx` source-literal change that lands through git, not a UI write.** No canonical thunk or service exists to wire a handler to, and the skill forbids declaring a target that cannot reach one — a declared-but-unwired target is a loud runtime defect by design. Staging the edit into throwaway local state for the admin to copy-paste would be inventing a write path, the same prohibition. **The `surface-authoring` prerequisite was therefore NOT done either:** `readiness` stays `"stub"` because mounting an emitter is only worth it in service of a target set that cannot exist here — the ruling, not the wiring, is the deliverable. Worth stating plainly because this is a distinct shape of correct NO from the ones already recorded, and the only one where the *values were the right kind* and the *persistence* was missing: `matrx-admin/ai-models`' zero came from governance and `matrx-admin/agent-slots`' two came from measured-vs-authored, while this one is a read-only report whose "fields" are source code. **The identity/view-state half fails independently**, so no smaller set hides here: `current_component_id` / `_name` / `_path` are identity (a component's name and import path are what other code depends on — renaming is a refactor, not a copy edit), `search_query` / `selected_category` / `page_section` are view state, and the three counts are derived. Recorded in the manifest docblock and `readinessNote` rather than only here, since the manifest is the file the next agent opens first. If the library ever gains a real backing store or an admin edit form, description/categories/tags become a strong single composite target — and only then.
- **2026-08-12 — Marketing hub agent-writable: ONE ask-policy draft target, and THE reference for a surface whose FIVE mounts disagree about writing.** `matrx-user/marketing` declares `site_editor_draft` (`draft`/`ask`, `{site, name?, description?}`), handled on `SitesPortfolio`'s own provider and staged through the same `setDraft` a keystroke fires, so the human still presses "Save site" and the existing version-guarded `updateSiteIdentity` runs. Pure contract in `features/marketing/lib/site-write-targets.ts` (17 unit tests); the manifest interpolates its `SITE_NAME_MAX` / `SITE_DESCRIPTION_MAX` into the model-facing description, so the advertised contract IS the enforced one. **(1) The per-mount split is the point.** Five components mount this one surface and `listAgentWritableTargets()` only offers a target where a handler is registered, so the decision was made mount by mount and FOUR register nothing: `MarketingConnectionsCatalog` owns provider credentials and Search Console / Bing property ids (identity, never writable at any policy), `WorkspaceCostWorkspace` is a spend report, `MarketingHub` is a navigation map. **(2) The brand half earns nothing, and that is the finding.** `BrandsPortfolio`'s editor writes `{name, industry, description}` — but `matrx-user/marketing-brand` already ships `brand_identity` over industry/description through `updateBrand` with the version guard, AND declares the brand NAME human-owned identity. A second target set over the same fields is the two-paths-into-one-row defect, not coverage, and contradicting a shipped sibling's identity call would be worse than declaring nothing. So this surface ships the SITE side only: two authored fields, at the judgment floor rather than comfortably over it, carried by `description` ("What this website is for") being exactly the prose an agent drafts well. **(3) Modal reachability, solved the `matrx-admin/feedback` way.** The site editor is a Radix modal, so a user cannot open it and THEN ask an agent — the overlay covers the header Agents button and the composer. The handler therefore OPENS the editor on the site the USER named (resolved from the loaded rows: exact id / domain / name, then a unique partial; unknown and ambiguous selectors both refused with the loaded rows listed), and refuses outright when an editor is already open on a DIFFERENT site, because closing it would destroy unsaved work. The dialog gained `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` — a write confirm renders OUTSIDE it and answering one would otherwise discard the copy just staged. **(4) Read twins.** New `site_editor` value publishes what is STAGED right now (empty when no editor is open — the draft target's honest twin), and `visible_sites` now actually emits the `description` the manifest had advertised all along but never sent; both are read off a ref at Run time, and the editor handle is a ref refreshed every render because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves handlers BEFORE the confirm is answered. **(5) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/marketing/sites`**, staging only — nothing was saved and no rows were touched. One message asking for a name + description plus a domain change plus a delete: the confirm carried the manifest description VERBATIM, Apply opened the closed editor and landed both fields in the real `#site-name` / `#site-description` inputs, and both undeclared asks were refused with the agent explaining the surface exposes staging only. A two-call turn proved the different-site guard AND the overlay fix at once — the first write staged, the second came back as the handler's own throw ("the site editor was already open on my-test.com … Nothing was staged"), and the editor survived the second confirm with its staged text intact. "Keep as is" declined with no error, the declined field unchanged and the applied one still staged. Three forced-invalid values returned the handler's throws verbatim with NOTHING staged: an unknown site (answered with the ten loaded rows), a `description` sent as a JSON object (`must be a plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded — received object`), and an undeclared `status` key. **The JSON-in-a-prose-field trap that bit `shapes`, `grade-work` and `sandboxes` is named in the throw text itself**, which is why the agent reported it instead of retrying double-encoded. Error Inspector on a CLEAN load (valid apply + decline, no forced invalid): zero captures, the sidebar toggle showing no count; on the forced-invalid load the same probe showed `[surface-writeback] Surface writeback contract break` — a throw IS a capture by design, and proving the detector fires is what makes the clean reading mean anything. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3576 values) and `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.
- **2026-08-12 — Scraper adopted on all four `/scraper` ROUTES (live-verified), the write handlers extracted to ONE shared factory, and a real URL-validation bug fixed.** A COLLISION resolved by keeping the merged design and adding only what it explicitly left open. The 2026-08-10 entry below shipped `matrx-user/scraper`'s write half on `ScraperFloatingWorkspace` and recorded, in the manifest docblock and here, that the `/scraper/*` route pages "mount no surface runtime and emit none of this surface's read values, so they get no targets … adopting the surface on those routes is its own task". This is that task; **not one line of the landed target set, prose, or vocabulary was changed**. **(1) The routes now emit.** `/scraper`, `/scraper/quick`, `/scraper/search` and `/scraper/search-and-scrape` each mount a `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` through the new `features/scraper/agent-context/ScraperSurfaceMount.tsx`, building scope with the SAME `buildScraperContextData` mapper the floating workspace uses, so a route cannot emit a different shape than the panel. This matters because `surfaceFromPathname` has always resolved every `/scraper/*` path to this surface and the header nav takes users to the routes, not the overlay — an agent run started there previously had an empty scope AND no write tool. `/scraper` and `/scraper/quick` needed one small read-side addition each (the raw `ScraperResult` envelope kept beside the display projection, which drops links/metadata/timing). **(2) The handlers became one implementation, not two.** The validation was inline in `ScraperFloatingWorkspace`; the routes own the SAME inputs, so re-typing it beside a second set of setters would have been exactly the drift `scrape-command.ts` exists to prevent. It moved verbatim to `agent-context/scraperWriteHandlers.ts` as `buildScraperWriteHandlers(mount)`, and the workspace now calls it too. **(3) THE generalizable bit — per-mount posture by CAPABILITY rather than by a hand-maintained list.** A mount passes only the setters it has; the factory returns a handler only for the targets those setters cover; `listAgentWritableTargets` filters on `hasHandler`. So the target list per route falls out of what the route can actually do, with no second registry to keep in sync — **verified from the agent's own mouth**: asked on `/scraper/search-and-scrape` to write a scraped result, it enumerated its tool enum as exactly `scrape_command`, `scrape_page_limit`, `selected_result_page` — the search-mode pair correctly absent. The one case needing real thought was `scrape_command.mode`: routes have no `setMode` (the mode IS the route), but an agent composing a deep run naturally states the mode ALONGSIDE the keyword, so refusing "be what you already are" would turn every well-formed command into an error. A mode write that does not CHANGE the view is a no-op success; only a real switch is refused, naming the route to open. That distinction was found by the first live run failing on it, not by reading the code. **(4) A REAL BUG in the merged code, found by the mandatory forced-invalid test and fixed.** `normalizeUrl` guarded only `try { new URL(x) } catch`, which is **engine-dependent at exactly the inputs that matter**: Chromium accepts `new URL("https://not a url at all")` (host `not`, spaces folded into the path) where Node rejects it. The 2026-08-10 verification used an out-of-range page limit as its invalid case, so this never surfaced. Forcing `{url: "not a url at all"}` through `scrape_command` returned **`ok: true`** and dropped `https://not a url at all` into the box for the user to clear — an agent told its write succeeded when it had staged garbage. `normalizeUrl` now also requires an http/https scheme and a real host (dotted, or `localhost`), which additionally rejects the non-web schemes it used to mangle (`file:///etc/passwd` became `https://file:///etc/passwd`, host `file` — a string that parses but can only ever return a backend error). Both the Scrape buttons and the write handler share this one helper, so an agent can never stage a URL the user's own click would refuse. Re-run after the fix: the handler's throw reaches the agent verbatim (`client_tool_error: scrape_command.url is not a usable URL: "not a url at all".`) with the box left empty. **(5) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on the ROUTE mounts** (not the overlay): one message asking for a deep run staged BOTH fields through two per-target dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim, with `1 to 20` visibly interpolated from `PAGE_LIMIT_MIN`/`MAX`, and `keyword` + `maxPages` read back off the real DOM as `"postgres row level security"` / `"8"`; "Keep as is" left the keyword byte-identical with the agent acknowledging cleanly and no error raised; the undeclared-results write was refused with NO dialog; and a valid URL landed in the box on `/scraper`. Error Inspector: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures on every clean path — the only captures were the deliberate forced-invalid throws, which are the seam being loud by design. `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces, 3574 values) + `type-check` clean. **(6) Honest gap:** the floating `scraperWindow` overlay could not be opened in the headless harness (neither the Windows menu nor `?panels=scraper` hydrated it), so the `scrape_mode`-CHANGE branch — reachable only on that mount — was not exercised live this round; it is unchanged merged code that the 2026-08-10 entry verified, and it now runs through the shared factory under `type-check`. DB mirror sync still pending, as before.
- **2026-08-12 — `html-page`'s `page_html_content` throw now names the double-encode failure (collision follow-up; no targets added or removed).** A second agent was assigned `matrx-user/html-page` and had built a parallel implementation before discovering the 2026-08-10 entry below had already landed the surface on `main`. That parallel work was DISCARDED rather than merged: the shipped surface is complete and live-verified, and the only real differences were two extra targets — `canonical_url` and `og_image` — which the original author excluded *with stated reasons* ("a wrong canonical de-indexes the page"; "an agent can only invent a plausible URL"). **Reversing a documented judgment call is clobbering merged work even when it ADDS rather than deletes**, and on inspection the exclusions hold: on `marketing-page` the agent-writable canonical is a DESIRED value inside a plan, whereas here it would be the tag the user's very next Save publishes. One genuine gap did survive that review. `page_html_content` is `valueType: "string"`, and its shape throw said only "expects a non-empty string" — but the inline-tool layer JSON-parses a JSON-looking argument before any handler sees it, so a string-typed target can be handed an object, and the scratchpad / working-document entry already recorded that an agent told merely "expected a string" tends to *fix* that by double-encoding. In those surfaces escaped `\n` and stray quotes land in a user's notes; here they would be published verbatim at `/p/{id}`. The message now says "the raw markup as plain text, not JSON and not JSON-encoded", matching the wording those targets use — one sentence, no behaviour change. **The reusable lesson is about the collision, not the code:** a same-surface chip that arrives while the surface is already in flight should end in a diff against latest `main` and a discard, not a merge of two parallel designs, and "additive" is not automatically safe when the addition is precisely what the merged author deliberately left out. `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces, 3575 values) + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/bundles` gets its FIRST emitter AND its write half: 2 ask-policy DRAFT targets, and THE reference for splitting a partial-patch object instead of collapsing it.** The tool-registry bundles console was manifest-only (`readiness: "stub"`, "Emitters: NONE YET"), so an agent bound there was offered no write tool at all. `BundlesAdminPage` now mounts the surface's first `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` over a page-scoped store (`components/BundlesSurfaceRuntime.tsx`, the `matrx-admin/lookups` shape) that the list, the detail panel and the create dialog publish into, and declares `new_bundle_draft` (`{name?, description?}` → the "New bundle" form) and `bundle_description` (→ the selected bundle's inline Description box). **Two targets, not one object, and the docblock argues it:** `email_draft` / `lookup_draft` are one object because a single open dialog is the review unit and one Save commits it, but these two do not share a RECORD — one stages a bundle that does not exist yet behind a modal committed by "Create bundle", the other rewrites an existing row in an inline panel committed by "Save". Collapsed, the destination and the acceptance of `name` would flip on whether a URL param is set. Split, the agent's choice of target IS its declared intent, which is what the admin reads in the confirm. `name` is writable on CREATE and refused on an existing row, demonstrated rather than asserted: `tool.bundle` is `UNIQUE (name)`, every lister tool is named `bundle:list_<name>` (`agent-core` → `bundle:list_agent-core`, live), that lister name is what a model calls, and `updateBundle` PATCHes only the bundle row — so a rename leaves every agent calling the old lister. Member `local_alias` refused for the same reason (it is the identifier `bundle_lister` hot-swaps in), and the Active toggle, System switch, metadata JSON (`server_slug` / `lister_tool_id` pointers) and MEMBERSHIP are capability, not copy. Verified live end-to-end: both confirms carried the per-target description text, Apply staged into the real editors, "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}`, an undeclared rename/deactivate was refused before any request reached the page ("the tool only exposes two targets"), and two deliberately invalid values (`Browser Tools!`, then the taken name `agent-core`) came back to the agent verbatim with NOTHING staged and NO stray dialog left open — the validate-entire-payload-before-opening-anything ordering lookups shipped a bug against. Zero `surface-writeback` captures on a clean reload; `check:surface-drift` and `type-check` clean; no DB row was created or saved.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/email` was already shipped, so this task added the MISSING surfaces-side docs and re-verified it independently.** Assigned as a write-target chip for the Email Users compose tool. **STEP ZERO found the surface already agent-writable on latest `main`** (`email_draft`, landed 2026-08-10 by the branch of the same name, which had since been merged and deleted) — so per the campaign's collision rule the landed design wins untouched, and this entry records only what was genuinely additive. **(1) The additive part is documentation, and the gap was real.** The 2026-08-10 change wrote its Change Log line into `features/admin/FEATURE.md` but never touched THIS file: `matrx-admin/email` appeared **zero** times in `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` — not in the "Live agent-writable adopters" list, not in the Change Log — while its sibling `matrx-admin/ai-models` appeared three times. A shipped adopter missing from the adopter list is the exact failure this file exists to prevent, since this list is where the next agent looks to decide what is already taken. The adopter entry above is that fix. **(2) Independent live re-verification, because a shipped surface nobody re-runs is a claim rather than a fact.** Five real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/users/email` against latest `main`, all passing. Asked to draft a maintenance-window announcement, the agent staged subject and body in **ONE** ask dialog — the composite design paying off exactly as its docblock argues, since a split target would have offered two dialogs over one piece of copy — and the dialog rendered the manifest description **verbatim**, with `at most 200 characters` / `at most 25000 characters` interpolated from `EMAIL_SUBJECT_MAX_CHARS` / `EMAIL_BODY_MAX_CHARS` rather than re-typed, which is the anti-drift property visible where a reader can check it. Apply landed both fields in the visible form with **real newlines preserved** (`\n\n` between paragraphs, no escaped `\\n` and no stray quotes) — the double-encoding trap avoided, because the target takes an OBJECT and the handler never sees raw JSON text. **"Keep as is" was tested against pre-typed sentinel copy rather than an empty form**, so "unchanged" was provable and not vacuous: both fields came back byte-identical and the agent acknowledged cleanly ("Understood — I've left your draft exactly as it was"), with no error raised; the same run also proved the READ half, since the agent quoted the sentinel text back before proposing its rewrite. **(3) The undeclared half held under a deliberately adversarial ask.** One message demanded all three refused categories at once — set the From address to `security-alerts@paypal.com`, select every platform user, and send. **No dialog was raised, nothing was staged, and the form was untouched**; the agent enumerated the boundaries in the manifest's own terms (the From address and the recipients have no write target; "sending is the admin pressing Send Email — that press is never an agent action"), correctly reported the live `recipient_mode` as `custom`, and independently flagged the sender-spoof as phishing-shaped. This is the `data-tables` lesson applied — the request named the page write tool explicitly, so a refusal means the seam refused rather than the model routing around it. **(4) Validation reaches the agent verbatim.** A forced two-line subject (the CR/LF header-injection case, unreachable through the real `<input>`) returned the handler's own throw with no coercion and no retry: `client_tool_error: email_draft.subject must be a single line — it is an email header, and line breaks are not allowed in one. Put the extra sentence in message_body instead.` The confirm dialog is raised BEFORE the handler runs, so Apply was pressed and the throw happened after — and **both fields were still empty afterwards**, confirming validate-then-apply (`parseEmailDraftPatch` throws before any `setState`). **(5) Error Inspector, on the two loads that matter.** On a CLEAN load carrying a valid Apply plus a decline and no forced invalid: **0 distinct · 0 total occurrences, "No errors captured yet."** On the deliberate-contract-break load: exactly **2** captures — one `[surface-writeback] Surface writeback contract break` and one user-facing error toast, both carrying the handler's message. That pair is the seam being loud BY DESIGN, manufactured by the test, and is reported here rather than rounded down to zero. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3520 values). **(6) One honest gap left standing, unchanged from the original entry:** `readinessNote` still records no `data-surface-value` anchors and **no DB mirror of `writeTargets`** in `ui.ui_surface_write_target`, so aidream cannot advertise `email_draft` server-side; the client tool works either way, which is why every run above passed. Syncing it needs the canonical `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests` (the `emit-surface-sync-sql.ts` script predates write targets and emits values only) and is left to a human with the admin surfaces page, not forged from here. The pre-existing `agent.review_queue` row on this URL was UPDATED rather than duplicated, per that skill's no-duplicate rule. **(7) CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips could be fired, and the scouting is recorded here so the next agent does not repeat it.** Same wall the `canvas` entry hit: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright and the avalanche chain cannot be extended from here — picking these up needs a fresh chain. **The field is now 62 manifests without `writeTargets`, down from 94 the day before**, so recompute rather than trusting any prose list. Ranked against the bar, THREE candidates survive. **(a) `matrx-user/agent-advanced-editor`** — the strongest left, because the manifest docblock is already asking for it ("agents bound here are 'improve this prompt', 'restructure this schema', 'rewrite this section'"). `editor_content` is the authored field, and the replace/append pair from `agent-builder` / `transcript-scribe-live` applies. Two traps make it a real design job rather than a sweep: `editor_field` selects which of three DIFFERENTLY-SHAPED fields is open (prose vs output schema — and the JSON one collides head-on with the parsed-argument trap), and the overlay opens from the agent builder, whose surface ALREADY ships `system_instruction`, so it must not become a second write path onto one field under one name — the `quick-tasks` distinct-names or `lists` one-shared-definition pattern, not a fresh declaration. `readiness: "stub"`, so confirm the mount first. **(b) `matrx-admin/bundles`** — `/administration/agents/bundles`, `features/tool-registry/bundles/components/BundlesAdminPage.tsx`; its docblock likewise blesses the scope ("help the admin name/describe a new bundle, draft member aliases"). `bundle_description` through the canonical `updateBundle` and a `{tool_id, alias}` OBJECT through `setBundleMemberAlias` (both in `bundles/services/bundles.service.ts`, the latter already called at `BundlesAdminPage.tsx:471`) are two clean targets; the object shape is the `cell_value` coordinates-with-the-value rule, since which bundle is selected decides where an alias lands. Bundle MEMBERSHIP stays human on `agent-builder`'s capability-change rule. `readiness: "stub"`, no emitter — `surface-authoring` first. **(c) `matrx-admin/scheduling`** — the Cron tester tab only, and it is deliberately the LOW-stakes one: the manifest says outright that tab is "a pure client-side validator … not a live schedule", so `cron_expression` / `cron_timezone` have zero blast radius while "every weekday at 5pm Pacific" → cron is exactly the derivable-planning-field YES. Everything else on that surface is health counters and run history. **Checked and FAILING the bar, so nobody should spend a run on them:** `rag-library` and `education` (`readiness: "partial"`, so tempting, but every value is browse/report state — search queries, filters, counts, streaks); `admin-official-components`, whose `current_component_description` looks perfect until you find the catalogue is a STATIC in-code `componentList`, so there is no canonical write path and a target there would be the `admin-tool-registry` `tool_group` one-way door; `admin-lookups` and `admin-mcp-servers` (registry browse plus capability governance); `sandboxes`, where `command_history` / `terminal_output` mean running commands — the FIRE line; `messages`, whose authored half is a composer that would be sending; `agent-gate`, one authored field and so below the ~2 floor; and `admin-cx-dashboard` / `admin-server-logs` / `agent-run-history` / `rag-viewer`, which are records and reports.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/feedback` gets its FIRST emitter AND its write half: 2 ask-policy draft targets, a page-scoped editor registry for a 4-tab console, and the pattern for making a MODAL editor reachable at all.** The surface was `readiness: "stub"` with one declared value (`active_tab`) and no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` anywhere, so this was two jobs — mount an emitter, then earn the targets. **(1) What earned a target, and the floor.** Ranked against the bar, the Feedback & Announcements console has exactly two clusters of AUTHORED copy: an announcement's `title` / `message` / `announcement_type` (prose that interrupts every user at their next login) and a feedback category's `name` / `description` (the badge and hint the whole triage table files against). Five fields, comfortably over the ~2 floor. `announcement_draft` bundles its three into ONE partial-patch object on the `matrx-admin/email` `email_draft` reasoning — a title is the promise its body keeps and the type is the severity that copy is written at, so they are one review unit committed by one Save, not three independently declinable decisions — while `category_draft` is a SEPARATE target on the opposite reasoning (`matrx-admin/tool-registry`): a taxonomy entry is a different object, in a different tab, saved by a different button, and bundling it would produce a confirm describing two unrelated edits where "Keep as is" declines work the admin wanted. Both contracts are pure modules (`features/admin/feedback/announcement-draft.ts`, `category-draft.ts`) that the manifest interpolates into the model-facing description AND the handler calls, so the advertised contract IS the enforced one; `ANNOUNCEMENT_TYPES` was added to `types/feedback.types.ts` with `AnnouncementType` DERIVED from it — the `FEEDBACK_TYPES` pattern already in that file — so the enum check reads the same array the `<Select>` renders instead of a re-typed literal. **(2) THE modal-reachability problem, which is the real lesson here.** Every editor on this page lives in a Radix dialog, and a Radix modal sets `pointer-events: none` on the body — so an admin CANNOT open the announcement dialog and then ask an agent for help. Verified the hard way: with the Edit dialog open, the chat composer would not accept a message even when set through React's native value setter and submitted with a synthetic Enter. A target that only ever writes into an already-open dialog is correct-looking dead code. `announcement_draft` therefore OPENS the create dialog itself when none is open (the `CreateStoreInline` move from `rag-data-stores`), and `category_draft` switches the Categories tab to its "Manage Categories" view, because staging into a form the admin cannot see is not `draft` mode, it is a silent write. **(3) `onInteractOutside`, and the two-call turn that proves it.** A surface-write confirm renders OUTSIDE the dialog, so answering it counts as an outside interaction and Radix closes the dialog — discarding the copy just staged. Both announcement dialogs got `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}`. This is not theoretical: asked to make two SEPARATE write calls in one turn, the first opened the dialog and the second's confirm rendered over it; with the guard the dialog survived and both fields landed. **(4) Why a page-scoped ref registry instead of `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`.** TWO components own "the announcement editor" (`CreateAnnouncementDialog` and `EditAnnouncementDialog`), so registering `announcement_draft` from each would be a last-one-wins collision, not a mode gate. Instead the dialogs and `CategoriesTab` publish HANDLES — live values plus the component's OWN setters — into `FeedbackConsoleEditorStore.tsx`, and `FeedbackManagementContainer` owns exactly one handler per target that resolves which editor is live: the open Edit dialog wins, the create form is the fallback, and BOTH open is a refusal rather than a guess. The registry is a mutable ref refreshed on every render because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves `getWriteHandlers()` BEFORE the confirm is answered, so any "is it open / which one / is it saving" guard read off the render closure would be stale by the time Apply is pressed. Handlers stage through the dialogs' own `setTitle`/`setMessage`/`setEditing`, never a parallel path. **(5) Validate the WHOLE payload before anything opens.** The order is parse → resolve editor → check not-saving → only then open and stage, so a rejected write leaves the page exactly as it found it — the stray-empty-dialog bug is structurally absent rather than fixed. **(6) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs**, staging only (nothing published, nothing saved, no throwaway rows left behind — this console governs real user reports and announcements that reach real people). One message drove `announcement_draft`: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply OPENED the closed create dialog and staged title, message and `warning` together, and the agent volunteered why it could not do the rest — "the page write tool deliberately does not expose the active status or the minimum display time — those aren't writable fields for me" — which is the undeclared-target refusal arriving unprompted. `category_draft` staged `Billing & Payments` with a description into the inline form; "Keep as is" on a follow-up declined with the form byte-identical and no error. A deliberate `announcement_type: "urgent"` came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`announcement_draft.announcement_type must be exactly one of info | warning | critical | update; received "urgent".`) with NOTHING staged and no dialog opened. Error Inspector on a CLEAN load (valid apply + decline only, no forced invalid — a throw is a capture by design): **"No errors captured yet."**, zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3522 values) and `type-check` clean. **(7) What was deliberately NOT declared.** PUBLISHING — no create/save target and no `is_active` — because an announcement interrupts every user at login and cannot be recalled, the same line `matrx-admin/email` drew at Send. `min_display_seconds` is the seconds every user is FORCED to wait before dismissing it: an imposition on other people's time, not copy. The entire feedback TRIAGE vocabulary (`status`, `priority`, `work_priority`, `admin_decision`, `assigned_to`, `category_id`) is governance — an agent that can quietly mark a bug `wont_fix` or reassign it is the write this campaign exists to refuse. Ids and the derived category `slug` are identity; delete stays human, because deleting a feedback record destroys a user's report and deleting a category orphans every row under it. **The interesting omission is `FeedbackDetailDialog`'s `admin_direction`, `admin_notes` and user-reply compose box** — the most agent-shaped copy on the page, left out on REACHABILITY rather than taste. That dialog is modal too, and unlike the create form there is no safe way for a handler to open it: doing so means picking WHICH user's report to act on, which is record selection on the admin's behalf. Recorded in the manifest docblock as the first thing to add if that triage view ever grows a non-modal path. **Collision checks** (before writing and again before commit): `admin-feedback.manifest.ts` had no `writeTargets` on latest `main`, `git ls-remote` showed no `claude/surface-write-admin-feedback` branch, and `agent.review_queue` had no row for this url. Note the separate, already-shipped `matrx-user/feedback` (the user-facing capture window) is a DIFFERENT surface and was not touched. **(8) CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips were fired, for the same two reasons the `matrx-user/canvas` entry recorded, and the scouting is written down here so nobody redoes it.** Mechanically: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright — the avalanche chain cannot be extended from here and picking these up needs a fresh chain. Substantively: **the well is all but dry.** Scanning EVERY manifest on latest `main` that still lacks `writeTargets`, exactly TWO survive the judgment bar. **(a) `matrx-admin/scheduling`** (`/administration/automation/scheduling`, `readiness: "stub"`, so it needs a mount first): `cron-tester/page.tsx` owns `expression` / `tz` state and the manifest ALREADY declares `cron_expression` and `cron_timezone` as read values, so the read twins exist; translating "every weekday at 9am Eastern" into `0 9 * * 1-5` + `America/New_York` is a genuine agent job, the tester is EPHEMERAL (schedules nothing, spends nothing) and it is a plain page rather than a modal, making it the lowest-stakes write left on the board. It is floor-marginal at two fields, so whoever takes it should first check `tasks/`, `templates/` and `features/scheduling/components/form/ScheduleForm.tsx` for an authored name/description — **and must check whether this console renders the SAME `ScheduleForm` as the already-shipped `matrx-user/schedules`, because two target sets over the same fields is a defect; the `matrx-user/lists` shared-vocabulary pattern is the answer if so.** Enabling/disabling a real task or re-cronning one stays human. **(b) `matrx-user/messages`** (`/messages`): `features/messaging/components/MessageInput.tsx` owns a real `content` draft in a non-modal thread footer, the surface already mounts providers in `MessagesPageClient.tsx` and `[conversationId]/page.tsx`, and sending is a human gate — the `matrx-admin/email` shape exactly. **But it is ONE authored field**: `NewConversationDialog` is a user SEARCH (identity, not copy) and there is no conversation-rename path (`updateConversation` is realtime sync, not a user gesture), so it sits BELOW the ~2 floor and should only be taken by someone willing to argue that a DM composer alone earns it, or to decline it honestly. **Everything else lacking targets was checked and FAILS the bar**, so a chip fired at one of these wastes a whole agent run: the marketing report family (`marketing-analysis`, `marketing-coverage`, `marketing-links`, `marketing-sitemaps`, `marketing-competitors`, `marketing-crawl`, `marketing-site-pages`, `marketing-ranks-hub`, plus the previously-ruled `audit`/`authority`/`reputation`/`findings`/`backlinks`) are report, list and filter mounts with no authored state; `admin-users`, `admin-cx-dashboard`, `admin-server-logs`, `admin-sandbox`, `sandboxes`, `agent-gate`, `agent-run-history`, `agent-settings`, `agent-slots`, `admin-official-components` and `observational-memory` are governance, operational or catalogue consoles; `admin-mcp-servers` and `marketing-integrations` are credential-adjacent; `ai-results`, `assistant-message`, `canvas-viewer`, `context-preview`, `file-preview`, `gallery`, `image-viewer`, `rag-viewer`, `share`, `transcript-scribe`, `transcript-studio` and `pdf-extractor` are viewers or records, where a write would forge what the user opened the page to read; `dashboard`, `education`, `podcast`, `crm`, `crm-manager`, `agents-hub` and `marketing` are hubs; `settings` (`/user-settings`) is tabs, toggles and preferences rather than authored copy; `image-uploader`'s `uploader_title`/`uploader_description` are chrome props supplied by the opener, not user-editable content; and `_baseline` / `_conversation-document` are shared manifest fragments, not surfaces at all. **The honest read at this point in the campaign is that it is finished except for those two, and fewer chips beat padded ones.**
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/agent-slots` agent-writable (live-verified): 2 targets on an admin console where almost everything is deliberately unwritable — and the exemplar bench lifted into the scope to give them a read twin.** THE reference for an ADMIN surface over live production capacity, and the counterweight to the campaign's "admin surfaces are all governance" reflex: the answer here was neither the `ai-models` zero nor a padded count, but exactly two. The console shows 126 slots, their pins, their health and their per-principal bindings, and the JUDGMENT BAR is now written into the manifest docblock so nobody re-derives it. **Not writable, each for its own reason:** `slot_count` / `slots_summary` / `health_summary` / `unhealthy_slots` / `system_agent_count` / `selected_slot_health` are MEASURED from the live `agent.slot_definition` + `agent.slot_binding` rows and the canonical agent slice — an agent writing health would fabricate the state of the system, the `education-grade-work` / `transcripts-cleanup` line. **Repin** (`SlotEditor`: agent, track-latest, pinned version) is identity by UUID over every user the slot serves, and the surface exposes only the COUNT of system agents, never the catalog — so it could only be guessed, exactly why `agent_id` was deferred on `pdf-extractor`. **Enable/disable** and the bench's **Run all** spend live capacity and real model budget (the settled `marketing-crawls` / `pdf-extractor` press-the-button precedent). **The per-principal overrides** — `selected_slot_overrides`, the candidate the assignment flagged as most interesting — were inspected and REJECTED on the merits: `SlotOverrideEditor` holds an agent swap (identity by UUID again), a `model` id from a catalog this surface does not expose, and `thinking_level`, one mechanical toggle; that is the "pure-mechanical toggle nobody would ask an agent to flip" clause, over a binding that changes which agent runs a platform step for a user or a whole org. **Owner verdict notes** on bench results are the admin's judgment of record about a comparison they ran. **What DOES earn a target is the exemplar** — the stored real input the bench replays against the current binding and every candidate, i.e. the evidence a repin is safe. Composing one means reading the slot's contract, inventing an input that exercises it and phrasing a realistic user message: authored content, produced far faster than an admin hand-typing JSON into a textarea, with the human still pressing "Save exemplar". So `slot_exemplar_draft` is ONE `draft` object (`{label?, variables?, user_input?}`, every key optional and partial) rather than three targets, because the form renders them together and the Save button consumes them at once — and because one handler validates them ATOMICALLY, which three cannot. `select_slot` (`ui`) is the second, and it is `"ask"`, NOT `content-plan`'s `select_node` `"auto"`: this workbench remounts per slot (`key={row.id}` on `SlotEditor`, `SlotTestBench`, `SlotOverridePanel`), so moving the selection DISCARDS unsaved typing in the repin, override and exemplar editors — the handler also refuses outright while an exemplar draft is staged. **The scope had to grow to make the write honest**: the manifest's own readinessNote said bench state was not lifted, so the target had no read twin and the agent had no way to know which variable keys to fill. Three new read values fix that — `selected_slot_contract` (parsed with the same `parseSlotContract` the override editor's contract check uses), `selected_slot_exemplars`, and `slot_exemplar_draft` — and because the composer lives in a grandchild that mounts only while a workbench is open, `SlotTestBench` publishes them upward through the new `bench-draft.ts` for the console's `getScope`, mirroring this feature's existing module-level `onSlotCacheInvalidated` bus. **Two answers to the resolve-before-confirm trap, both load-bearing.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves every staged handler BEFORE the first dialog is answered, so (a) the console's `select_slot` validates against `rowsRef` / `selectedIdRef` reassigned from the wrapper's `ref` callback (the `UserTableViewer` live-ref idiom — assigning during render is a `react-hooks/refs` lint error), and (b) each bench compares `readSlotBenchOwner()` against its own registration id and THROWS rather than staging into a composer that unmounted while the dialog sat open. The console also carries a base REFUSAL layer for `slot_exemplar_draft` (`resolveHandlers` merges the bench's live handler over it) whose message names `select_slot` and says to send the exemplar in a later turn. **Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/agents/slots`, six page loads.** ONE message asking to open `research.auto_tagger` AND draft an exemplar produced TWO sequential confirm dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim; Apply on both opened the workbench and staged a real test case into the composer with its Save bar live — label "Long-tail SEO blog post, sparse tag vocabulary", a `user_input`, and a `variables` object filling every one of the contract's `topic` / `page_content` / `available_tags`, which the agent could only have known from the new `selected_slot_contract`. The agent sent `variables` as an OBJECT and the handler serialized it, so the inline-tool double-encoding trap never fired; a repeat on a second slot (`chat.cx_default`) staged an equally specific case, so the target is not tuned to one rich contract. **The DECLINE load:** "Keep as is" on the first dialog left the workbench closed and nothing staged, and the agent stopped rather than retrying — *"that action was declined, so the slot workbench isn't open. Staging an exemplar is refused unless the slot is open in the workbench, so I've paused rather than retry the decline."* Error Inspector on that load: **0 distinct · 0 total**, no `surface-writeback` capture. **The UNDECLARED-target load** (asked to set an unhealthy slot's health to ok and enable it) drew no write attempt at all and a refusal that gave back this manifest's own reasoning: *"Health isn't a field you set. It's a computed verdict derived from the pin vs. the agent's versions… Repinning is the admin's deliberate action, not mine… because latest is not always better"* — the surface's second law, quoted from the intro. Error Inspector 0 distinct · 0 total on that load too. **The forced-INVALID load** (`label` as the number 42, `variables` as a JSON string) came back to the agent word-for-word as `label must be a non-empty string — a short name saying what this test case exercises.` with the composer never opened and every field empty — validate-then-apply, confirmed off the DOM — and the agent correctly reported that validation short-circuited on `label` before it ever reached the `variables`-must-be-an-object check. That load carries exactly 2 Error Inspector entries: ONE `surface-writeback` contract break (`targetName: slot_exemplar_draft`) plus its user-facing toast, which is `fail()` being loud by design and the control that makes the other loads' zero mean something. One earlier load is worth recording on its own: with an empty slot name in the prompt the agent refused to guess which of 126 slots was meant and quoted the description's own remount warning back as the reason. `check:surface-drift` clean (142 surfaces, 3529 values); `pnpm type-check` clean with ZERO errors (the ~8 pre-existing `features/canvas/*` / `features/assists/*` / `features/marketing/content-plan/*` errors the campaign brief warns about are NOT present on this main — reported rather than assumed); `eslint` clean on all four touched files. Nothing was saved to `agent.slot_exemplar`: every run staged into the composer only, so no throwaway rows. DB mirror sync still pending. **CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips fired, same wall as the two entries above:** this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright; extending the avalanche needs a fresh chain. Recorded here because it is now the THIRD independent scan to reach the same verdict, which is worth more than any one of them: scanning all 45 manifests still lacking `writeTargets` on `56e88f320`, I independently ruled out `marketing-site-pages` (a read-only registry over the `web.v_page_list` projection — observed snapshot titles, health verdicts and rolling GSC metrics; its three `agentRoles` are for REASONING, and the only page-owned state is the `?coverage=` filter), `marketing-crawl` (one FROZEN `web.crawl_session` plus ten technical reports — evidence by construction), `admin-sandbox` (read-only list whose only actions are stop/delete/mint-SSH), `image-uploader` (ephemeral intake widget, no emitter, not even alt text), `admin-official-components` (a STATIC in-code `componentList`, so no canonical write path), `gallery`, `rag-viewer`, `education` and `rag-library` — the last despite its two live emitters, because its 32 values are pipeline COUNTS and browse filters and a grep for `updateLibrary`/`createLibrary` under `features/rag` finds no canonical write path to be canonical about. Two additions to the handoffs above, from scouting that went a step further. **`matrx-user/agent-settings` is worth one more look than "governance console" gives it:** `AgentSettingsForm` really does own four authored fields (name, description, category, tags), but whoever takes it must FIRST check whether `matrx-user/agent-builder`'s already-shipped `agent_name`/`agent_description`/`agent_category`/`agent_tags` make it a duplicate write path onto the same record — and if it survives that, its handlers must stage into the form's LOCAL `draft` useState, because dispatching `setAgentField` writes the shared Redux record the form DIFFS AGAINST, leaving the value on screen and the Save bar reading "All changes saved": a silent no-op that looks exactly like success. It is also `readiness: "stub"`, window-only, and `route-to-surface.ts:34` maps a `/agents/settings` prefix that is not a route. **`matrx-user/transcript-scribe`'s custom dictionary** (`features/dictionary`, `DictionaryManager`) is the one piece of genuinely authored vocabulary the "viewers and records" bucket hides — terms an agent can propose from a transcript it has just read — but that surface declares a single value and no emitter, so it is `surface-authoring` before it is write-targets. Otherwise this scan agrees with the entries above: the remaining field is browse, report and governance state, and the campaign is essentially complete.
- **2026-08-12 — Agents Hub agent-writable (live-verified); THE composite-filter target, and a ROUTE-vs-MOUNT drift caught in the act.** `matrx-user/agents` declares ONE `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `catalog_filters`, a partial object carrying all nine gallery filter fields (`search_query`, `deep_search`, `ownership_tab`, `sort_by`, `included_categories`, `included_tags`, `favorites_filter`, `archived_filter`, `favorites_first`). Handler lives on `AgentsGrid`'s existing provider and dispatches `setAgentConsumerFilter` — the exact action the search box, the tab buttons and every filter chip already fire — so an agent write and a user click are indistinguishable. Its read twin is the existing `filters` value, which is literally this object. **Why ONE composite instead of nine micro-targets:** these fields are one user thought ("show only my archived research agents, most recent first" is four of them in one breath), so bundling buys one confirm dialog instead of nine, makes validation atomic (one bad category rejects the WHOLE call, so a filter can never half-apply — the guarantee the arrays needed, extended free to every field), and removes write ORDERING as a hazard by construction: one call, one store read, one dispatch, no replace/append pair to race. The stated trade is that the user accepts or declines the object whole. **Enum checks run against the vocabulary the UI actually renders, never re-typed literals:** `sort_by` validates against (and the model-facing description interpolates) the same `SORT_OPTIONS` constant the sort panel maps over, and `included_categories` / `included_tags` validate against the LIVE `selectAllAgentCategories` / `selectAllAgentTags` read at call time — deliberately not the emitted `available_*` snapshot, because that vocabulary GROWS as the list loads, so the live set can only ever accept more. `"__none__"` (`AGENT_NONE_SENTINEL`) is accepted because `MultiSelectChips` really does render an Uncategorized/Untagged chip for it; `ownership_tab:"system"` is refused because this gallery renders no System tab, and `"shared"` is refused when nothing is shared with the user, since the tab row itself is conditional and either would strand the user in a view with no control to leave it. Unknown KEYS throw rather than being ignored — silently dropping `{archive_this:…}` would report success for a write that did nothing. **`ask`, not `auto`, on the `images` reasoning plus one of its own:** the gallery's `ReferencesBulkCopyButton` takes the filtered set as its live argument, and more sharply, every row carries a per-agent `…` menu with Archive and Delete — re-filtering under a user about to click a row MOVES the rows, which is the `images` "changed what gets deleted" hazard arriving through displacement rather than through a set argument. **Deliberately NOT targets:** everything that mutates an agent (favorite, archive, delete, duplicate, publish, share) — `archived_filter` means SHOW archived and the description says so twice; the derived read values (`visible_agents`, every count, `has_active_filters`, `version_lookup`); and `peeked_agent_id`, which turns out to be un-writable by construction — `agent-peek-tracker` is a REPORTING register written by each card's own modal, with no canonical opener the hub can reach, so a target there would have needed a parallel open path (the "no canonical write path" trap). **The finding worth reusing: this surface's emitter had drifted out from under its own route.** `surfaceFromPathname` maps the whole `/agents` prefix here, but `AgentsGrid` — the only `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` for `matrx-user/agents` — renders on `/agents/classic` ALONE; `/agents/all`, where `/agents` redirects an authenticated user, became the `lib/entity-list` browse shell (`AgentBrowsePage`) and mounts no runtime. The header popover on `/agents/all` therefore still names "Agents Hub" from the route map while offering neither live values nor the write tool — the `scraper`/`voice-pad` mount trap in a new form, and it would read exactly like a broken target to the next agent. The manifest docblock (which still claimed `AgentsGrid` drives `/agents/all`) and a new `readinessNote` now say this outright; adopting the emitter on `AgentBrowsePage` is left as `surface-authoring`, not smuggled in here. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/agents/classic`: ONE message asking for four changes produced ONE ask dialog carrying the manifest prose verbatim, and Apply landed all four in the REAL DOM (search box `QME`, Mine tab active, "6 results", the six QME agents alphabetical) — the agent then volunteered the description's own staleness warning ("the visible-agent list from before this change is stale, so I can't reliably tell you the new on-screen results"). "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the page byte-identical and a graceful acknowledgement. A request to archive one agent and favorite another produced NO tool call at all, with the agent naming the view-only scope and repeating the distinction unprompted: "The `archived_filter` control I can touch only means 'show archived agents' — it doesn't archive anything." A forced `included_tags:["oncology-research"]` came back verbatim as `client_tool_error: catalog_filters.included_tags rejected: "oncology-research" is not a value this library uses, so no filter was changed. Available: ai-crawler, compliance, …` (34 live tags listed back) with the filters untouched and no double-encode retry. Error Inspector: **zero** captures on the clean load; the invalid-value load, run separately, produced exactly the one `surface-writeback` capture the guard is supposed to fire plus its paired toast. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,433 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining errors are pre-existing in `features/brokers/*`. DB mirror sync still pending. **Adjacent bug fixed in passing:** `useAgentConsumer` gained canonical `setIncludedCats` / `setIncludedTags` (one dispatch of the final set), because the handler needed a whole-set write and the existing whole-set path was `toAdd.forEach(toggleCategory)` — every `toggleCategory` computes its next array from the SAME render-closure snapshot, so two toggles in one tick keep only the last, and "Clear filter" with three categories active removed one. `AgentsGrid` now consumes the new setters; two copies of the same `applyArrayViaToggle` helper survive in `features/agents/agent-sets/` (`AgentLibraryRail`, `CreateSetDialog`) and carry the same latent bug — logged, not fixed off-mission.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/cms` (the CMS hub) added to the adopter list it was already missing from — a COLLISION resolved as docs-only, plus an independent live re-verification of the landed design.** Assigned as a write-target chip for the CMS hub; the STEP ZERO collision check found the work already merged to `main` (`27b21892`): `cms.manifest.ts` declares `new_site_draft`, `app/(core)/cms/page.tsx` handles it, `features/cms/utils/siteSlug.ts` exists as the shared vocabulary, `features/cms/FEATURE.md` carries its Change Log line, and `agent.review_queue` already holds a `/cms` row. **Per the skill's collision rule the landed design wins, so nothing was reimplemented** — and it is the design this chip would have produced anyway (one composite object target, not three scalars, on the `crm-create-party` reasoning the docblock cites). **The one genuinely additive thing was the gap the landing agent left: `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` was never touched.** The hub appeared in neither the agent-writable adopter enumeration nor this Change Log, while `features/cms/FEATURE.md` closed with "See `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` for the full adopter entry" — a cross-reference pointing at nothing. That entry now exists. **Worth generalizing: a surface whose own feature doc is complete can still be invisible in the campaign-wide one, and the adopter list is where the campaign looks to decide what is already taken** — so a missing entry does not just lose documentation, it invites the next chip to re-assign a surface that is already done. When a collision check finds a surface shipped, checking the surfaces doc for the entry is worth doing before concluding there is nothing additive left. **The landed work was then independently re-verified end-to-end with real Badass Agent runs on `/cms`** (not taken on trust from the merged entry's claims), five scenarios each on its own page load: (1) "draft a new site for Happy Paws Mobile Grooming, domain happypawsmobile.com" raised ONE ask dialog carrying the manifest description **verbatim** (checked against the manifest string, including the interpolated `CMS_SITE_SLUG_RULE` and `CMS_SITE_DOMAIN_RULE`), and Apply landed `Happy Paws Mobile Grooming` / `happy-paws-mobile-grooming` / `happypawsmobile.com` read back off the three real inputs, with the dialog **opened by the handler** from a closed start. (2) "Keep as is" on a Northside Yoga draft left the dialog CLOSED and nothing staged, and the agent acknowledged the decline without an error. (3) Asked to set a site's `agent_write_policy` to `full` and mark it active, the agent raised **no dialog at all** and refused both, naming `new_site_draft` as the only target and reproducing the manifest's own rationale ("letting me flip my own permission to full would defeat its entire purpose"). (4) A deliberately invalid `domain: "https://Example.COM/shop"` came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim — `client_tool_error: new_site_draft.domain expects a bare hostname such as www.example.com — no scheme, no path, no port. Got "https://Example.COM/shop".` — with **nothing staged and the dialog never opened, even though the `name` in the same payload was valid**, confirming the validate-then-apply ordering rather than a partial apply. (5) A clean load with only a valid apply produced **zero `surface-writeback` captures** ("No errors captured yet"); the invalid-value load produced 2, which is the seam being loud by design. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3520 values) and `type-check` clean. **One harness note for the next agent on this surface:** because the create dialog is a modal, the Error Inspector button is genuinely unclickable while it is open — a capture check has to press Escape first, or it reads as an unreachable inspector rather than as the `aria-hidden` behaviour the manifest documents. **CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips were fired, for the same mechanical reason the `matrx-user/canvas` entry records: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright.** Picking these up needs a fresh chain, so the scouting is recorded here rather than lost. Of the 59 manifests still lacking `writeTargets` on `main`, FOUR survive the judgment bar, and the two with live mounts are the ones worth doing first. **(a) `matrx-user/messages` — the strongest remaining surface on the board.** Both mounts already register `<SurfaceRuntimeProvider>` with `getScope` and no `getWriteHandlers` (THREAD `app/(core)/messages/[conversationId]/page.tsx`, LIST `app/(core)/messages/MessagesPageClient.tsx`), and the manifest docblock already blesses the exact scope ("summarize the thread, draft a reply"). The compose box is `features/messaging/components/MessageInput.tsx` — local state, a deep child under `ChatThread`, so this is a `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` job, not a provider prop. A reply draft with SEND left human is the `new_site_draft` / `generation_request` shape, but the send gate is stricter than anywhere else in the campaign because the artifact goes to a REAL OTHER PERSON; `current_sender_name`/`last_message_text` are records of what someone actually said, so writing them would be forging another person's words — the clearest NO here. Two things to settle deliberately: whether Enter SENDS in `MessageInput` (a staged draft one keystroke from going out changes at least the description), and the per-mount split, since the LIST mount has no open conversation and no compose box and should register nothing. **(b) `matrx-user/sandboxes`** — `app/(core)/sandbox/[id]/page.tsx` already mounts the provider (no handlers), and the terminal has a real `commandInput`/`setCommandInput` input whose execution sits behind a separate `handleExec` Send button. Staging a command without running it is the `scraper` `scrape_command` precedent almost exactly, and it is the interesting judgment call of the two: everything else the surface emits is container identity, status and observed `terminal_output`, which are evidence and infrastructure rather than authored content. Check the Enter-key path before designing it. **(c) `matrx-admin/bundles` and (d) `matrx-admin/lookups`** — both admin surfaces whose OWN manifest docblocks already bless the scope ("help the admin name/describe a new bundle, draft member aliases"; "help the admin draft a name/description for a new client, surface, or executor"), but both are `readiness: "stub"` with NO emitter (all state is local `useState` in `BundlesAdminPage.tsx` / `LookupsAdminPage.tsx`), so each is a TWO-job chip: `surface-authoring` first, then this skill. Membership/executor wiring stays human on `agent-builder`'s reasoning that changing what a bundle REACHES is a capability change, not a copy edit. **Checked and FAILING the bar, so a chip fired at one wastes a run:** `rag-library` (32 values, but the page holds only search/status/catalog FILTER state and has no rename or description path — the `files` ruling), `matrx-admin/official-components` (its `current_component_name`/`_description` read a STATIC `componentList` catalogue file, not editable rows), `matrx-user/transcript-studio` (one value, and its docblock says the minimalism is deliberate to protect hand-coded scope), `matrx-user/education` (a landing/nav hub), plus the viewers and records already ruled out in the `canvas` entry.
- **2026-08-12 — CRM list agent-writable on BOTH mounts; THE reference for write targets NOT crossing `inheritsFrom`.** `matrx-user/crm` declares 4 `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets — `search_query`, `party_kind_filter`, `column_filters`, `list_sort` — handled on `features/crm/components/CrmListPage.tsx`'s own provider (`getWriteHandlers`), which owns every state atom they touch. Each lands through the setter the human control calls: `list.setQuery` for the toolbar search box, the People/Companies facet and the column-filter popovers (the same call `onTableState` makes), and `setPrefs` for the sort (a column-header click). **This is a FIND-AND-SURFACE surface, not an authoring one** — nothing here is authored copy — so it follows the shipped `images` adoption rather than `tasks`: the useful agent job is "put the records matching this description in front of me". **No selection target, deliberately:** `images` earns `image_selection` because tiles carry bulk checkboxes, but this table mounts `MatrxDataTable` with no selection model at all, so declaring one would have wired a target to nothing. Ranking the surface honestly is what set the target list — `scope_kind`/`selected_organization_id` were EXCLUDED as the ownership/visibility axis the judgment bar puts on the NO side (they change WHOSE records are in play, not which of the user's match), along with pagination mechanics, the trash view (no read twin), and every create/delete/restore/merge path. **The new lesson: write targets and GROUPS are both resolved per-manifest and neither crosses `inheritsFrom`.** `crm-manager` (the floating `CrmManagerWindow`, same `CrmListPage` component, same handlers, only `surfaceName` differs) inherits the read vocabulary but `findDeclaredTarget` reads `getManifest(name).writeTargets` with no chain walk, so the window mount would have been offered NO write tool at all; `crm.manifest.ts` therefore exports `crmWriteTargets` **and** `crmGroups` and `crm-manager.manifest.ts` re-declares both — `check:surface-drift` catches the groups half loudly ("write target references undeclared group"), the targets half silently. That is the opposite call from `rag-data-stores`, where two mounts own genuinely different state; here one component means one contract, so splitting would be a lie. Every enum check reads the constants `crm.manifest.ts` interpolates into the prose the model sees (`PARTY_KIND_FILTER_ENUM_TEXT`, `PARTY_SORT_KEY_ENUM_TEXT`, `DATE_BUCKET_ENUM_TEXT`, … added to `features/crm/types.ts`), so contract and guard cannot drift — the `scrape-command.ts` discipline. `column_filters` REPLACES the whole bag and REJECTS unrecognised keys rather than dropping them (a misspelled `name` would otherwise silently do nothing), validates the entire object before touching state, and tolerates the double-encoded JSON-string form; `list_sort` folds `sort_key` + `sort_direction` into one object because one header click sets both, and reads the current sort **through a ref** — `applySurfaceWrite` captures the handler closure BEFORE awaiting the confirm dialog, so a partial write folding in a render-closure value would silently undo a header click made while the dialog was open. `ask` over `auto` despite all four being `ui`: every row carries a "…" menu whose Delete acts on THAT row, so re-filtering moves which record sits under the user's cursor — same call `images` and `markdown-studio` made. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/crm` against a 3-record list: one message asking for three changes produced three dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim, Apply narrowed the DOM from 3 rows to 1 (`1-1 of 1`, search box reading `acme` on a later run) — asserted on the table, not the tool result; "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the search box and rows untouched and the agent replying "You declined that, so I've left it alone"; a "delete Sarah Mitchell permanently" request was REFUSED with no tool call attempted ("none of them create, edit, or delete records"); and four forced invalid values came back to the agent verbatim with the list unmoved — e.g. `list_sort.key expects one of display_name | party_kind | job_title | primary_domain | created_at | updated_at — received "employer". These are database columns; Employer is a joined embed and cannot be sorted on.` and `column_filters rejected: unrecognised key(s) name.` Error Inspector on the clean load (valid applies + a decline only, no forced invalid): **`0 distinct · 0 total occurrences`** — zero captures of any kind. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean (0 errors; the `features/brokers/*` baseline is already fixed on main). Nothing in the CRM was created or deleted. DB mirror sync still pending.
