Writes reuse the surface's canonical state paths and preserve identity, lifecycle, and undeclared operations as human-only.
- **2026-08-11 — Content Plan Entities: restored the write-half docs a parallel merge dropped, deleted the merge debris, and re-verified the surface live.** No new targets — this is reconciliation of ALREADY-MERGED work plus an independent verification, which is what the collision rule asks for when a surface is taken while you are on it. Two agents built `matrx-user/content-plan-entities` in parallel and both merged; main resolved to the "lifted draft" CODE while the DOCS that survived came from the other, superseded design, so the repo described targets that do not exist. **What is actually live:** 3 ask-policy targets registered by `EntityManager.tsx` through `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` against a parent-owned `draftRef` — `entity_draft` (`draft`, stages `{label?, entity_type?}` into the OPEN editor dialog and throws when none is open), `save_entity_draft` (`entity`, commits that draft through the dialog's own create/update path), and `add_entities` (`entity`, appends roster rows through `createPlanEntity` with duplicate-label refusal and a post-write label check). Read twin: `entity_editor_draft`. **Deleted as debris:** `features/marketing/content-plan/lib/entity-write-targets.ts` and its 21-test suite — they validated the superseded `open_entity_editor` / `create_entity` targets and an `entity_draft` that accepted `source_type_id`, which the shipped target explicitly refuses; nothing imported them but their own test, so main was shipping a passing test suite certifying a contract no code implements. The superseded entry in the content-plan FEATURE.md is now marked as such rather than deleted, and its claim that `source_type_options` was added as a read value is corrected — it never was. **This surface had NO adopter-list entry and NO Change Log entry here at all**; the merge dropped both sides' surfaces docs even though the code shipped. That is the second instance of the hazard the 2026-08-10 "Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs" entry warned about, and it argues the warning belongs where merges actually land, not only in this log. **Independently re-verified with a real Badass Agent run** (prpinjectionmd roster, 44 entities): `add_entities` asked with its manifest description verbatim, applied, and persisted a real row (removed afterwards via the roster trash button); a same-message delete request AND a node-attachment request were both refused with the agent enumerating the targets it actually has and naming the node surface as the right home for attachment; `entity_draft` with no editor open returned its own throw to the agent verbatim. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,410 values) clean; content-plan jest suites pass; `type-check` shows the same 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors before and after, and zero new ones. **Open usability finding, deliberately NOT fixed** (it is the subject of a pending review-queue question, and pre-empting a human decision is not additive): `entity_draft` requires the editor dialog to already be open, but that dialog is MODAL — with it open, a `fixed inset-0 z-[10000]` `aria-hidden` overlay covers both the header "Agents for this page" button and the chat composer. Measured, not argued: `elementFromPoint` over the Agents button returns the overlay and a real click times out, while the identical click succeeds once the dialog is closed. So the target is reachable only by starting the turn with the editor closed and opening it while the agent is mid-thought — a race the user has to win, and the agent's own advice ("click Edit, then tell me and I'll re-run") is impossible because the overlay covers the composer it is asking the user to type into. Letting the handler OPEN the editor on a row named in the value would remove the race entirely; that is the call the review row asks Arman to make.
- **2026-08-11 — Agent Review Queue agent-writable; THE self-dealing reference, and THE no-selected-row reference.** `matrx-admin/agent-review` declares 2 ask-policy targets on the Agent Repair Board. `review_feedback_draft` (`mode:"draft"`) stages review prose into a row's feedback textarea; `review_triage_classification` (`mode:"entity"`) re-classifies `lane` / `priority` / `workstreams` / `required_tools`. Handlers in `features/admin/agent-review/components/AgentReviewWriteTargets.tsx`. **The exclusion is the whole point of this adopter.** This queue is where agents register the work they built for Arman to check, so the STATUS transitions — Approve, Request changes, Archive, Restore — are not declared as targets at any policy. Not `ask`, not "manual for now": absent. An agent approving or archiving its own review row is self-dealing, and an `ask` dialog would still park a sign-off one click away from the artifact under review; the human presses the button that changes state, and an agent's contribution is the feedback text and the routing. `id`/`created_at`/`source` (identity and provenance), `url` (re-aiming a review at something the reviewer never saw), and `show_archived` (a mechanical view filter) are excluded too. **No selected row, so the row is an argument.** The page renders the entire queue and has no selection concept, so both targets take a `row_id` drawn from `queue_sample` and refuse loudly when it is missing or not loaded — the alternative, inferring a row from position or from an ancestor's text, edits somebody else's review item. **Draft mode required lifting real state:** the feedback buffer lived in `ReviewItemCard`'s `useState`, where the surface could neither publish it nor stage into it, and an entity write would have left the admin's textarea showing stale text next to a changed database. It now lives in `AgentReviewClient` as `feedbackDrafts` keyed by row id, published as the new `feedback_drafts` read value (only genuinely unsaved text — an entry that matches what is stored is not pending anything), and the handler calls the same setter the admin's typing calls. **The triage target patches, never replaces:** `metadata` is a versioned envelope, so it goes through the page's own `metadataWithReviewTriage` + `updateReviewQueueRow` pair — the exact path the "Apply suggested triage" button uses — validating the RESULT against `reviewTriageSchema` and carrying `version`, `assignment` and `verification` through untouched (claim state belongs to the `agent-review-queue` skill's atomic SQL protocol; a page write would stomp another agent's live claim). A row whose triage is missing or invalid is based on the page's own deterministic `suggestReviewTriage`, with the agent's fields applied on top. **The error-swallowing trap here is RLS, not optimism:** `updateReviewQueueRow` does throw on a Postgres error, but a super-admin RLS policy filters a forbidden UPDATE to ZERO rows without raising anything, so a non-admin would have been told "saved" forever. `refresh()` now resolves with the rows it just read, and the entity handler re-reads and throws unless the new classification is actually there. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs against a throwaway queue row (created and deleted for the test — never a row Arman is triaging): ONE message asking for both changes produced two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim, Apply landed both (`{ok:true, mode:"draft"}` / `{ok:true, mode:"entity"}`), and expanding the row showed the agent's text sitting in the real `Feedback for …` textarea beside Save feedback / Request changes / Approve while the DB still had `feedback: null` — draft staged, nothing saved. The triage persisted (verified over MCP: lane `browser_ui`, priority `high`, workstreams `[responsive_ui, verification]`, tools `[browser, frontend_code]`, with `assignment`/`verification`/`version` intact). "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` and left the row alone. A deliberately invalid enum came back to the model as the handler's own throw verbatim (`review_triage_classification: priority must be one of critical | high | normal | low (got "urgent").`) with the stored priority still `high` — validate-then-apply — and an unknown `row_id` was refused the same way. Error Inspector on the clean load (valid applies + a decline, no bad values): **zero** `surface-writeback` captures; the captures from the invalid-value probes were deliberately induced and live in their own browser session, since a handler throw is a capture by design. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,411 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining type errors are pre-existing and all in `features/brokers/*`. The surface has no `ui_surface` row yet, so the DB mirror waits on seeding + manifest sync; the client tool works either way, as this run shows.
- **2026-08-11 — Scanner write-target docs restored + two contract defects fixed; the adopter list's missing entry.** The scanner's write targets (`scan_title`, `scan_page_labels`) shipped with working CODE but **no record in this file** — `features/pdf/FEATURE.md`'s own entry closed with "See the surfaces FEATURE.md Change Log for the full verification record," and that record did not exist, nor did `matrx-user/scanner` appear in the agent-writable adopter list above. This is the second instance of the hazard the 2026-08-10 "Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs a merge dropped" entry names, so it is worth stating as a rule: **a `FEATURE.md` conflict resolved by taking one side wholesale silently reverts documentation for shipped code, and the campaign's cross-file pointers are how you notice.** The adopter clause above is now written; nothing about the shipped design changed. Two real defects found while reading it, both fixed: (1) **the constants block had drifted from the handler it exists to bind.** `features/pdf/scanner/types.ts` declares `SCAN_TITLE_MAX_LENGTH` / `SCAN_PAGE_LABEL_MAX_LENGTH` with a comment stating the limits live there so "what the agent is TOLD and what the page ACCEPTS cannot drift apart" — but the live handler in `ScannerSurfaceRuntime.tsx` re-typed its own `MAX_LABEL_CHARS = 120` literal and never imported them, while the page-label constant still read `80`. The manifest advertises 120 and the shipped handler enforced 120, so the constant was the wrong one: it is now 120, `assertLabelString` takes the bound per target, and both handlers import from `./types`. Enforcement is byte-identical to what shipped — this closes the drift without touching verified behaviour. (2) **a superseded handler draft was still in the tree.** `features/pdf/scanner/useScannerWriteHandlers.ts` was imported by nothing, yet implemented a CONTRADICTORY contract — positional-array `scan_page_labels`, an undeclared third target `scan_page_label`, an 80-character bound, and a doc block asserting "Position, not identity, is the agent's coordinate system… An agent must never have to know about `itemId`", the exact inverse of the shipped id-keyed map — and it was `types.ts`'s only reader, which is how that constant drifted. Deleted; the `types.ts` comment now names its real consumer. **Independently re-verified live** (real Badass Agent runs on `/tools/scanner`, three uploaded pages, values read back from the actual DOM inputs rather than the agent's summary): ONE message asking for a title and three page labels produced two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose verbatim, and Apply landed both — title `"Q3 Vendor Invoices"`, labels `["Invoice cover","Signature page","Appendix A"]` — which also clears the multi-target stale-closure trap (`applySurfaceWrite` resolves every handler before the first confirm; the post-commit refs held). Staged values survived a page reload via the resumable manifest. "Keep as is" left the title at `"Q3 Vendor Invoices"` with no error and a graceful acknowledgement. Asking to DELETE a captured page was refused with the agent naming exactly the two writable targets and pointing at the review tile's own control. Two deliberately invalid values came back as the handlers' own throws verbatim — the positional array (`scan_page_labels expects an object map keyed by item_id … A positional array silently mislabels every page after a reorder or insert`) and a slash in the title (`scan_title becomes the saved PDF's filename, so it cannot contain "/" or "\"`) — with **nothing staged** in either case (validate-then-apply confirmed by re-reading the inputs). Error Inspector: **zero captures on the clean load**; the probe load showed exactly 4, which is the correct number — two induced failures × (one `surface-writeback` contract break + its paired user-facing toast) — reported here rather than rounded to zero. `check:surface-drift` clean (140 surfaces, 3,410 values).
- **2026-08-11 — Knowledge System write targets LIVE-VERIFIED and documented (the implementation landed undocumented and unverified); plus a re-typed-literal fix.** `matrx-user/knowledge`'s 9 write targets were merged to main without an entry in this file's adopter list or Change Log, without a Change Log line in any of the three features that own its mounts, and — as far as could be established — without the skill's mandatory live-agent run. This entry closes that gap: the surface is now in the adopter list above, and everything below was confirmed against a real Badass Agent on a running dev server rather than read off the code. **All three mounts exercised.** *Extraction grid* (`ExtractionDatasetClient`): ONE message asking for a rename + a grid filter + a rewrite of an extracted DETAILS cell produced exactly one ask dialog (for the `entity` rename) carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply persisted the new name through `updateJob` and the header re-rendered to it; `extraction_query` applied with NO dialog, correctly, because it is `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"auto"`, and the search box showed `chiropractic`; and the cell rewrite was refused, with the agent both naming the three declared targets AND volunteering the evidence argument the manifest intro makes ("Extracted rows are evidence pulled from your own source documents… i.e. falsifying the extraction"). A deliberately over-long name, with the agent explicitly told not to pre-validate, came back to it as the handler's own throw verbatim — `client_tool_error: extraction_dataset_name expects at most 120 characters (got 271).` — with nothing written. "Keep as is" left the name unchanged, returned no error, and the agent acknowledged the decline gracefully. *Graph canvas* (`KgGraphCanvas`): `graph_search` applied with no dialog (`auto`) and `graph_detail_level` raised the tier behind one ask dialog, confirming the surface's stated `auto`-vs-`ask` line holds where it costs a server round trip. *Suggestion queue* (`SuggestionsManager`): `suggestions_filter` produced its ask dialog with the REPLACE semantics for `statuses` spelled out in the prose the user actually reads, applied as `statuses:["pending"]`, and a same-message request to ACCEPT the first suggestion was refused — the agent named the queue's only two targets, noted that `focused_suggestion_id` "explicitly does not accept, reject, defer, or star anything", and added that "accepting a suggestion writes to your scopes, which is a decision I shouldn't make silently even if I had the capability", which is the manifest's doctrine reaching the model intact. **The finding worth carrying forward is an environment trap, not a code defect.** The first verification attempt reported the rename FAILING twice, which reads exactly like a broken entity handler. It is not: `page_extraction_jobs` is RLS-gated to owners, the seeded dataset used for the first run is owned by someone else, and `updateJob`'s `.update(...).select("*").single()` therefore comes back `PGRST116 — The result contains 0 rows`. The page's OWN inline rename fails identically on that dataset, which is the check that separates the two — **exercise a `mode:"entity"` target on a row the logged-in user owns, and confirm the human control fails too before filing the handler as broken.** The dataset renamed during verification was restored to its original name; nothing else was left changed. **One code change:** the 120-character cap was hand-typed twice — once in the handler's throw, once in the manifest's model-facing prose — so the number an agent is TOLD and the number that is ENFORCED could drift silently. Both now interpolate `EXTRACTION_JOB_NAME_MAX_LENGTH` from `features/page-extraction/data-review/constants.ts`. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces) + `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync. Honest limit on the Error Inspector check: its panel could not be read programmatically in this harness, so "zero captures" is NOT claimed here — what can be said is that no `surface-writeback` capture text appeared anywhere in the document after a valid apply, and the two induced failures (over-long name, undeclared cell write) behaved loudly by design.
- **2026-08-11 — Voice Playground settings agent-writable; THE reference for a guard that must read CURRENT state.** `matrx-user/chat-voice` declares 3 ask-policy `mode:"draft"` targets over the ONLY authored state the surface has: `voice_instructions` (full replace), `append_voice_instructions` (the `agent-builder` replace/append pair applied to a VOICE prompt — a pronunciation rule or a brevity guardrail is exactly the kind of one-line addition that should not cost a 3,000-character round trip), and `voice_id`. Handlers live in `features/voice-agent/hooks/useVoicePlaygroundWriteHandlers.ts` and dispatch the SAME `updateConfig` action `InstructionsEditor`'s textarea `onChange` and `VoicePicker`'s `onValueChange` fire, so an agent rewrite and the user's own typing are indistinguishable. `voice_id` validates against the `VOICES` constant the picker renders its options from, and the manifest interpolates that same constant into the model-facing description, so the vocabulary the agent is told, the vocabulary the handler accepts, and the options on screen cannot drift. **Per-mount split:** handlers are registered by `PlaygroundSettingsSheet`, which renders only on `/chat/voice/playground`, so the `/chat/voice` intro route mounts the same provider and offers an agent no write tool at all. That split is load-bearing rather than cosmetic — `updateConfig` returns SILENTLY for a non-playground preset, an unknown instance id, and a falsy `voiceId`, so handlers on the intro route would report success for a write that never happened; every handler accordingly re-reads the store after dispatching and throws when the value did not land (the `war-room` optimistic-write finding, in its Redux-reducer form). **The generalizable finding is the guard.** Every handler refuses while a voice session is live, because mid-session the change either does nothing (the instructions already went out in `session.update`) or silently alters what the user is mid-conversation with. `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler BEFORE it shows the confirm dialog, so a guard reading a status captured in a render snapshot can act on a value that went stale while the dialog sat open — the `image-studio` trap. Reading `store.getState()` at call time is immune to that by construction, and it was verified head-on rather than argued: a dialog was opened with the page IDLE, a session was started WHILE that dialog was open, and Apply pressed afterwards was still refused with `A voice session is live right now (status: "requesting-mic")…` and the voice left unchanged. (That live state was produced by stalling the app's own `/api/voice-agent/token` route, so the real `start()` path parked the real slice in a real non-idle status with no realtime socket and no provider spend — the way to exercise a live-session guard without putting a conversation on the wire.) **The exclusions are most of the surface, and they are the point.** `connection_status` / `mic_muted` / `connection_error` / `session_started_at_ms` / `active_turn` are live session and DEVICE state — muting someone's microphone is not a copy edit — and starting or ending a session is not a target either. `transcript_text` / `transcript_turns` / `last_user_utterance` / `last_assistant_utterance` / `turn_count` / `total_interruptions` / `latency_summary` are the RECORD of what was actually said and how the session behaved; writing them would forge the transcript, the same input-vs-output line `voice-pad` drew around `transcript_entries` and `transcripts-cleanup` around `raw_transcript_text`. `conversation_id` / `voice_agent_id` are identity. `realtime_tools` / `realtime_tool_names` stay human-only because what the voice agent may REACH is a capability change, not a copy edit (the `agent-builder` and tool-registry line), and `session_preset` is derived from the route — "writing" it would mean navigating the user elsewhere. **`mode: "draft"` follows `image-generate` / `marketing-crawls` / `education-assessment`:** a settings form the user reviews and then executes with their own press. Nothing here persists to any record — the values live in `voiceAgentSlice` and are consumed when the user next taps the mic — so each description leads with "staged … for the NEXT session" and says nothing is persisted, which is the sentence the user actually reads first in the dialog. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on the playground, session idle: ONE message asking for two changes produced two per-target ask dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply landed both through the handlers and the REAL controls moved (voice picker Ara → Leo, instructions textarea 3,249 → 3,352 characters with a `## Brevity` section appended at the end and the original prompt byte-preserved), confirmed by re-opening the sheet and reading the DOM. "Keep as is" left the voice on Leo with no error and a graceful agent acknowledgement. A deliberately invented voice id came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`voice_id must be exactly one of: ara, eve, leo, rex, sal. Received "morgan", which is not a voice this provider offers.`) with nothing staged (validate-then-apply). Asking to mute the mic and toggle the `web_search` realtime tool was refused with the agent naming exactly the three declared targets and citing the read-only rationale. Error Inspector on the clean load: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures (the one entry was a pre-existing `ai_v2_downgrade` backend-fallback warning); the captures on the probe run were the two deliberately induced ones, in their own browser session. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,410 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining type errors are pre-existing and all in `features/brokers/*`. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync. Also corrected the manifest's stale `readinessNote`, which listed the `registry.ts` entry and the `/chat/voice` route mapping as pending when both have been wired for some time.
- **2026-08-10 — War Room cockpit agent-writable (live-verified); THE reference for confirming an OPTIMISTIC write.** `matrx-user/war-room` declares 4 ask-policy `mode:"entity"` targets: `room_name` and `room_description` (authored copy, through `updateRoomIdentity` — the RoomIdentityButton's own thunk), `active_thread_title` (the same authored-label class one level down, through `renameThread` — what `useThreadActions.rename` dispatches), and `add_threads` (DECOMPOSITION, the `add_subtasks` shape from tasks, through `createThread` with the canvas anchor and appended `position` QuickAddThread uses). Handlers live in `features/war-room/components/room/useWarRoomWriteHandlers.ts` and register on the `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` `WarRoomShell` already mounted — only `getWriteHandlers` was missing. **Everything is `entity`, and the manifest says why:** the room has NO staging buffer — the identity popover and the thread-title input both persist on commit — so a `draft` target would have no editor state to stage into and no read twin that reflected it; the ask dialog is the review step a draft mode would otherwise provide. **The generalizable finding: a canonical write path is not automatically an honest one.** All three thunks hide their failures — `updateRoomIdentity` is OPTIMISTIC (it upserts into the store, then rolls back and reports if the server rejects), while `renameThread` and `createThread` swallow the error into a toast and return void/null. A bare `await dispatch(...)` therefore reports success for a write the database refused, which is the one failure mode that makes an agent confidently wrong. So `room_name`/`room_description` fail on `updateRoomIdentity`'s `false`, and the other two re-read the store afterwards (`threadsById[id].title`, the returned thread) and throw when the value did not land — the store is the only honest evidence. `active_thread_title` resolves the staged thread EXACTLY as `buildWarRoomRoomScope` resolves the `active_thread_id` it emitted (so the read twin and the written row are the same thread) and throws when nothing is staged, rather than writing into nothing. `add_threads` caps at 12 per call and stops at the first failure rather than reporting a partial batch as success. Deliberately NOT writable: `room_id`/`room_project_id`/`room_organization_id` (identity, ownership, the RLS basis), `room_resources` + assignments (association plumbing, not authored content), `is_recording` (a live capture), thread delete/park/pin/hide (destructive or the user's own filing), and `view_mode`/`density`/`projected_tab`. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on a throwaway room: ONE message asking for three changes produced three per-target ask dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim, all three applied through the handlers and persisted (`war_rooms.title`/`.description` and `threads.title` confirmed by SQL), the tool result read back `{ok:true, target:"room_name", mode:"entity"}`; `add_threads` created two threads at appended positions 1 and 2; "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with no error and a graceful agent acknowledgement; and two deliberately forced failures came back verbatim — the handler's own `add_threads expects a non-empty array of thread title strings.` for a bare `42`, and the seam's `No mounted surface declares write target "delete_thread".` for an undeclared destructive target. Error Inspector on the clean run: zero `surface-writeback` captures (the only entry was the pre-existing `request_context_changed` server-stream warning); the two captures on the negative run were the induced ones. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.

The CODE for all of it is intact on main; only the docs were reverted, so this file was claiming surfaces are not agent-writable when they are. Both sides were reconciled by hand — main's new `war-room` / `chat-voice` adopters and Voice Playground / War Room entries kept, the dropped ones restored, nothing overwritten. **This is the second recorded occurrence** (see the 2026-08-09 Agent Builder restoration): this file is a campaign-wide hotspot, and resolving a conflict in it by taking one side wholesale silently reverts documentation for shipped code. Reconcile both sides, and check the dated-entry count before and after.
- **2026-08-10 — Scratchpad + Working Document agent-writable (live-verified); THE "read-only from outside, writable from inside" case, and the STALE-CLOSURE reference.** `matrx-user/scratchpad` and `matrx-user/working-document` each declare the SAME 4 `applyPolicy:"ask"` / `mode:"entity"` targets — `document_content` (whole-body replace), `append_document_content`, `replace_selection`, `insert_at_cursor` — exported once as `CONVERSATION_DOCUMENT_WRITE_TARGETS` from `manifests/_conversation-document.manifest.ts`, the same file that already shares their value set. One export, not two copies, because ONE editor (`WorkingDocumentEditor`) renders both surfaces and ONE handler block services them; two lists would drift from that single implementation, and a target whose handler drifted away is a loud runtime defect by design. Each manifest still carries its own docblock arguing why the same targets are right from its own side. **The judgment call is the scratchpad, and it is the reusable one:** its manifest says the cloud agent only READS it, which sounds like a refusal. It isn't. That restriction is about the agent out in the chat, and it is enforced where it lives — the scratchpad is published to a conversation as a read-only `user_scratchpad` context entry, and the chat's `ctx_patch` path cannot write it. It says nothing about an agent the user runs from INSIDE the scratchpad, which is the only agent these targets are ever offered to, on the user's explicit request, behind a per-target confirm — exactly the case the surface was split off to serve ("clean it up, bullet it, make a table"). Inside either document it is just text, so the writable parts are identical. **Like `html-page` and `mermaid-editor`, this surface mounted NO `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`** — it only emitted scope through `UnifiedAgentContextMenu` at trigger time — so the provider was added around `WorkingDocumentEditor`, reusing the existing `getApplicationScope` (built on `useWorkingDocumentSurfaceScope`) as `getScope` rather than writing a second builder. **`mode:"entity"` is a truth claim, on the `mermaid-editor` precedent:** this editor has no Save button; `useWorkingDocument.onChange` — the SAME function every keystroke goes through — writes the canonical slice immediately and its 700ms debounce commits the durable row, and the panel chrome literally reads "Auto-saved". `draft`'s "staged — review and save" would have been a lie in a dialog with nothing to save. The dialog now reads "This is saved immediately", verified live. Recovery is stated as **version history, never ⌘Z**: unlike Monaco or the mermaid reducer this editor mounts no undo stack, and a programmatic React value change does not enter the textarea's native undo history — so no description claims one, and the `history.row_versions` trigger that captures every commit is named instead. **The safety gate is `has_conflict`, and it is real rather than defensive:** `useWorkingDocument`'s commit returns early while a refused-save conflict is unresolved, so a write staged over it would appear in the editor, never persist, and enlarge the very merge the user is about to reconcile — every handler refuses loudly with that reason (they also refuse a view-only sharee, whose durable writes RLS silently drops). Deliberately NOT targets: `document_version` (the optimistic-concurrency token — writing it would forge the check the safety story rests on), `conversation_id` / `conversation_context` / `active_scope_ids` (the conversation is a REFERENCE, not this surface's content), the binding/identity fields (re-pointing a document at another durable row has its own picker and merge dialog), every derived value (`is_dirty`, counts, `current_heading` — an agent moves them by writing text, which IS the evidence loop), `document_title` (the doc auto-names itself from content via `useAutoLabel` while unnamed, and a non-empty title means a human chose it), and `editor_mode` / `cursor_offset` (the human's pane and caret; `cursor_offset` is READ to aim `insert_at_cursor`, never written). **The new general lesson is the STALE CLOSURE.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves a handler BEFORE awaiting the confirm, so when an agent stages several targets in one turn every closure is captured up front; a handler reading `draft` off its render closure would splice into a snapshot taken before the previous target landed — on a document editor, that means replacing the WRONG range. The live body is therefore read through a ref advanced SYNCHRONOUSLY as each write applies, not merely on render. The matching **ordering wrinkle is declared, not papered over**: `replace_selection` and `insert_at_cursor` take their offsets from the live DOM, so when the textarea's value has diverged from that ref — an earlier same-turn whole-document write React has not re-rendered yet — they refuse and tell the agent to re-read `selection` / `cursor_offset` and issue the edit as its own step, rather than cutting at offsets into superseded text. `insert_at_cursor` pads to a blank line only at its two join points, because a document-wide newline collapse would silently rewrite spacing the user chose elsewhere. All four throws say **"plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded"** in the message itself: the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, and an agent told only "expected a string" tends to "fix" that by double-encoding — escaped `\n` and stray quotes are extremely visible in a user's own notes. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on the global scratchpad panel: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim plus "This is saved immediately", Apply restructured 279 chars of messy notes into headed sections in the visible editor, `replace_selection` on a highlighted line rewrote exactly that range with the rest of the document byte-identical, "Keep as is" left the body untouched and the agent reported the decline without an error, an `document_version` request produced NO tool call at all (the agent enumerated exactly the four real targets and explained the token is read-only), and a forced empty `document_content` returned the handler's own throw verbatim as `client_tool_error: document_content expects non-empty text … Emptying the document is a human gesture, not an agent one.` with no double-encode retry. Error Inspector on the clean path: zero `surface-writeback` captures (one unrelated `request_context_changed` stream warning). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **Getting in matters, same as `mermaid-editor`:** these surfaces have no `urlPattern`, and `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` prefers the ROUTE surface whenever it disagrees with the mounted runtime — so on `/chat` the popover offers chat agents and these targets are invisible. The panel's own runtime wins on routes with no route→surface mapping (`/artifacts` was used here), which is where the header popover shows "Scratchpad".
- **2026-08-10 — The live chat route agent-writable (live-verified) — THE record-vs-draft reference.** `matrx-user/chat` declares 2 ask-policy targets: `input_draft` (`mode:"draft"`) taking `{text, mode?: "replace" | "append"}` and landing through the SAME `setUserInputText` action `AgentTextarea` and `NewChatLandingInput` dispatch on every keystroke — so an agent-staged draft joins the undo stack, the sacred draft-protection rules and the send flow exactly like typed text — and `conversation_title` (`mode:"entity"`) through the canonical `renameConversation` thunk that every conversation list already uses, never a raw supabase update. Handlers sit on `ChatRoomClient`'s own provider, the surface's ONLY `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`, which backs all three routes (`/chat/new` through `ChatNewClient`'s `landingContent`, `/chat/a/[agentId]`, `/chat/[conversationId]`); the other three files referencing this surface name (`RunSettingsEditor`, `RunControlsTabPanel`, `NewChatLandingInput`) turned out to be v3 context-menu wiring that mounts no runtime, so there was no second mount to give a posture to. **The judgment call this surface exists to record: chat is a RECORD wrapped around ONE authored field.** The composer holds the message the user has not sent yet — the textbook "authored content an agent drafts better/faster" case, and fully reversible because the user still presses send. The transcript is the opposite: `all_messages`, `last_user_message`, `current_message_text` and `full_conversation_text` are what was actually said by whom, and an agent rewriting them does not edit a draft, it fabricates history. The route HAS a user-driven "Edit & resubmit" path; it stays a human affordance precisely because the record's value is that a human wrote it. **SENDING is not a target** either: the send is the outward-facing act that spends a turn, bills a model and — through the three inbox modes — can steer or interrupt a run already in flight, so the human press stays the gate exactly as it does for `podcast-studio` and `image-generate`. Also undeclared: the run configuration (`added_tools` / `added_skills` / `model` / `sandbox_binding`), on `agent-builder`'s reasoning that changing what an agent may REACH is a capability change, not a copy edit; `attached_resources`, whose read half publishes lean refs to what is ALREADY attached and never the user's library, so an agent could only guess UUIDs (the `education-fastfire` set-picker line); switching the bound agent, since `initial_agent_id` is read-only provenance by this feature's own model; and `working_document` / `scratchpad`, lean refs whose bodies belong to their own surfaces. `variable_values` was considered and left out: its per-variable shapes come from a snapshot of custom components and scope bindings, chat agents rarely declare any, and a target whose happy path could not be exercised live is not one to ship. **The one non-obvious guard** is on the title: a fresh chat holds a CLIENT-MINTED conversation id and has no `chat.conversation` row until its first turn commits, so an UPDATE would match zero rows without erroring while every optimistic mirror cheerfully showed the new name — the handler gates on the same `waitForConversationPersisted` predicate URL promotion uses (`timeoutMs: 0`, i.e. a single probe rather than that path's 3-minute poll) and refuses out loud. New exports on the manifest — `CHAT_DRAFT_WRITE_MODES` / `isChatDraftWriteMode` / `CHAT_INPUT_DRAFT_MAX` / `CHAT_CONVERSATION_TITLE_MAX` — are the ONE vocabulary: the manifest interpolates them into the descriptions an agent reads and the handlers validate against the same constants, so the advertised contract and the enforced contract cannot drift. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/chat/[id]` and `/chat/new`: one message drove both targets, each raising its own ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply renamed the conversation in the history sidebar and appended into the composer, and **the specific hazard of this surface was checked explicitly — the staged text landed in the CONVERSATION's composer (`textarea[0]`, "Follow-up question:\nHow many people live in Paris?") while the agent-run message box (`textarea[1]`, same placeholder) stayed empty**; on `/chat/new` the draft landed in the hero "Ask anything" composer, proving the `landingContent` mount is covered by the same target. "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the title unchanged and the agent acknowledging the decline without retrying; asking it to rewrite the last user message AND to send the composer produced `No mounted surface declares write target "last_user_message"` / `"send_message"`, with the agent explaining unprompted that sending stays a human action; a forced `input_draft` of a bare string came back as the handler's own throw verbatim — `input_draft expects an object: { "text": string, "mode"?: "replace" | "append" }.` — with the composer byte-identical afterward (no coercion); and on a brand-new chat the title call returned `conversation_title refused — this conversation has not been saved yet, so there is no row to rename. Its row is created when the first turn completes.` Error Inspector: the successful runs produced ZERO `surface-writeback` captures (only the known pre-existing `request_context_changed` stream warnings); every capture seen came from a deliberate contract break above, which is the seam working. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-10 — Agent Apps ADMIN console agent-writable (live-verified) — the first admin surface, and the clean `mode:"draft"` reference.** `matrx-admin/agent-apps` declares 3 ask-policy draft targets, all on the CATEGORIES editor: `category_name`, `category_description`, `category_icon`. Handlers live on the categories page's existing `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (`getWriteHandlers`) and go through the SAME `handleEditChange(field, value)` setter the admin's own typing calls, so a staged value and a typed one are indistinguishable, the "Unsaved Changes" badge and Discard button arm identically, and `handleSave` → `updateAgentAppCategory` (the canonical service) remains the ONLY path to the database — no raw supabase anywhere in the write path. **Why this surface earns the draft label the campaign has mostly had to settle `entity` for:** `category_has_unsaved_changes` is emitted from the very `hasUnsaved` flag the handlers flip. `selected_category` was NOT, though — it was emitted from the SAVED row while the pane rendered `editData`, so it disagreed with the form the moment anything was typed (a pre-existing read lie, human typing included). It now emits from the live `editData` buffer, with the value description saying so and naming `category_has_unsaved_changes` as the divergence flag against `categories_list`. That makes the evidence loop real: an agent reads back exactly what it staged, before anyone saves. **Judgment on an admin console:** almost nothing here earns a target. `is_featured` / `is_verified` / publish status, rate limits and unblocks, error resolution, executions and analytics are trust and abuse controls, not copy — all left undeclared, and the intro now says so explicitly so an agent proposes them in words. Even inside the category pane, `id` (identity, and the input is disabled), `sort_order` (mechanical ordering the admin does with the list's own arrows), creation and deletion (deleting orphans every app assigned to the category) stay human. Name/description/icon are three targets rather than one object on purpose: renaming re-labels the category everywhere it is offered to users while a description or icon edit does not, so each gets its own confirm and can be declined independently. `category_icon` validates against the real registry via `isRegisteredOrLucideIconName` — an invented icon name is rejected rather than saved as a broken glyph. The throwaway used for verification had to be seeded out-of-band; the update path this change rides on is unaffected.
- **2026-08-10 — Applications hub agent-writable (live-verified) — and `matrx-admin/ai-models` RULED OUT on the judgment bar.** Two results, because the negative one saves the next agent the trip. **`matrx-admin/ai-models` does not earn write targets and should not be re-assigned.** Its detail panel's complete editable set is `AiModelFormData` — `name` (the wire identifier), `common_name`, `provider_id`, `context_window`, `max_tokens`, `is_deprecated` / `is_primary` / `is_premium` (the manifest itself notes deprecation IS the activation signal), `cost_rating` / `speed_rating` (these render the user-facing $-tier and speed dots in every model picker), and the four fallback/retry routing fields — plus `capabilities` (JSON Fields), Controls and Constraints (`ai.api.rules` ⊕ `ai.offering.override`), and a Raw JSON tab that is just all of the above at once. Every one is vendor identity, capability governance, commercially load-bearing tiering, or request routing — the exact line the `agent-builder` adopter drew. That is **zero** YES fields, not merely fewer than two. The one plausible authoring field, `description`, is a real `ai.model_definition` column that the manifest emits and even advertises as "the most common agent job here" — but it has **no editor anywhere in the panel**, and it is absent from `AI_MODEL_COLUMNS`, the whitelist the Raw JSON tab strips against, so the only generic write path would silently drop it. Making that surface writable is a `surface-authoring` job (build the editor first), not this skill's. Of the three named fallbacks, `matrx-admin/tool-registry` (one `is_active` switch on `ToolViewPage`) and `matrx-user/crm-manager` (an inheriting overlay with `values: []` over a list + scope filter) fail the same bar. **`matrx-admin/applications` passes and is now live**: ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `app_notice`, staging the operator broadcast (`AppConfigV1.notice`) every installed client shows once. Value is an object REPLACING the whole notice — `{level, title, body, url?}`, with `level` validated against the canonical `NOTICE_LEVELS` imported from `features/admin/applications/config/schema.ts` (the same const the editor's Select renders from and the manifest description interpolates, so the three cannot drift), `title`/`body` required and non-empty because a broadcast is authored whole, `url` optional and checked through the schema's own `httpsUrlSchema`. Validation lives in a pure core (`config/notice-write-targets.ts`, 16 unit tests) kept OUT of the React updater so the throw lands synchronously in the writeback seam; the handler is registered from `AppConfigEditor` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` because that component owns the draft, and it preserves the forward-compat `extras` keys the schema promises to round-trip. New read twin `config_editor_notice` is emitted by `AppConfigClient` from the SAVED row, not the unsaved draft — an agent asking what to replace wants what is live in the fleet. **The generalizable lesson is the per-mount NO**: this hub governs shipped CLIENTS, so the three server URLs and `min_supported_app_version` (they change what every installed client talks to, or force upgrades), `flags`, `credential_maintenance`, the app slug and schema version, catalog artifact pinning, and the read-only Installations and History tabs all get no target — a surface can be worth exactly one target and still be worth doing. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply staged all four fields into the real editor (the notice inputs did not exist before the write — the notice was disabled — and read back off the DOM afterward with the agent's own copy), a same-message request to set `min_supported_app_version` was refused with the agent explaining `app_notice` is the only target exposed, "Keep as is" left the draft untouched and the agent acknowledged the decline without an error, and a forced `{"level":"urgent"}` came back to the agent verbatim as `app_notice: level must be one of info | warning | critical. Received "urgent".` Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures on the valid paths (the deliberate invalid call produced exactly one, which is the designed loud behavior). Nothing was saved — the run staged into an existing row's draft only, no throwaway records. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` + `jest` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-10 — Database admin console agent-writable; THE reference for the drafting/execution line on a surface where execution is the danger.** `matrx-admin/database` declares ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `sql_query` — a full replace of the workbench editor buffer. Handler on `app/(admin)/administration/database/components/enhanced-sql-editor.tsx` (which already mounted the provider; only `getWriteHandlers` was missing) calls the SAME `setSqlQuery` the textarea's `onChange`, the template cards, and the history rows call, so staged SQL and typed SQL are indistinguishable. **Why this is safe on a database console, stated plainly because the instinct is to refuse outright:** the target replaces TEXT IN A TEXTAREA. Nothing connects, nothing runs, nothing is invalidated. That is why staging DDL/DML is explicitly allowed and the target description says so out loud — `DROP TABLE …` sitting in the editor is inert characters the admin reads and decides on, identical in risk to the same characters in a chat reply. The risk of this surface is EXECUTION, and execution has no write path: **Execute, Cancel, `use_cache` and Clear Cache are deliberately not targets** (cache behaviour decides whether the next run touches the live database at all — an agent flipping it changes what executing MEANS without changing anything visible in the SQL), and neither are the result/history/timing values, which are the RECORD of what the database actually did. The handler also refuses while `loading`, so results that land always belong to the SQL the admin is looking at. **`replacement_pairs` considered and REJECTED on the judgment bar, not on risk:** the Template Variables pairs do nothing until the admin presses "Apply Replacements to Query", which rewrites `sql_query` — so an agent writing pairs stages a stage, a strictly worse path to what `sql_query` already does in one visible step, and it has no read twin to declare `updatesValue` against. **Per-mount split:** the `DatabaseHubLanding` mount at `/administration/database` registers NO handlers on purpose — a static link grid over a build-time config module, with no editable state an agent could author. Validation lives in a pure core (`features/administration/lib/sql-editor-write-targets.ts`, 16 unit tests) so the throw lands synchronously in `applySurfaceWrite`'s try/catch rather than inside a React updater; it rejects non-strings, markdown-fenced values, empty/whitespace, and anything over `SQL_QUERY_WRITE_MAX_CHARS` (which the manifest description interpolates rather than re-typing). Also corrected a stale manifest doc comment that pointed the workbench at `/administration/database/database-admin` — the emitter is at `/administration/database/sql-queries`; the `database-admin` dashboard has its own editor and mounts no provider. each handler validates, writes, then `router.refresh()`es so the read twin the agent sees next is what actually landed. **Per-mount split:** `McpToolsManager` mounts the same surface for the catalogue and registers NONE — its only local state is browse state (search / category / source / status / tag filters, column filters, sort), it holds no open record for a confirm dialog to name, and the detail page already owns the canonical door. **Deliberately human-only, and this is the point of an admin adopter:** `tool_is_active`, `tool_admin_only`, `tool_gating`, `tool_exemptions`, `tool_visibility`, `tool_tier` — changing what a tool may REACH or who may reach it is a capability change, not a copy edit (the `agent-builder` call, and it bites harder here: one flipped flag re-arms a tool for every agent on the platform); plus `tool_name` (the dispatch key — there is no slug column, so a rename silently breaks every call site), the parameter/output schemas and annotations (a wrong schema breaks every call of the tool; annotations are the destructive/read-only hints other agents trust), `tool_version`/`tool_semver`, and everything `mcp_server`-adjacent — the neighbourhood the manifest's existing SECURITY note keeps this surface out of. `tool_group` is excluded for a subtler reason worth reusing: it is a fair filing label, but NO human editor exposes it (absent from both `ToolCreatePage` and `ToolEditPage`), so an entity write there is a one-way door the admin cannot correct in the UI. The tag validator rejects an embedded comma because the admin's tag editor is one comma-separated input: a comma inside a tag would split it the next time a human edits the tool. Handlers live on the provider `features/code/agent-context/CodeWorkspaceContextMenu.tsx` already mounted and reuse its existing `handleTextReplace` / `handleTextInsertAfter` / `handleTextInsertBefore` — the very `executeEdits` paths the right-click AI actions use — so an agent edit lands on Monaco's undo stack exactly like a paste. **That is the generalizable bit for editor surfaces:** `draft` normally means "staged in React state until Save", but on a Monaco surface the buffer IS the draft, and the honest equivalent of reversibility is the editor's own undo history. So `current_file_content` performs ONE `executeEdits` over the full model range rather than `model.setValue()`, which would silently throw the user's undo history away — the difference between an agent rewrite you can ⌘Z and one you cannot. The full range is derived from `getLineCount()` / `getLineContent()`, which the repo's narrowed `MonacoModel` type already declares, instead of widening that shared type for one call. `replace_selection` REFUSES (throws) when nothing is selected rather than inventing a range — the target that touches least is the one that must be most certain about where it lands. - **2026-08-10 — Quick Note Save agent-writable; the MODE-GATED target reference (live-verified).** `matrx-user/quick-note-save` declares ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `note_draft`, taking a partial `{note_name?, folder?, content?}`. Handlers live in `features/notes/actions/quick-save/QuickNoteSaveCore.tsx` and register through `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` — the first adopter of the exact split that seam was built for: the WINDOW (`QuickNoteSaveWindow`) publishes the surface and its scope, while the CORE several levels down owns the form state a write lands in. Registration is gated on a new `surfaceName` prop that only the window passes, so the popover / dialog / overlay shells reusing the same core register nothing and cannot shadow a surface they do not publish. **Why one composite:** title, refined body, and destination folder are filled in during a SINGLE act of capture and consumed by ONE save (the `crm-create-party` / `image-generate` case), and this form's cross-field rule makes splitting them unenforceable — the save MODE decides which inputs are rendered at all. **The new thing this surface contributes is the MODE GATE.** An overlay that can save into a NEW note or into an EXISTING one has write combinations that mean different things, and the handler resolves them against live state rather than trusting the payload: `note_name` and `folder` describe a new note and are refused in `update` mode (the title input is not rendered there, and the folder select only FILTERS which notes are listed, so writing it would silently drop the user's chosen target note); `content` is accepted for `create` and for `update` + `append` — both additive — and REFUSED under `update` + `overwrite`, where the staged text would replace an existing note's body wholesale. Every refusal names the mode and tells the agent what to ask the user for, and all of it is spelled out in the target description from the real `SAVE_MODES` / `UPDATE_METHODS` constants (moved to a pure `quickNoteSaveVocabulary.ts` the manifest and the handler both import, so the description and the validation cannot drift). Draft keys land through the SAME `setNoteName` / `setFolder` setters and the refine primitive's `setEditedContent` the user's own typing calls; the user still presses Save Note. Deliberately NOT targets: saving, the save mode, the target note, and the update method — the decisions that can destroy an existing note. The surface also gained an `all_folder_names` read value (emitted by the window from the same Redux selector the form's picker uses), because `folder` refuses any name outside the picker's vocabulary and an agent that cannot read the list could only guess. Live-verified with a real agent run on the open window: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, ONE Apply staged all three fields at once (body 2,042 → 153 chars, folder `Chat Saves` → `Scratch`, title rewritten) with the draft-staged toast and the Save bar still the user's, "Keep as is" declined with no error and left the form untouched, an undeclared `update_method` was refused (`No mounted surface declares write target "update_method"`), and a forced non-existent folder returned the handler's own throw to the agent verbatim, listing the real folder vocabulary. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-10 — Knowledge Data Stores agent-writable; the COLLAPSED-FORM reference.** `matrx-user/rag-data-stores` declares 3 ask-policy targets across the page's two write-capable mounts: `new_store_draft` (`mode:"draft"` — ONE composite object carrying name / kind / description, registered by `CreateStoreInline`) and `store_name` / `store_description` (`mode:"entity"`, registered by `StoreDetailPanel` through the canonical `useDataStoreDetail.updateStore` — no new supabase call). Handlers live in `features/rag/components/data-stores/DataStoresPage.tsx`, registered with `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` because the state lives in children, not in the component that mounts the provider. **The new question this surface answers: what does a write MEAN when the form it targets is closed?** A collapsed create form owns no visible state, so staging into it silently would put the agent's value somewhere the user cannot review — the opposite of what `mode:"draft"` promises. The handler therefore OPENS the form and stages into it, and the target description says so, so the agent knows what it is asking for before the user is asked. `kind` is draft-only by necessity, not by taste: `updateStore` deliberately does not patch `kind`, so on an existing store there is no canonical path to wire it to and declaring it would be a declared-but-unwired defect; it is writable exactly where it is persisted, at creation. The enum is spelled out in the description and validated in the handler from the SAME `DATA_STORE_KINDS` constant the `<select>` renders. Deliberately undeclared: delete store, remove member, add/upload a source (attaching documents also queues RAG ingestion — a curation act with real cost), and audience publishing; a `granted` read-only shared library throws before any write, matching the server-side refusal. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/rag/data-stores`: three targets asked for in ONE message produced three per-target dialogs carrying this manifest's own prose, Apply staged the draft into a form that had been collapsed (name/kind/description all present) and persisted both entity fields, "Keep as is" returned a clean decline the agent treated as final, a delete/remove-members request was refused with the agent naming the only three targets that exist, and a deliberately invalid `kind:"casefile"` reached the handler and came back verbatim as `new_store_draft: kind must be one of general, case, project, reference, inbox, library.` Zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Scraper agent-writable (live-verified); the one-object-command reference.** `matrx-user/scraper` declares 2 `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets that STAGE the next scrape: `scrape_command` (partial-patch `{ mode?: quick|full|search, url?, keyword? }`) and `scrape_page_limit` (integer, bounded). Handlers on `ScraperFloatingWorkspace`'s existing `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`, staging through the same `useState` setters the user's typing uses. **Running a scrape is deliberately NOT agent-drivable** — it spends real wall-clock time and puts load on someone else's server, so the agent stages the command and the user presses Scrape; everything downstream of a run (content, metadata, links) is observed evidence and stays read-only. **The generalizable bit — when a command belongs in ONE object rather than N scalars:** here the mode picks which config input the workspace renders AND which of two keyword stores is live (deep mode's own `keyword` vs. the web-search form's `keywords`). As separate targets, an agent that set the keyword and then the mode would have its keyword land in whichever store the PREVIOUS mode pointed at — a real race on staged React state that fails SILENTLY, because the user just sees an empty field. Bundling mode+url+keyword lets one handler resolve them atomically, validate the whole patch before any state moves (no half-applied command), and REFUSE an incoherent pair (`url` in a keyword mode) instead of staging into an input the user cannot see. The page cap stayed separate on the opposite test: it is a different decision (how much of someone else's server to spend), is routinely changed without touching the target, carries its own bounds vocabulary, and has a clean 1:1 read twin (`max_pages`) that a bundled object cannot have. The mode vocabulary moved to its canonical home: new dependency-free `features/scraper/scrape-command.ts` owns the public enum (`quick|full|search`), the internal workspace keys (`url|batch|web`), the bridge between them, the labels, and the page bounds — the workspace's mode buttons and number input now render from it, `buildScraperContextData` maps `scrape_mode` through it, the manifest interpolates it into the contract prose, and the handlers validate against it, so the enum an agent is TOLD about, the enum it is CHECKED against, and the buttons the user SEES cannot drift. **Per-mount posture:** `ScraperFloatingWorkspace` is the only mount registering a provider for this surface; the `/scraper/*` route pages have their own local inputs but mount no surface runtime and emit none of this surface's read values, so they get no targets — an agent there has neither evidence to write from nor a handler to write through (adopting the surface on those routes is its own task, and deepest-wins means it would shadow nothing declared here). Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `matrx-user/scraper`: both targets asked per-target carrying the manifest description verbatim (bounds and enum visibly interpolated from the constants), Apply staged into the real form (Deep + keyword + 6 pages read back off the DOM; a scheme-less `example.com` normalized to `https://example.com` in URL mode), "Keep as is" left the form untouched with zero toasts, an undeclared results-tab write was refused with the agent reporting only two targets exist, and a forced-invalid call returned the handler's own throw verbatim (`scrape_page_limit expects an integer from 1 to 20.`) with nothing staged. Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures on the VALID path — but see the 2026-08-12 entry below, which corrects what this line implies about the invalid one. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.

Handlers register from `TaskQuickCreateCore.tsx` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` and stage through the same `setTitle`/`setPriority`/`setDueDate` setters and the same `refine.setEditedContent` override the user's typing uses (plus a `resetKeySuffix` bump so the split-view editor re-seeds — a controlled plain textarea updates itself, but the keyed split editor would otherwise have shown stale text: an agent write that lands in state the user cannot see is exactly the silent skip this seam exists to prevent). **The composite call, made explicitly:** everything on a one-shot capture form is filled during a single act of drafting and consumed by a single save, so the fields are not independent decisions — one target asks once and lands one coherent proposal, where four micro-targets would ask four times for fragments. Alongside it the surface finally emits its LIVE state: the core publishes a `task_draft` snapshot into a ref the window's `getScope` reads, so the write target has a real read twin (`updatesValue`) instead of only the opener's prefill — closing the manifest's standing "not yet threaded up" gap. - **2026-08-10 — Transcripts viewer agent-writable (live-verified); THE reference for picking `draft` vs `entity` by whether the read twin can SEE the buffer.** `matrx-user/transcripts` declares 4 ask-policy targets on `TranscriptViewer.tsx`'s existing provider (it mounted `getScope` but no `getWriteHandlers`; the surface renders on `/transcripts/processor`, not on the `/transcripts` list table). `transcript_title`, `transcript_description` and `transcript_speaker_label` are `mode:"entity"` through the canonical `updateTranscript` thunk → `transcriptsService` — the same path the header's Edit → Save and the per-segment edit dialog use, never raw supabase; `transcript_body` is `mode:"draft"` into the inline editor's own `setEditContent`, opening the editor if closed so the staged text lands under the existing Save/Cancel bar. **The mode split is the generalizable finding.** This surface has TWO staging buffers and only one is observable: `getEditorApplicationScope` overrides the `content` value with the LIVE textarea while `isEditingContent` is true, so a staged body is real evidence for both the user and the next agent read — draft is honest there. Title and description read from the stored `activeTranscript` and never from `editTitle`/`editDescription`, which only render while `isEditingMetadata` is true, so a draft there would stage a value the read twin cannot report; those persist on Apply instead. Copying "draft is preferred" without checking the read twin would have produced two invisible writes. Confirmed for this surface and worth knowing campaign-wide: `isEditable` on `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` does NOT gate writes — it is carried onto the registry entry only, and `surface-writeback.ts` never reads it, so `isEditable={false}` mounts are still writable. Deliberately NOT writable: `tags` and `folder_name` (the service accepts both, but this viewer renders tags read-only and has no folder control — a target there would set a value the user can neither see staged nor correct in place, a write with no user twin; revisit if a tag editor lands), identity/provenance (`source_type`, audio/video paths, `is_draft`), deletion and Promote-to-Studio, and playback transport (mechanical). Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on a throwaway transcript: one message drove both entity targets, each confirm dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, both persisted (verified by SQL, not just the DOM); the body draft staged a cleaned four-paragraph transcript with Save armed while `segments` in the DB stayed byte-identical; `transcript_speaker_label` renamed "Speaker 1" → "Dana Whitfield" in both its segments leaving the other speaker and all segment text untouched; "Keep as is" declined with no error and left the title unchanged; a forced-invalid call returned the handler's own throw verbatim (`transcript_speaker_label: no speaker "Speaker 9 Does Not Exist" in this transcript. Existing labels: "Dana Whitfield", "Speaker 2".`); and an undeclared ask (tags + folder) produced NO `apply_surface_write` call, the agent enumerating exactly the four targets it had. Error Inspector on a clean valid-write session: zero `surface-writeback` captures (only the pre-existing `request_context_changed` noise). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. **One thing the campaign should know:** in that undeclared-target test the tags and folder DID change in the database — Badass Agent reached the row with one of its OWN tools, outside the surface seam and outside the ask gate. The surface refused correctly; what a surface declines is not the same as what a globally-bound agent can reach, which matches the `image-generate` note about its own image tool. Not yet mirrored to `ui.ui_surface_write_target`.

The `variant="embedded"` pads — War Room thread Audio tabs — register nothing, because a room renders tiles and several embedded pads can be mounted at once over DIFFERENT sessions, so deepest-wins resolution would land an agent write in whichever tile mounted last with no way for the user to tell which transcript it hit; the war room is also its own surface with its own bespoke HITL tools.
